# The Wish-Fulfilling Tree of Auspicious Moments <note>original: "Muhurtakalpadruma"; a 'Kalpadruma' is a divine tree from Hindu mythology that grants all desires</note>

## Composed by the illustrious and most learned scholar, Vitthala Dikshita

->And it was<-

->Edited and refined by the astrologer <term>Jyotirvid: a scholar of astronomy and astrology</term> Krishna-shankar Keshava-<-
->Rama, a resident of Suryapura <note>modern-day Surat</note>.<-

### In Mumbai

->Printed and published by Pandurang Jawaji<-
->at his own "Nirnaya Sagar Press."<-

--- Page 5 ---
<meta>This is the opening page of the manuscript, serving as a formal introduction or preface (Prastāvanā) to a work on elective astrology.</meta>

## ## Introduction ##

> "A Brahmin should study and understand the Veda along with its six limbs <note>The Vedāngas or 'limbs of the Veda' are auxiliary sciences: phonetics, ritual, grammar, etymology, meter, and astrology.</note> as a matter of duty, without seeking any personal reward."

According to this sacred scripture, and also from the words of the holy teacher Shri Shankaracharya:

> "The Veda should be studied daily, and the duties prescribed therein should be performed well."

Because it is absolutely necessary for the twice-born <note>term: Dvija (twice-born). Refers to members of the three upper social classes whose second 'birth' is their initiation into Vedic study.</note> to perform the duties mentioned in the Vedas after studying them with their six limbs; and because this status of being "twice-born" is dependent upon the initiation ceremony <note>term: Upanayana. The sacred thread ceremony marking the start of formal education.</note>, as stated in the verse:

> "The first birth is from the mother, and the second is from the initiation."

Furthermore, the scripture states:

> "One should initiate a Brahmin in the eighth year and teach him; a Brahmin should establish the sacred fires in the Spring and perform the New and Full Moon sacrifices."

Knowledge of the years, knowledge of the Spring season, and knowledge of the New and Full Moon sacrifices cannot be managed at all without Astrology <note>term: Jyotisha. This encompasses astronomy, mathematics, and astrology.</note>. Among the limbs of the Veda, Astrology is considered the organ of sight. Just as human life is rendered useless without eyes, one must certainly study the science of Light <note>note: original: "Jyotih-shastra", another name for Astrology/Astronomy.</note> which serves as the eyes.

And because it is said:

> "It consists of three branches: Siddhanta (mathematical astronomy), Samhita (omens and collective destiny), and Hora (horoscopy)."

Since the collection of auspicious moments <note>term: Muhurta. A unit of time or an 'election' chosen for starting an activity.</note> is counted within the Samhita division, and because of the saying of the elders:

> "A task started at an auspicious moment will be accomplished quickly, but one started at an inauspicious moment will never succeed."

Considering the supreme utility of the knowledge of auspicious moments, the venerable Shri Vitthal Dikshit, a crest-jewel among astrologers, composed the **"Tree of Desires for Auspicious Moments"** <note>note: original: "Muhurta Kalpadruma". A Kalpadruma is a divine, wish-fulfilling tree in Indian mythology.</note>. This work is the source of the highest benefit for astrologers. Until now, by any scholar...

---
->CC-0 In Public Domain. Ujval Handu Collection<-

<summary>This preface establishes the religious and practical necessity of astrology (Jyotisha) as the 'eyes' of the Vedas, specifically introducing Vitthal Dikshit's treatise on selecting auspicious times for rituals.</summary>
<keywords>Astrology, Jyotisha, Muhurta Kalpadruma, Vitthal Dikshit, Upanayana, Vedic limbs, auspicious timing, Brahmin duties</keywords>

--- Page 6 ---
<meta>This page continues the editor's preface regarding the recovery and publication of the astrological manuscript.</meta>

(4)

Realizing that this work had not been refined or offered at the lotus feet of the goddess of learning, and that its circulation remained extremely rare, I spent a long time searching through the houses of traditional astrologers. Eventually, I acquired an ancient copy of the *Tree of Desires for Electional Astrology* <note>original: "Muhurta Kalpadruma"; a 'Muhurta' is an auspicious moment chosen for starting an activity</note> which was divided into three parts and in a state of total dilapidation. Deciding that this work certainly deserved to be set in print, I completed the editing process to the best of my ability and handed it over to Mr. Pandurang Jawaji, the proprietor of the Nirnaya Sagar Press, for publication.

Since I was performing this revision using a single, extremely worn-out and fragmented manuscript, the maxim "even the walking man may stumble" <note>original: "gacchataḥ skhalanaṃ kvāpi"; a Sanskrit proverb suggesting that errors are natural when one is making progress</note> applies here. Should any inaccuracies meet your gaze, they are not the fault of the original author, but rather mine, caused by the lack of a clean manuscript and the resulting fluctuations of my own mind. I ask the most learned scholars to forgive me and to point out any errors they encounter so that they may be cleansed; in this way, everything may be made perfectly square and correct in the second edition.

It is clearly evident from two verses at the conclusion of the *Kunda-siddhi* <note>A technical work on the construction of sacrificial pits</note>—specifically the verses beginning "Thus the pavilion..." and "The moon, the ages..."—that the illustrious ornament of astrologers, Vitthala Dikshita, graced the city of Varanasi (presided over by the god Shiva) in the Shaka year 1541 <note>approximately 1619 CE</note>. There also exists a work by the honorable Vitthala Dikshita titled *The Method of the Desire-Granting Vine* <note>original: "Kalpavalli-paddhati"</note>, a treatise on natal astrology <term>Jataka-paddhati: a manual for calculating and interpreting individual horoscopes</term> consisting of sixty-six verses. I shall also publish that work as time permits.

->**The servant of the learned, the Astrologer Krishna Shankar Keshavram Raik.**<-

<summary>The editor, Krishna Shankar Keshavram Raik, describes his efforts to rescue a fragmented manuscript of the Muhurta Kalpadruma from obscurity and requests scholarly indulgence for any errors. He also provides historical context for the author, Vitthala Dikshita, dating him to early 17th-century Varanasi.</summary>

<keywords>Muhurta Kalpadruma, Vitthala Dikshita, Electional Astrology, Nirnaya Sagar Press, Varanasi, 1619 CE, Jataka-paddhati, manuscript editing</keywords>

--- Page 19 ---
<meta>This is the opening page of the Muhurtakalpadruma, a classical Sanskrit treatise on electional astrology (Muhurta). It establishes the traditional invocation and the method for calculating the 60-year Jupiter cycle.</meta>

<page-num>1</page-num>

-> Om <note>The sacred syllable representing the ultimate reality</note> <-

# The Wish-Fulfilling Tree of Auspicious Times
<note>original: "Muhurtakalpadruma"</note>

-> Salutations to the Lord Ganesha <-
-> (The Invocation) <-

Having installed Ganesha—the friend of the helpless and the destroyer of obstacles—within my heart, and having thoroughly reflected upon the sayings of the ancient sages, I begin this "Wish-Fulfilling Tree of Auspicious Times." (1)

O Goddess of Speech <note>Saraswati</note>! Fill this composition of mine with a succession of blessings. This is my humble petition at your lotus feet regarding this work titled the "Wish-Fulfilling Tree of Auspicious Times." (2)

-> (The Flower of the Year) <-

Now, the calculation of the Year:
Take the Shaka era year <note>The traditional Indian calendar system starting in 78 CE</note>, add twelve (12), and divide by sixty (60). The remainder is the year known in the regions south of the Narmada River. If one adds nine (9) to that same remainder, it is declared as the year according to the Jupiterian cycle <note>term: Brihaspati Chakra (the 60-year cycle of Jupiter)</note> in other regions. (3)

Now, the sixty years are described according to the six verses spoken by the sage Narada:
Prabhava, Vibhava, Shukla, Pramoda, Prajapati, Angira, Shrimukha, Bhava, Yuva, and Dhata. (4)
Ishvara, Bahudhanya, Pramathi, Vikrama, Vrisha, Chitrabhanu, Subhanu, Tarana, Parthiva, and Vyaya. (5)

<summary>This opening page introduces the "Muhurtakalpadruma," beginning with traditional prayers to Ganesha and Saraswati before explaining the mathematical formula to identify the current year within the 60-year Jovian cycle.</summary>
<keywords>Muhurta, Electional Astrology, Ganesha, Saraswati, Shaka Era, Jupiter Cycle, Narada, Calendar Calculation</keywords>

--- Page 20 ---
<meta>This page continues the astronomical and calendrical calculations following the introduction of the Jovian cycle. It lists the remaining years of the 60-year cycle and defines the rules for "lost years" and seasonal divisions.</meta>

<page-num>2</page-num>

Sarvajit, Sarvadhari, Virodhi, Vikriti, and Khara;
Nandana, Vijaya, Jaya, Manmatha, and Durmukha. || 6 ||
Hemalamba, Vilamba, Vikari, Sharvari, and Plava;
Shubhakrit, Shobhana, Krodhi, Vishvavasu, and Parabhava. || 7 ||
Plavanga, Kilaka, Saumya, Sadharana, and Virodhakrit;
Paridhavi, Pramadi, Ananda, Rakshasa, and Nala. || 8 ||
Pingala, Kalayukta, Siddhartha, Raudra, and Durmati;
Dundubhi, Rudhirodgari, Raktakshi, Krodhana, and Kshaya. || 9 ||
<note>These verses complete the list of the 60 years of the Jupiter cycle (Samvatsara). Each name carries a specific omen; for example, 'Kshaya' means 'destruction' and marks the end of the cycle.</note>

### ( On the Knowledge of the Expunged Year )

When Jupiter, moving with accelerated speed <term>Atichara: accelerated planetary motion</term>, skips a sign other than the four starting from Aquarius, and does not return to the previous sign while in retrograde, then a year is considered "lost" or expunged. This is highly censured in the region between the Narmada and Ganges rivers. || 10 ||
<note>original: "Somodbhava-Janhubhava-antarale" referring to the lands between the Narmada and Ganges.</note>
If, by either true or mean motion, Jupiter does not enter a new zodiac sign during a lunar year, that year is considered an intercalary year <term>Adhivatsara: an extra or overlapping year</term>. Like an intercalary month, it should be avoided for auspicious ceremonies. || 11 ||

### ( On the Knowledge of the Northern and Southern Hemispheres )

The Northern and Southern celestial hemispheres begin from Aries and Libra respectively. The Southern and Northern solstices <term>Ayana: the sun's half-year journey</term> are said to begin from Cancer and Capricorn. The seasons, beginning with Spring, are defined by pairs of signs starting from Pisces. || 12 ||
The seasons are named Spring, Summer, the Rainy season, Autumn, Winter, and the Dewy season in sequence. The lords of these seasons are the masters of the signs corresponding to their names.

### ( On the Knowledge of Months )

Months are of four types: Solar, Lunar, Savana (civil), and Sidereal. || 13 ||
A civil month consists of thirty days. A sidereal month is determined by the moon's passage through the constellations. A solar month is determined by the sun's entry into a zodiac sign <term>Sankranti: the sun's transit</term>. A lunar month ends with the new moon; in the region north of the Vindhya mountains, the month begins with the dark fortnight <term>Krishnadi: the Purnimanta system where the month ends on a full moon</term>. For religious vows and in other regions, the month begins with the bright fortnight <term>Shukladi: the Amanta system where the month ends on a new moon</term>. Both the bright and dark fortnights are used accordingly.

<note>The text highlights the regional differences in Indian timekeeping, specifically the Purnimanta system of the North versus the Amanta system of the South.</note>

<vocab>
<term>Samvatsara</term>: A Jovian year in the 60-year cycle.
<term>Guru</term>: The planet Jupiter, whose motion defines the major calendar cycles.
<term>Masa</term>: A month, calculated in four different ways (solar, lunar, etc.).
<term>Ritu</term>: A season; the Indian calendar recognizes six distinct seasons.
<term>Gola</term>: The celestial sphere or hemisphere.
<term>Saura</term>: Solar measurement of time.
<term>Savana</term>: Civil time based on simple day counts (30 days per month).
<term>Nakshatra</term>: A lunar mansion or star cluster used in sidereal time.
<term>Uttarayana</term>: The northward journey of the sun (winter solstice to summer solstice).
<term>Dakshinayana</term>: The southward journey of the sun (summer solstice to winter solstice).
</vocab>

<summary>This page lists the final years of the 60-year Jovian cycle and explains the astronomical conditions under which a year is "lost" or expunged from the calendar. It also defines the celestial hemispheres, the six seasons, and the different methods for calculating a month based on solar, lunar, and civil cycles.</summary>
<keywords>Jovian cycle, Samvatsara, Jupiter's motion, intercalary year, Indian seasons, solar month, lunar month, Uttarayana, Dakshinayana, Hindu astronomy</keywords>

--- Page 21 ---
<meta>This page continues the astronomical and astrological treatise, transitioning from the discussion of years to the specific properties of lunar days (Tithis).</meta>

<page-num>3</page-num>

The two fortnights, the bright and the dark <note>original: "shveta-asitau," referring to Shukla Paksha (waxing) and Krishna Paksha (waning)</note>, are associated with the gods and the ancestors respectively; they are considered auspicious for good works and inauspicious for others. After thoroughly examining many ancient texts, this work has been composed by Vitthala Dikshita. In this book titled *The Desire-Granting Tree of Electional Astrology* <note>original: "Muhurta Kalpadruma"</note>, the chapter known as the "Flower of the Year" is now concluded. || 16 ||

->Thus ends the "Flower of the Year" in the<-
->*Muhurta Kalpadruma* composed by Shri Vitthala Dikshita.<-

->( The Flower of the Lunar Days )<-
->( The Deities Ruling the Lunar Days )<-
Fire, the Creator, the Mountain-Daughter, the Lord of Hosts, the Serpent, the War-God, the Sun, the Lord, the Unattainable Goddess, Time, the All-Maker, the Sustainer, the Love-God, the Auspicious One, and the Moon; finally, the Ancestors are the lords of the lunar days. <note>These correspond to the 15 days of the lunar fortnight: Agni, Brahma, Gauri, Ganesha, Shesha, Kartikeya, Surya, Shiva, Durga, Yama, Vishvadeva, Vishnu, Kamadeva, Shiva, and the Moon/Pitris.</note> 1

->( Classifications of the Lunar Days )<-
The lunar days are categorized as Joyous, Prosperous, Victorious, Empty, and Full <note>The five groups: Nanda, Bhadra, Jaya, Rikta, and Purna</note>. In the bright fortnight, these are considered respectively blameworthy, mediocre, and highly recommended; in the dark fortnight, the effects are reversed. Furthermore, they become "Perfectly Accomplished" when they coincide with the days of Venus, Mercury, Mars, Saturn, and Jupiter. 2

A lunar day that exists during two consecutive sunrises, or one that does not touch a sunrise at all, is generally avoided. Specifically, the ninth lunar day is considered unfavorable, as are those lunar days that are stripped of the tenth day's influence. 3

->( Inauspicious Hours to be Avoided for Urgent Matters )<-
For urgent and necessary actions, the initial hours <note>term: "ghatika" (a unit of 24 minutes)</note> of the lunar days must be avoided according to these counts: 4, 6, 8, 9, 12, 14, 8, 9, 14, 25, 10, 5, 10, and 5. The remaining hours are considered auspicious. || 4 ||

|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| 8 | 9 | 14 | 25 | 10 | 5 |

<note>term: "Dagdha Tithis" (Burned Days) are specific lunar days considered unlucky when the Sun is in certain zodiac signs.</note>
When the Sun resides in Sagittarius or Pisces; in Taurus or Aquarius; in Cancer or Aries; in Virgo or Gemini; in Scorpio or Leo... <insert>the text continues to list the specific unlucky pairings of zodiac signs and lunar dates.</insert>

->The even lunar days starting from the second are considered as such. || 5 ||<-
<note>original: "dagdha-saṃjñā" (Burned). This refers to specific lunar days (tithis) that are considered inauspicious for beginning new works when the Sun occupies certain zodiac signs.</note>

->( The Beginnings of the Eras of Manu and the World-Ages )<-

The 15th, 3rd, 15th, 10th, 7th, 10th, 9th, 15th, 11th, and 15th <note>These refer to specific dates in the lunar months</note> are the beginnings of the Manvantaras <note>The eras of the various Manus, the progenitors of humanity</note>. Specifically, the 9th of the bright half of Kartika, the 3rd of the bright half of Vaishakha, the 15th of the month of Magha, and the 13th of the dark half of Bhadrapada are remembered as the beginnings of the Yugas <note>The four great world-ages: Satya, Treta, Dwapara, and Kali</note>. || 6 ||

---

->( The Flower of the Weekdays )<-

The Sun, the Moon, and the three starting with Mercury <note>Mercury, Jupiter, and Venus</note> are generally praised for auspicious works. Others have said that the waning Moon, the Sun, Mars, and Saturn are malefic, and Mercury is also malefic when joined with them. || 1 ||
Venus and the Moon are beneficial in the bright fortnight; the Sun and Mars are also beneficial then. Saturn is beneficial in the dark fortnight; others are said to be beneficial elsewhere. || 2 ||
A task performed on the day of a planet that is strong and situated in an "Upachaya" house <note>The 3rd, 6th, 10th, or 11th houses of a horoscope, associated with growth</note> will succeed. A task done on the day of a weak planet will not succeed even with great effort. || 3 ||

->( Classifications of Male, Female, and Neuter )<-

Mars, Jupiter, and the Sun are male; Venus and the Moon are female; Mercury and Saturn are neuter.

<meta>This page continues the technical exposition of Jyotisha (Vedic astrology), moving from planetary characteristics to the governance of the Vedas, social classes, and the mathematical calculation of planetary strength and time-keeping.</meta>

<page-num>5</page-num>

### ( Lords of the Vedas )
Jupiter, Venus, Mars, and Mercury are the lords of the four Vedas <note>The Rig, Yajur, Sama, and Atharva Vedas respectively</note> in their traditional order. ||4||

### ( Lords of the Social Classes )
Venus and Jupiter are the lords of the Priestly class <note>Brahmins</note>; the Sun and Mars are the masters of the Warrior class <note>Kshatriyas</note>; the Moon is the lord of the Merchant class <note>Vaishyas</note>; Mercury is the lord of the Laboring class <note>Shudras</note>; and Saturn is the lord of those outside the class system <note>Antyajas</note>. 5

### ( Exaltations and Debilitations )
Aries, Taurus, Capricorn, Virgo, Cancer, Pisces, and Libra are, in order, the signs of exaltation <note>The signs where a planet reaches its maximum strength</note> for the Sun and the other planets. The seventh sign from each of these is the sign of debilitation <note>The sign where a planet is at its weakest</note>. 6

### ( The Commencement of the Day )
->The distance in leagues between the Prime Meridian and one's local place,<-
->reduced by one-fourth, gives the time difference in minutes.<-
->If the place is west, add fifteen ghatikas <note>A unit of time equal to 24 minutes</note>;<-
->if east, subtract them to find the local constant. || 7 ||<-
The change of the weekday occurs at the moment of this calculated constant, starting from midday. However, all ritual activities should begin from sunrise, while the planetary hours <term>Horas: planetary hours used to determine auspicious timing</term> follow the change of the weekday. 8

### ( Lords of the Horas )
The lords of the hours <note>Horas</note> follow this sequence starting from the Lord of the Day: Sun, Venus, Mercury, Moon, Saturn, Jupiter, and Mars.

### ( Duration of a Hora )
The duration of a single Hora is established here as two and a half ghatikas <note>Exactly 60 minutes, as 1 ghatika is 24 minutes</note> of the day's length. 9

### ( Degrees of Arc )
For the Sun, the degrees to be subtracted are 14, 8, and 7. For the Moon: 13, 11, and 12. For Mars: 10, 3, and 4. For Mercury: 2, 9, and 8. For Jupiter: 16 and 6. 10
For Venus: 15, 5, and 1. For Saturn: 6, 4, and 5. These are the measures for the day; I shall now speak of the values to be subtracted for the night, starting from the Sun. 11

->( The Flower of Yogas )<-

The *Yogas* <term>Yoga: specific angular relationships between the Sun and Moon</term> beginning with *Vishkambha* are described; nine of them are known by names signifying opposition. In *Ganda* and *Atiganda*, six *nadis* <note>1 nadi = 24 minutes</note> are avoided; in *Shula*, five; and the first half of *Parigha*. 1
->In *Vajra*, the first three *nadis* should be<-
->avoided, as well as the entirety of *Vaidhriti* and *Vyatipata*.<-
->In the yoga named *Vyaghata*, nine *nadis* are to be shunned;<-
->some say the first quarter of these should be avoided. || 2 ||<-
In a bad *Yoga*, if the lunar mansion including *Abhijit* <note>the intercalary 28th constellation</note> is at an odd interval from the Sun's mansion, it is *Ekargala* <note>a specific astrological obstruction</note>; if the Sun and Moon are in even quarters, it is not auspicious. || 3 ||

Having thoroughly examined many texts and properly composed them, Vitthala Dikshita hereby completes the flower <note>The author uses "flower" (kusuma) as a metaphor for a chapter or section</note> named "Combinations" within the *Tree of Auspicious Moments* <note>original: "Muhurta-Kalpadruma"</note>. ॥ 4 ॥

### (The Flower of the Half-Lunar Day)

From the second half of the first day of the bright fortnight, the seven "Karana" <note>A Karana is a half-tithi or half-lunar day, used to determine the suitability of an action</note> occur in sequence: Bava, Balava, Kaulava, Taitila, Gara, Vanija, and Vishti. ॥ 1 ॥

From the second half of the fourteenth day of the dark fortnight until the end of the first half of the next cycle, the fixed Karanas occur: Shakuni, Chatushpada, Naga, and Kimstughna. ॥ 2 ॥

->In the bright fortnight, the latter half of the eleventh day and the fourth day,<-
->the first half of the eighth day and the full moon day,<-
->and in the dark fortnight, the latter half of the third and tenth days,<-
->and the first half of the fourteenth and seventh days, are known as Bhadra <note>Bhadra, also called Vishti, is a period generally considered inauspicious for beginning new ventures</note>.<- ॥ 3 ॥

If the Moon is in Leo, Scorpio, Aquarius, or Taurus, this period is not beneficial for the world of mortals. However, if the Bhadra occurring in the first half of the day falls at night, or if the one occurring in the second half falls during the day, it becomes auspicious for good works. ॥ 4 ॥

On the 8th, 7th, 15th, 14th, 10th, 11th, 3rd, and 4th lunar days respectively, after the passage of 5, 12, 20, 27, 5, 12, 20, and 27 ghatikas <note>A ghatika is a unit of time equal to 24 minutes</note>, a period of three ghatikas is considered auspicious. ॥ 5 ॥

| 8 | 7 | 15 | 14 | 10 | 11 | 3 | 4 |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| 5 | 12 | 20 | 27 | 5 | 12 | 20 | 27 |

Acts involving poison, imprisonment, fire, or weapons, and dealings involving buffaloes, camels, or horses, are indeed successful during the Vishti periods, as taught by the sage who was the priest of the Ikshvaku clan <note>Referring to Vashistha, the royal priest of the lineage of Lord Rama</note>. ॥ 6 ॥

Having thoroughly examined many texts and properly composed them, Vitthala Dikshita hereby completes the flower named "The Half-Lunar Day" <note>original: "Tithyardha"</note> within the *Tree of Auspicious Moments*. ॥ 7 ॥

### (The Flower of Auspicious and Inauspicious Combinations)

Mula, Hasta, Revati, Ashvini, Punarvasu, Mrigashira, Chitra, Shravana, the three Uttaras, and Pushya are auspicious when falling on a Sunday. On a Monday, Shravana, Rohini, Pushya, Shatabhisha, Rohini <note>repeated for emphasis or specific calculation</note>, Anuradha, Swati, Jyeshtha, Punarvasu, and Dhanishtha are favorable. ॥ 1 ॥

--- Page 26 ---
<meta>This page continues the technical instructions for selecting auspicious times (Muhurta) based on the alignment of lunar mansions (Nakshatras) with specific days of the week and planetary influences.</meta>

->( 8 )<-

On **Tuesday**, the following lunar mansions are auspicious: Ashvini, Ashlesha, Krittika, Magha, Purva Phalguni, Anuradha, Pushya, and Revati. <note>original: "बुध्याश्विसार्पाग्निभविश्वभाग्यमैत्रेज्यपौष्णानि" - these are poetic synonyms for the Nakshatras.</note> On **Wednesday**, Krittika, Anuradha, Pushya, Hasta, Chitra, Mrigashira, Rohini, and the three "Uttara" mansions <note>Uttara Phalguni, Uttara Ashadha, and Uttara Bhadrapada</note> are declared most excellent. 2

On **Thursday**, the mansions of Punarvasu, Pushya, Revati, Rohini, Shravana, Ashvini, Hasta, Chitra, Swati, and Anuradha are auspicious. On **Friday**, Purva Phalguni, Revati, Rohini, Purva Bhadrapada, Mrigashira, Anuradha, the three "Uttara" mansions, and Hasta are favorable. 3

On **Saturday**, Swati, Bharani, Shravana, Shatabhisha, Magha, Pushya, the three "Uttara" mansions, Jyeshtha, Ardra, and Mrigashira are auspicious. Even though all these are listed, the first two mentioned in each set form the **Nectar-Achievement Union** <term>Amrita Siddhi Yoga: a highly favorable astrological alignment believed to grant success and longevity</term>. 4

The following mansions are **not recommended** on the days starting from Sunday: Anuradha, Uttara Ashadha, Shatabhisha, Ashvini, Mrigashira, Ashlesha, and Revati. Furthermore, Bharani, Chitra, Uttara Ashadha, Dhanishta, Rohini, Ardra, and Revati are also discouraged. 5

Counting from the Sun's current mansion, the 7th, 18th, 19th, 14th, 15th, 10th, and 5th mansions are considered to belong to the Southwest direction <note>Nairrita</note>. The five mansions starting from the 21st, 22nd, 23rd, 24th, and 25th are associated with earthquakes and other natural disturbances. One should not perform auspicious deeds during these unions. 6

For auspicious works, the following pairings are good: Sunday with Hasta, Monday with Rohini, Tuesday with Ashvini, and Wednesday with Anuradha. Thursday with Pushya and Friday with Revati are also excellent. However, Saturday with Rohini should be avoided after the fifth part of the day. 7

According to the sage Vashistha, the **Nanda** lunar days <note>1st, 6th, 11th</note> are auspicious on Tuesday and Sunday; **Bhadra** <note>2nd, 7th, 12th</note> on Monday and Friday; **Jaya** <note>3rd, 8th, 13th</note> on Wednesday; **Rikta** <note>4th, 9th, 14th</note> on Thursday; and **Purna** <note>5th, 10th, 15th</note> on Saturday. 8

The union of Thursday with Pushya, Saturday with Rohini, and Tuesday with Ashvini are "Perfect Success Unions" <note>Siddha Yoga</note>. However, sages declare that these are **not** recommended for marriage, travel, or entering a new house, in that specific order. 9

Even if "Death Unions" <note>Mrityu Yoga</note> exist, some say they are neutralized by the strength of the Moon after midday. Except for travel, a "Good Union" on an otherwise "Bad Day" is acceptable for success after half the day has passed. If the Sun is on the 7th lunar day or Wednesday is the first day of the lunar fortnight, a specific union of the day and date occurs...

If the sum <note>of the lunar day and the weekday</note> equals thirteen, that day is considered censurable <note>inauspicious</note> for all types of work. || 11 ||

Bad astrological combinations <term>Kuyogas: unfavorable alignments of planets and stars</term> arising from the combination of weekdays and lunar mansions <term>Nakshatras</term>, or lunar days and weekdays, or all three together, should be avoided in the lands of the Vangas <note>Bengal</note>, the Hunas <note>Huns/Central Asians</note>, and the Khasas <note>Himalayan regions</note>; however, in other regions, they are considered acceptable. || 12 ||

The following sequences <note>of stars and weekdays</note> create the "Ananda" and other yogas in order:
Sun in Ashvini, Moon in Mrigashira, Mars in Ashlesha, Mercury in Hasta, Jupiter in Anuradha, Venus in Uttarashadha, and Saturn in Shatabhisha. || 13 ||

The twenty-eight yogas <note>astrological combinations</note> are named as follows:
1. Ananda (Joy), 2. Kaladanda (Staff of Death), 3. Dhumra (Smoky), 4. Prajapati (Creator), 5. Saumya (Gentle), 6. Dhvaksha (Crow), 7. Dhvaja (Banner), 8. Shrivatsa (Favorite of Fortune), 9. Vajra (Thunderbolt), 10. Mudgara (Hammer), || 14 ||
11. Chhatra (Umbrella), 12. Maitra (Friendly), 13. Manasa (Mental), 14. Padma (Lotus), 15. Lumpaka (Plunderer), 16. Utpata (Calamity), 17. Mrityu (Death), 18. Kana (One-eyed), 19. Siddhi (Success), 20. Shubha (Auspicious), 21. Amrita <note>original: "Sudha"</note> (Nectar), || 15 ||
22. Musala (Pestle), 23. Gada (Mace), 24. Matanga (Elephant), 25. Rakshasa (Demon), 26. Chara (Movable), 27. Sthira (Fixed), and 28. Vardhamana (Increasing). These are the fruits of the twenty-eight combinations. || 16 ||

After thoroughly examining many ancient texts, Vitthala Dikshita has composed this work. Thus ends the section titled "The Flower of Auspicious Combinations" within the book named *The Desire-Granting Tree of Electional Astrology* <term>Muhurta Kalpadruma</term>. || 17 ||

### The Flower of Auspicious Moments
### (Muhurta-Kusumam)

The auspicious moments <term>Muhurtas</term> of the day are presided over by the following deities: Raudra (Shiva), Kundali, Mitra, the Ancestors, the Vasus, the Waters, the Universal Gods, Abhijit, Brahma, Indra, <margin>1. Indra</margin> the Dual Gods (Indra-Agni), the Demon, the Waters, Aryaman, and Bhaga.
The moments of the night begin with Raudra and Ajaikapad <margin>2. From Ajaikapad, eight in order</margin>, followed by Ahirbudhnya, Pushan, the Ashvins, Aditi, Jupiter, Vishnu, the Sun, Tvashtar, and Vayu. The duration of a Muhurta is defined as the thirtieth part of a full day and night. || 1 ||

The eighth <term>Muhurta</term> <note>a unit of time approximately 48 minutes long</note> named Maitra, the twelfth, the fifteenth, and the fourth; likewise the twelfth, the sixth, the fifteenth, and the second; these should be avoided by one seeking auspicious results on the days beginning with Sunday. Furthermore, in the "Kingdom of the White Moon" <note>referring to the bright fortnight</note>, the eighth Muhurta should be avoided, and for Mars, the seventh Muhurta at night should be shunned by the wise. ॥ 2 ॥

## The Flower of the Lunar Mansions
### The Lords of the Lunar Mansions

The Ashvins, Yama, Agni, Prajapati, the Moon, Shiva, Aditi, Jupiter, and the Serpents; then the Ancestors, Bhaga, Aryaman, the Sun, Tvashtar, Vayu, and Indra's Wife; Mitra, Indra, the Giants, the Waters, the Universal Gods, Brahma, the Great Lord, and the Vasus; the Lord of Waters, Aja-Ekapada, Ahirbudhnya, and finally Pushan—these are the presiding deities of the Lunar Mansions <note>Nakshatras</note> starting from Ashvini. ॥ 1-2 ॥

### The Fixed and Other Classifications of Mansions and Days

The three "Latter" mansions <note>Uttara Phalguni, Uttara Ashadha, Uttara Bhadrapada</note> along with Rohini, when falling on a Sunday, are termed **Fixed** or **Steady**. Swati, Shravana, Dhanishta, Shatabhisha, and Punarvasu, when falling on a Monday, are termed **Movable** or **Unsteady**. ॥ 3 ॥

The three "Former" mansions <note>Purva Phalguni, Purva Ashadha, Purva Bhadrapada</note> along with Bharani and Magha, are termed **Cruel** or **Fierce**; these are associated with Tuesday. Vishakha and Krittika, falling on a Wednesday, are termed **Ordinary** or **Mixed**. ॥ 4 ॥

Abhijit, Hasta, Ashvini, and Pushya are termed **Light** and **Swift**; these are associated with Thursday. Mrigashira, Revati, Anuradha, and Chitra are termed **Soft** or **Friendly**; these are associated with Friday. ॥ 5 ॥

Ardra, Mula, Ashlesha, and Jyeshtha, falling on a Saturday, are known as **Sharp** or **Dreadful**. Know them as such.

The work and actions performed during these times are equal to a sacrifice, as clearly stated in the scriptures. ||6||

### (The Classifications of Lunar Mansions as Downward, Upward, or Sideways-Facing and Their Respective Tasks)

The "Ordinary" and "Fierce" lunar mansions <note>referring to specific groupings of Nakshatras like Krittika or Magha</note> are said to be downward-facing, as are Pushya and the three stars of Vishnu <note>Shravana, Dhanishtha, and Shatabhisha</note>. The "Fixed" stars and Ardra are upward-facing. Finally, Punarvasu, Jyeshtha, Hasta, Ashwini, Swati, and the "Soft" stars are sideways-facing. ||7||

The construction of step-wells, ponds, and similar structures should be done under downward-facing stars. Under sideways-facing stars, one should engage with <term>horses, cows, elephants, and plowing</term> <note>Sanskrit: turanga-go-hasti-haladikam; activities involving draft animals and agriculture</note>. Under upward-facing stars, one should undertake the construction of ceremonial umbrellas, houses, and similar elevated structures. ||8||

### (The Wearing of New Clothes)

Under the <term>three "Latter" stars, Ashwini, and the two Punarvasus</term> <note>Sanskrit: uttarashvibha-punarvasu-dvaye; referring to Uttara Phalguni, Uttara Ashadha, Uttara Bhadrapada, Ashwini, and Punarvasu</note>, under <term>Rohini</term>, the five stars of the "Hand" <note>Hasta and its neighbors</note>, and the final star <note>Revati</note>; when the Ascendant is auspicious, and on the three days of Mercury <note>Wednesday and associated timings</note>, the <term>wearing of new clothes should occur on non-Empty lunar days</term> <note>Sanskrit: vastradharanam-ariktake-tithau; avoiding the 4th, 9th, and 14th lunar days known as Rikta or "Empty"</note>. ||9||

Under the two Revatis, the five stars starting with Hasta, and Dhanishtha; on the days of the Sun, Mars, Jupiter, and Venus; a married woman whose husband is living should wear glass, conch-shell ornaments, gems, clothes, and jewelry. ||10||

The two Punarvasus, Rohini, and the three "Latter" stars are not auspicious for a married woman to wear new clothes for the first time. Likewise, the star Purva Ashadha <note>literally "the one who lies in the water"</note> should be avoided for the ritual bath. ||11||

Divide the length of the cloth into three parts. If the middle part is stained with soot or similar marks, or if the ends are burnt or torn, it is said to be undesirable. This rule applies everywhere—to beds, sandals, and other personal items. ||12||

When a gift is presented by a King, or given out of love, or given at a wedding, or by the will of a Brahmin <note>literally "an earthly god"</note>, then clothes and jewelry may be worn even on forbidden lunar days, weekdays, or under inauspicious planetary alignments.

Under Dhanishtha, Ashwini, and the five stars of the "Hand," and the two Punarvasus, the wearing of clothes is auspicious. O Wise One, avoid Saturday and Wednesday, the transition days <note>Parva; New/Full moon</note>, the "Empty" days, the sixth lunar day, and Sunday.

### (The Buying and Selling of Cattle)

Under the two Revatis, the two Punarvasus, the two Dhanishthas, Jyeshtha, Hasta, and Vishakha, the buying and selling of cows is recommended. Some say this may also be done under <term>the "Former" stars, Bharani, Anuradha, and Mrigashira</term> <note>Sanskrit: purvasripamitra-chandre</note>. ||15||

One should not bring cattle into the fold under Chitra, the "Fixed" stars, Shravana, or on the New Moon, the "Empty" days, Tuesdays, or the eighth lunar day.

<summary>This page details the astrological classifications of Nakshatras (lunar mansions) based on their "facing" direction and provides specific Muhurta rules for wearing new clothing and trading livestock.</summary>

<page-num>12</page-num>

Travel and remaining in place, as well as protection, should occur when the Ascendant is auspicious, the eighth house is vacant <note>The "purity" of the eighth house refers to it being free of planets, which is considered vital for safety during travel</note>, and the moon is in its own sign.

### ( Deeds Concerning Elephants and Horses )

All activities related to elephants should be performed under the Moveable, Light, and Soft constellations <note>Sanskrit: Nakshatras</note>.
Perform the best deeds for horses under the Light constellations, the two Vasus <note>Dhanishta</note>, the two twins <note>Ashwini</note>, and the constellations of the Sun, Mercury, and the Wind <note>Swati</note>.
From the Sun's constellation, count five and then one <note>a total of six</note>; these two pairs of constellations, along with the Abhijit constellation, lead to prosperity. || 17 ||
If Saturn is situated in the constellation named after the elephant, or in the sixth constellation from the current one, or in the tenth or fourteenth house, that elephant will bring victory to the King in battle; thus it is stated in the Science of Sounds <note>Svara Shastra</note>.

### ( Needlework, Markets, and Sales )

Needlework should be done under the constellations of Aditi, Vasu, Mriga, Ashwini, Tvastr, and Maitra <note>Punarvasu, Dhanishta, Mrigashira, Ashwini, Chitra, and Anuradha</note>, on days other than the "Empty" lunar days <note>Rikta Tithis: 4th, 9th, and 14th</note>, and not on Tuesdays.
Setting up a shop or marketplace is auspicious when Venus and the Moon are in the Ascendant, or in the 2nd, 11th, 10th, or 1st houses, provided the Sun and malefic planets are absent, and under the Swift, Friendly, and Fixed constellations. || 19 ||
Sales bring wealth when conducted under the Fierce, Mixed, or Serpent constellations, provided the malefic planets are in the 3rd or 11th houses and the benefic planets are in the angles, trines, or the 2nd house <note>1, 4, 7, 10, 9, 5, or 2</note>. Avoid the sign of Aquarius, Tuesdays, and the "Empty" lunar days. || 20 ||

### ( Forging Iron Weapons and Related Works )

The forging of iron weapons is auspicious under the Mixed, Dreadful, Mriga, and Horse constellations.
The wearing of garments and leather goods should occur under the constellations of Mitra, the Moon, Vasu, Aditi, Chitra, and the Ashwins. || 21 ||

### ( Minting Silver Coins and Seals )

The minting of silver coins and seals is highly recommended under the Fixed, Soft, Swift, and Moveable constellations, on days other than Saturday or Monday, on a "Full Victory" lunar day, and when the Ascendant is auspicious and the planets Venus and Jupiter are visible.

## ( The Annual Eating of the First Grains )

The auspicious eating of newly harvested grain <note>The ritual of Navannaprashana, or the first tasting of the season's harvest</note> should be performed under the movable, soft, and light constellations <note>Nakshatras</note>, in a favorable Ascendant, excluding the months of Chaitra and Pausha <note>roughly March-April and December-January</note>, and avoiding Tuesdays, the ninth lunar day, and the harmful "poison-vessel" astrological timings. When performed with the fixed constellations, one should eat from vessels made of bronze and other such materials. || 23 ||

## ( The Use of Footwear and the Making of Ornaments )

The enjoyment of comfortable beds and footwear <note>paduka</note> should occur under the fixed, soft, and divine constellations <note>specifically Shravana, Jyeshtha, Dhanishtha, and Rohini</note>, and on the days of Mercury, Jupiter, or the Sun. The crafting of ornaments should be done under fixed, movable, or light constellations; this may be done on any day of the week, including during the auspicious timing known as Pushkara. || 24 ||

If the ornaments are made of gemstones, they should be crafted under the fierce or sharp constellations on the days of the Sun or Mars. If they are made of pearls and similar materials, they should be crafted under soft, light, or movable constellations, or on the days of Venus or the Moon, avoiding the "empty" lunar days <note>Rikta tithis: the 4th, 9th, and 14th</note> and the New Moon. || 25 ||

## ( The Three-Fold Increase Yoga )

If a "Blessed" lunar day <note>Bhadra tithi: 2nd, 7th, or 12th</note> falls on a Saturday, Tuesday, or Sunday, and coincides with specific constellations <note>Vishakha, Uttarashadha, Purvabhadrapada, Punarvasu, Krittika, or Uttaraphalguni</note>, the configuration is called Tripushkara <note>The Three-Fold Increase</note>. This yoga causes any event—gain or loss—to be tripled in its effect. If it occurs with the constellations Dhanishtha, Mrigashira, or Chitra, the effect of loss or gain is doubled. || 26 ||

## ( Prohibitions when the Moon is in Aquarius or Pisces )

While the Lord of the Night <note>the Moon</note> moves through the two signs of the Water-pot and the Fish <note>Aquarius and Pisces</note>, one should not prepare beds or canopies, thatch a roof, perform funeral rites, travel toward the southern direction <note>the direction of Yama, the god of death</note>, or collect wood and grass. || 27 ||

## ( Knowledge of Good and Evil from the Onset of Disease )

If a disease begins under the three "Eastern" constellations <note>Purvaphalguni, Purvashadha, Purvabhadrapada</note>, or under Ardra, Jyeshtha, Ashlesha, or Swati, there may be no recovery of life.

<meta>This page continues the technical astrological instructions for health, recovery, and daily activities based on lunar mansions (Nakshatras) and planetary alignments.</meta>

<page-num>14</page-num>

In the lunar mansions of Anuradha <note>associated with the deity Mitra</note> and Revati <note>associated with Pushan</note>, recovery from a very dangerous illness occurs after one month. In Uttarashadha <note>associated with the Vishvedevas</note>, one becomes free of disease in 28 days. 28

In Magha <note>associated with the Pitris</note>, recovery takes 20 days <note>original: "nakhaih," a numerical code for 20</note>. In Hasta, it takes 15 days <note>a fortnight</note>. In Dhanishta, Vishakha, Krittika, or Ashwini, it takes a specific period. In Mula, it takes 9 days. In Bharani, Shatabhisha, Shravana, or Chitra, it takes 11 days. In the mansion known as Purvabhadrapada, 29

In Punarvasu, Pushya, Uttaraphalguni, or Rohini, freedom from disease is said to occur after seven nights. However, if the stars or the Moon are unfavorable here, or if the lunar day <term>Tithi: a lunar day in the Hindu calendar</term> is particularly inauspicious, the suffering will be severe. 30

If the Moon at the time of the patient's illness falls in the same "pulse" <note>Nadi: a classification of stars used to determine temperament and compatibility</note> as the Sun's current position, and falls within the groups of stars starting with Anuradha, Ardra, Bharani, Jyeshtha, Vishakha, Dhanishta, Uttaraphalguni, Shatabhisha, Purvaphalguni, Punarvasu, Ashwini, Magha, Vishakha, Shravana, Purvabhadrapada, or Ashlesha; or the second group including Pushya, Chitra, Revati, Purvashadha, Ashlesha, Uttarabhadrapada, or Mrigashira; or the third group including Uttarashadha—then death may occur. 31

### ( Taking Medicine )

In the movable, light, and soft stars <note>categories of Nakshatras</note> and in Mula; when the rising sign is a dual-natured sign; when Venus, Jupiter, Mercury, or the Moon are present; on their respective weekdays or on a Sunday; when the 12th, 7th, and 8th houses are astrologically vacant; on an auspicious lunar day; and when the birth star is not afflicted and the beneficial planets are strong—then the wise say one should administer medicine. 32

### ( The Bath After Recovery from Illness )

When the Moon is not in an opposing position, on days other than Friday or Monday, during a movable rising sign, on a day that is not a "void" lunar day <term>Rikta Tithi: the 4th, 9th, or 14th lunar days, considered hollow or unlucky</term>, and avoiding the fixed, ending, or sharp stars—a man should bathe at the end of his illness to signify recovery. 33

### ( Snake Bite )

A snake bite occurring in the two stars associated with serpents <note>Ashlesha</note>, or in Mula, Ardra, or Bharani, or in the "mixed" stars, leads to death.

### ( Planting Vines and Trees )

The planting of vines and similar plants should be done in the soft, fixed, or swift stars, or in Shatabhisha, Mula, or Vishakha. 34

### ( Reservoirs of Water )

In Purvashadha, Shravana, Shatabhisha, Magha, Anuradha, Revati, Pushya, the fixed stars, Dhanishta, or Hasta...

<page-num>15</page-num>

If Jupiter and Mercury are in the ascendant <note>the zodiac sign rising on the eastern horizon</note>, and Venus and the Sun are also present, while the Moon is in a water sign <note>Cancer, Scorpio, or Pisces</note>, a reservoir of water should be constructed. || 35 ||

A wise person should avoid the days of the Sun and Mars, the lunar days of loss <note>Tithi-kshaya</note>, and the "Empty" days <note>Rikta-tithis: the 4th, 9th, and 14th days of the lunar fortnight</note>. Avoiding the signs of Aries, Leo, and Capricorn in the ascendant, one should perform the digging of a reservoir when the Moon is favorable and positioned in the "downward-looking" stars <note>Adhomukha Nakshatras</note>. || 36 ||

### ( Dancing and Singing )

Perform dancing and singing during the stars of Dhanishtha, Shatabhishak, Jyeshtha, Mrigashira, Hasta, Revati, Pushya, Anuradha, and the three Uttaras. This should be done when Mercury is in a sign ruled by Mercury, or when Mercury is in the ascendant, provided the fourth house is not occupied by Jupiter and is graced by the sight of benefic planets. || 37 ||

### ( Visiting the King )

Visiting the King <note>or high-ranking officials</note> should be done under the "soft," "fixed," and "swift" stars, as well as the two stars of Vishnu <note>Shravana and Dhanishtha</note>, on a Sunday—

### ( Religious Acts )

—and then, religious acts.
These should be performed under the "movable," "fixed," and "swift" groups of stars, as well as the "friendly" stars, when the ascendant is strong and in the divisional charts of Mercury or Jupiter. || 38 ||

### ( Wearing Armor, Arrows, and Swords )

Under the "fixed," "soft," and "light" stars, as well as those of Indra, Aditi, and Vishakha; on the days of Jupiter, Venus, and the Sun; and when Mercury is in a "fixed" sign during a favorable lunar day: one should take up the wearing of armor, arrows, and swords.

### ( Consuming Alcohol )

Then, for the consumption of alcohol, the ancient texts prescribe the "sharp" stars, Shatabhishak, and the "fierce" stars. || 39 ||

### ( Archery )

Under the two stars of Yama <note>Bharani</note>, the two stars of Aditi <note>Punarvasu</note>, the three stars of Hasta, the two stars of Vishnu <note>Shravana</note>, Revati, Mula, Mrigashira, the "fixed" stars, the two stars of the Ancestors <note>Magha</note>, and excluding Pushya: archery should be practiced, specifically during the "Awakening of Vishnu" <note>the festival of Vishnu Prabodhini</note>. || 40 ||

->( The Blind and Other Classifications of Stars and Their Results )<-

The stars <note>Nakshatras</note>, including the intercalary Abhijit, are classified as blind, dull-sighted, weak-sighted, and good-sighted. Counting from the star of the Creator <note>Rohini</note>, if the current star falls in the "blind" pair, there will be gain; however, this is not the case in the latter two categories. || 41 ||

->( Whether Lost Wealth Can Be Recovered )<-

If wealth is deposited, given away, invested, or lost during the Fixed, Fierce, Mixed, or Sharp stars, or during an eclipse, that wealth will not be recovered by the owner. || 42 ||

->( Considerations on Lending, Debt Clearance, and Accumulation of Wealth )<-

When the stars Ashvini, Vishakha, the Moveable stars, Pushya, or Anuradha are present, and the rising sign <term>Lagna<gloss>the zodiac sign rising on the eastern horizon</gloss></term> is a Moveable one, it is a time for financial investment. One should not give money when Mercury is in the eighth, sixth, or twelfth houses, nor should one take a loan on a Tuesday, as it will not lead to prosperity. || 43 ||

On the day of a solar transit <note>Sankranti</note>, or during the stars Hasta or Bharani, or on a Sunday, taking a loan does not lead to good fortune. The lords of the sages declare that clearing a debt is best on a Tuesday, while the accumulation of wealth should be done on a Wednesday. || 44 ||

->( Considerations on Serving a Master )<-

When the stars are of the Swift or Friendly class, on a Sunday or Wednesday, with a suspicious rising sign and the Sun in the tenth or eleventh house, and when there is planetary friendship between Mars and the lords of the zodiac signs, a master accompanied by a good army should indeed be served by a servant. || 45 ||

->( Arts and Crafts )<-

When the stars are Gentle, Fixed, Swift, or Moveable, and Jupiter or Mercury are in the house of the rising sign or the tenth house, and the Moon is in the divisional charts of Mercury or Jupiter—
**The study of arts and crafts—**

->( Alliances )<-

**And when Jupiter is a friend—**
->On the days of the benefic planets, an alliance is said to be successful. || 46 ||<-

->( Forming Friendships )<-

On the twelfth and eighth lunar days, and during the Taitila <term>Karana<gloss>an astrological period equal to half a lunar day</gloss></term>, it is auspicious. When the rising sign is aspected by Venus, that day is said to be fit for men to form friendships. || 47 ||

### ( Agricultural Work and Sowing of Seeds )

Agricultural work is considered auspicious when the moon resides in the movable, fixed, swift, or soft <term>Nakshatras</term> <note>Lunar mansions or star clusters used in Vedic astrology</note>, as well as in those dedicated to the Dual Divinities <note>Vishakha</note>, Mula, or the Ancestors <note>Magha</note>. One should avoid the "Empty" days <note>the 4th, 9th, and 14th days of the lunar month</note>, the 8th and 6th lunar days, movable <term>Lagnas</term> <note>ascendant signs</note>, and the signs of Aquarius and Leo, as well as the days of Saturn, Mars, and the Sun. ॥ 48 ॥

When the Moon and Venus are strong, and the Moon is in a watery sign <note>Cancer, Scorpio, or Pisces</note>, with Jupiter in the ascendant and the malefic planets being weak, agriculture is favored. Furthermore, according to the teachings of the sage Narada and others, one should calculate the position of the Sun: the stars from the Sun's position up to the 3rd, 10th, and 11th, and the 8th star, are considered beneficial for starting work. ॥ 49 ॥

The sowing of all types of seeds should be done during the soft, fixed, swift, or "windy" <note>Svati</note> constellations, or in Dhanishta, Magha, and Mula. However, one must avoid the three days following the Sun's entry into the constellation Ardra, as well as Tuesdays and inauspicious ascendant signs. ॥ 50 ॥

Counting from the constellation occupied by Rahu <note>the North Node of the Moon</note> for eight stars, and the four stars starting from the twenty-fourth star from the Sun, these are not auspicious for the sowing of seeds. ॥ 51 ॥

### ( Harvesting of Grain )

Harvesting of grain should be performed when the Moon is in Revati, Ashwini, Rohini, Purva Phalguni, Vishakha, Anuradha, Punarvasu, or Shatabhisha. One must avoid Saturdays, Tuesdays, and the "Empty" lunar days, and ensure the ascendant is a movable sign. ॥ 52 ॥

### ( Threshing of Grain )

Threshing <note>literally: crushing of the ears of grain</note> is excellent during the three constellations of Magha, the two constellations of Jyeshtha and Vishakha, and in Rohini, Anuradha, Revati, and Shravana.

### ( Planting of Crops )

The planting of crops is auspicious on Sundays when the Moon is in Mula, Uttaraphalguni, Vishakha, or Shatabhisha. ॥ 53 ॥

### ( Storage of Grain )

Avoiding the cruel and mixed constellations, as well as those dedicated to Shiva, Indra, and the Serpents, and avoiding the signs of Cancer, Aries, and Aquarius; the storage of grain on a good day leads to increase, especially under the influence of Jupiter or in the movable constellations, the three "Ushas," the two "Purvas," and Ashwini. ॥ 54 ॥

### ( Construction of Boats )

The construction of boats should take place during movable, "three-paired," soft, or Mula constellations, and during the swift constellations on the days of Venus, Jupiter, or the Sun.

When the Ascendant <note>the zodiac sign rising on the eastern horizon</note> is auspicious and the Moon is strong, the construction of a boat is ordained for prosperity. 55

->( The Steering of Boats )<-

Under the three stars of Vishnu <note>Shravana, Dhanishta, and Shatabhisha</note>, Mrigashira, Ashwini, and likewise Purva Phalguni and Anuradha; on an auspicious lunar day and Monday, the first steering of a boat upon the water should occur. 56

->( Setting Sail )<-

Under Mrigashira, the two stars of Vishnu <note>Shravana and Dhanishta</note>, the three "Light" stars <note>Ashwini, Pushya, and Hasta</note>, the "Eastern" stars <note>the Purvas</note>, and Anuradha; on an auspicious lunar day and under a favorable Moon and star, a voyage by boat is auspicious on a favorable day. 57

->( Purgatives, Vomiting, and Bloodletting )<-

Under Chitra, Anuradha, Rohini, Shatabhisha, the "Light" stars, and on the days of Jupiter, the Sun, or Mars, the administration of purgatives, emetics, or bloodletting quickly brings about health. 58

->( Heroic Exorcism )<-

Under Magha, Jyeshtha, Mula, Bharani, or Shatabhisha; when the Ascendant is Aquarius, and Mercury is in the fourth house and Venus is favorable; when the eighth house is empty of planets, and on the days of the Sun or Mars, success is achieved in <term>Virabhichara</term> <note>a ritual practice involving the invocation of heroic spirits or fierce deities for protection or overcoming enemies</note>. 59

->( Ordeals and Oaths )<-

On the days of Saturday or Tuesday, in a fixed Ascendant, when a malefic planet is rising, or when the Moon, Venus, or Jupiter are combust <note>too close to the sun to be seen</note>; or when the Sun is in the eighth house or during an intercalary month <note>an extra month added to the lunar calendar to sync with the solar year</note>, one should not perform a <term>Divya</term> <note>a divine ordeal or sacred oath used to determine guilt or innocence</note>. If performed then, it will produce the opposite of the desired result. 60

->( Rites of Peace and Prosperity )<-

Under the Moveable, Fixed, and Swift stars, and Magha, Vishakha, and Revati, the <term>Shantika</term> <note>rites to avert evil or misfortune</note> and <term>Paushtika</term> <note>rites to increase wealth or health</note> should be performed. This should be done when the Moon is in the fourth house, the Sun in the tenth, and Jupiter in the Ascendant, or whenever there is a pressing cause. 61

->( Consideration of Fire Offerings )<-

Counting from the Sun's star in groups of three, when the Moon reaches these: the Sun, Mercury, Venus, the Sun, the Moon, and Mars... <note>The text breaks off here, describing the calculation for the "residence of fire" to determine if burnt offerings will be accepted by the gods.</note>

Jupiter, the Sun, and also Ketu <note>original: "Shikhi"; the descending lunar node</note> are considered powerful. However, a sacrificial ritual <term>Homavidhi: a fire offering</term> is not recommended when a cruel planet is in power. || 62 ||

->( The Abode of Fire )<-

Take the number of lunar days starting from the bright fortnight, add one, and add the number of the current weekday. If this sum is divided by four (4) and the remainder is three (3) or zero (0), the Abode of Fire is on Earth, which brings happiness. If the remainder is one (1) or two (2), there is not even a trace of happiness. || 63 ||

->( Success in Undertakings )<-

When the 8th and 12th houses are pure <note>free from malefic planets</note>, and the Ascendant and the Moon are in the houses of increase <note>the 3rd, 6th, 10th, or 11th houses</note>, all types of work will reach success.

->( Rites for the Deceased )<-

Rituals for those who have departed to the other world <note>funeral or ancestral rites</note> should be performed under the constellations of Shravana, Jyeshtha, Mula, Ashlesha, or Swati, or during "swift" constellations. || 64 ||

->( Auspicious and Inauspicious Results of Birth in Mula and Other Stars )<-

Birth in the first three quarters of the Mula constellation is hostile to the father, the mother, and wealth, respectively. The fourth quarter is said to be auspicious. The noble sages say the results for the Ashlesha constellation are exactly the opposite. || 65 ||

The final hour <term>Ghatika: a unit of 24 minutes</term> of Jyeshtha and the first two hours of Mula are known by others as "Unenjoyable Mula" <note>Abhukta-mula; a particularly inauspicious time for birth</note>. Others say this period lasts for five hours in Jyeshtha and eight hours in Mula. || 66 ||

Others say the combined four hours <note>two from each star</note> are the danger zone; a child born then should be abandoned. Or, the father should not look upon the child's face for eight years unless a pacification ritual <term>Shanti: a restorative religious rite</term> has been performed. || 67 ||

---

--- Page 38 ---
<meta>This page continues the technical discussion of astrological timing and the physical descriptions of the lunar mansions (Nakshatras) used in Vedic divination.</meta>

<page-num>20</page-num>

If the first quarter of the Mula <note>The 19th lunar mansion, often associated with roots and foundational power, but considered volatile</note> constellation occurs at night, it causes harm to the father. If the second quarter occurs during the day, it causes harm to the mother. This affliction <note>original: "Rishta" - a term for astrological ill-fortune or physical danger</note> is not pacified by ordinary means; peace is only found after a year of purification, or perhaps after three months in that specific lunar sign. || 68 ||

Multiply the duration of the Mula constellation by six to get 36. Twenty-one of these units are considered auspicious. The first five units belong to the Sun (12), and from the 27 constellations, seven are assigned to humans. || 69 ||

In the months of Nabhas (Shravana), Chaitra, Urja (Kartika), and Pausha, the influence resides on the Earth; in other months, it resides in the other worlds. When the influence is situated on the Earth, it is full of faults and difficulties; elsewhere, the negative effects are minimal. || 70 ||

->( Praise of the Pushya Constellation )<-

Even if pierced by a cruel planet, or joined with one, and even if deprived of the strength of the stars and the Moon, the Pushya <note>The 8th lunar mansion, considered the most auspicious for spiritual and worldly work</note> constellation remains effective for marriage and the success of all daily tasks. It destroys even the most superior faults.

->( The Forms of the Constellations )<-

The horse's face (Ashvini), the female organ (Bharani), the razor (Krittika), the cart (Rohini), the deer's head (Mrigashira), the rising jewel (Ardra), the house (Punarvasu), the arrow (Pushya), the wheel (Ashlesha), the house-bed (Magha), the couch (Purva Phalguni), and the hand (Hasta).
The pearl (Chitra), the red jewel (Svati), the beautiful archway (Vishakha), the row of earrings (Anuradha), the lion (Jyeshtha), the bed (Mula), the elephant's tusk (Purva Ashadha), the tripod (Uttara Ashadha), the drum (Shravana), the pair of beds (Dhanishta and Shatabhisha), and the double-headed drum (Purva Bhadrapada). || 72 ||

The form of Abhijit <note>The "intercalary" 28th constellation</note> is a triangle. These are the forms of the stars. From the constellation at the mid-heaven to the constellation of the Sun, one should count them. Multiply by twenty, divide by nine, and the remainder is said to indicate the night-watch. || 73 ||

When the two Ashvinis are at the mid-heaven, Cancer is at 21 degrees; the Lion (Leo) is at 9 degrees; when the two fires (Krittika) are present, the Virgin (Virgo), the Moon, and the Deer (Capricorn) are at 10 degrees.

<meta>This page continues the technical astronomical calculations regarding the positions and star-counts of the lunar mansions (Nakshatras). It concludes the section on Nakshatras within the larger work.</meta>

By fourteen <note>original: "shakra", referring to the number 14</note> degrees in the mansion of Shiva <note>Ardra</note>, and the same in Aditi <note>Punarvasu</note>; by three <note>original: "guna"</note> degrees in Pushya. In Ashlesha, by fourteen degrees and three degrees. In the serpent-mansion <note>Ashlesha</note>, by nine <note>original: "ghana", 3 cubed</note> degrees; in Magha by eight <note>original: "vasu"</note> degrees; in the Purbaphalguni by nine degrees and in the Bhagya mansion <note>Uttaraphalguni</note> by seven degrees. 74

In the mansion of Aryaman <note>Uttaraphalguni</note>, Sagittarius begins at six <note>original: "anga"</note> degrees; in Hasta at twenty <note>original: "nakha"</note> degrees; in Chitra at twenty-four <note>original: "jina"</note> degrees. In Swati at four <note>original: "veda"</note> degrees, Capricorn begins at eighteen <note>original: "dhriti"</note> degrees; in Vishakha, Anuradha, and Jyeshtha <note>the triad of Mitra</note> by two, eight, and twenty-six <note>original: "danti-anga-lochana"</note> degrees respectively. In Purvashadha, Aquarius begins at fifteen <note>original: "vishva"</note> degrees; in Uttarashadha at twenty-two <note>original: "tithi"</note> degrees; and in Shravana at twenty-four <note>original: "nayana-ashvi"</note> degrees. In Shatabhisha, Pisces begins at seventeen <note>original: "jina"</note> degrees. 75

Aries begins at nine <note>original: "ghana"</note> degrees in Ashvini; Taurus begins in Rohini. In Dhanishta and Shatabhisha, the sign is Capricorn. By five <note>original: "shara"</note> degrees in the Purva mansions and eleven <note>original: "shiva"</note> in the Uttara mansions. Cancer begins in Ashlesha, and in the final mansion <note>Revati</note>, Cancer begins at four <note>original: "yuga"</note> degrees. 76

### (The Number of Stars in the Nakshatras)

There is one star each for Swati and Chitra. For the three Purva and three Uttara mansions, there are three stars each. There are three stars for Shravana <note>Vishnu</note>, Punarvasu <note>Jiva</note>, Abhijit, Rohini <note>Chandra</note>, Jyeshtha <note>Indra</note>, Ashvini, and Bharani <note>Antaka</note>. There are four stars for Vishakha <note>the dual-deity</note>, Dhanishta <note>Vasava</note>, Anuradha <note>Mitra</note>, and Aditi <note>Punarvasu</note>. There are five stars for Hasta and Rohini. There are five stars for Magha and Ashlesha <note>the serpent</note>. There are six stars for Krittika <note>the fire-mansion</note>. There are thirty <note>original: "danta", meaning teeth</note> stars for Revati, and one hundred for Shatabhisha <note>Varuna's mansion</note> and Mula. 78

### ( The Flower of the Sun's Transit )
<note>original: "Sankranti-kusumam". This title refers to a poetic or systematic arrangement of the rules governing the Sun's entry into a new zodiac sign.</note>

### ( The Names of the Transits such as 'Ghora' and Their Results )

The Transit is called *Ghora* (The Terrible) if it occurs on a Sunday or under "fierce" constellations; it is *Dhwankshi* (The Crow-like) on a Monday or under "light" constellations. It is declared *Mahodari* (The Big-Bellied) on a Tuesday or under "mobile" constellations; it is known as *Mandakini* (The Gentle River) on a Wednesday or under "soft" constellations, and on a Thursday under "fixed" constellations. 1.

->It is *Manda* (The Slow) on a Friday under "mixed" constellations,<-
->and on a Saturday under "sharp" constellations, it is *Rakshasi* (The Demonic).<-
->These respectively bring satisfaction to Shudras <note>the laborer class</note>, Vaishyas <note>the merchant class</note>, thieves, kings,<-
->Brahmins <note>the priestly class</note>, cattle, and the outcaste people. 2.<-

->If the transit occurs in the middle of a day divided into three parts,<-
->or in the first or last part, or at sunset, it affects the Brahmins and others.<-
->Likewise, during the watches of the night, it destroys spirits,<-
->night-wanderers <note>demons or nocturnal creatures</note>, actors, and cowherds. 3.<-

->If the Sun's transit occurs at sunrise, it affects the heretics.<-
->If the transit is auspicious, it brings well-being; if it is sinful, it brings sorrow.<-
->A transit occurring under one's birth star never brings happiness. 4.<-

### ( Consideration of the Meritorious Time for Transits )

When the Sun enters Taurus, Aquarius, Scorpio, or Leo, the 16 <note>sixteen</note> ghatikas <note>a unit of time equal to 24 minutes</note> before the transit are considered holy. When entering Gemini, Virgo, Sagittarius, or Pisces, the 16 ghatikas following the transit are the faultless ones.

<summary>This page details the astrological classification of the Sun's transit (Sankranti) into various categories like Ghora and Rakshasi based on the day and lunar mansion, predicting their effects on different social classes and the specific timing for merit-making.</summary>
<keywords>Sankranti, Astrology, Zodiac, Ghora, Rakshasi, Punyakala, Social Classes, Time Measurement</keywords>

--- Page 41 ---
<meta>This page continues the technical discussion of astronomical transitions (Sankranti) and the specific auspicious time windows (Punya-kala) associated with different zodiac signs and planetary movements.</meta>

<page-num>( 23 )</page-num>

When the Sun enters Aries or Libra, the auspicious time is thirty <term>Ghatikas</term> <note>A Ghatika is a traditional unit of time equal to 24 minutes</note> both before and after the transit. In Cancer, the final twenty Ghatikas are sacred, while in Capricorn, some wise scholars state the auspicious period is doubled <note>forty Ghatikas</note>. 5

### (Specific Rules for Cancer and Capricorn)

If the transit into Capricorn occurs before midnight, the second half of the preceding day is highly meritorious. If it occurs after midnight, the following half-day is auspicious. If the full midnight is reached, both days are considered. If the transit into Capricorn occurs after sunset but before sunrise, the following half-day is chosen. If it is Cancer, the first or second half of the day is used; for these two solstices, ritual bathing and charity are permitted even at night. 6

The moments closest to the transit are the most fruitful. Wise men declare the day to be meritorious when the Sun, adjusted for the precession of the equinoxes <note>original: "Sayana"</note>, reaches the zero-degree point of a sign.

### (Auspicious Times for the Moon and Other Planets)

The auspicious periods for the planets, starting from the Moon, are as follows: one Ghatika <note>for the Moon</note>, four <note>for Mars</note>, three and a quarter <note>for Mercury</note>, four <note>for Jupiter</note>, four and a half <note>for Venus</note>, four <note>for Saturn</note>, and eight <note>for Rahu and Ketu</note>. These are the durations that grant merit in their respective order. 7

### (The Personification of the Transit according to the Karana)

<note>In Indian astrology, the 'Sankranti' or transit is personified as a deity whose appearance changes based on the 'Karana' (half-day lunar period) in which it occurs. This helps practitioners visualize the 'quality' of the time.</note>

In the **Vava** <term>Karana</term>, the Transit deity wears white garments, eats cooked rice, and is a young girl. She sits upon a lion, holds a <term>Bhushundi</term> <note>an ancient spiked weapon or catapult</note>, and wears a garland of Punnaga flowers. She belongs to the divine caste and has musk marked upon her forehead. 8

In the **Balava** <term>Karana</term>, she wears yellow garments, eats milk-rice porridge, and holds a mace. She wears a garland of jasmine flowers, sits upon a tiger, and is anointed with soft saffron. She is a maiden of the 'Bhuta' <note>spirit/elemental</note> class. 9

->In the **Kaulava** <term>Karana</term>, she wears blue garments, holds a sword, and<-
->rides a boar. She is anointed with fine sandalwood paste and belongs to the serpent class.<-
->She eats food obtained by begging, is of an innocent age,<-
->and wears a garland of Bakula flowers. 10<-

In the fourth <term>Karana</term> <note>Taitila</note>, she eats fried foods, rides a donkey, and is depicted as sleeping. She has earth marked on her forehead, a garland of Ketaki flowers on her body, holds a staff in her hand, wears pale white clothes, belongs to the bird class, and is a young woman. 11

<meta>This page continues the astrological descriptions of the personified Transit (Sankranti) as she moves through the various Karana (astrological time-segments). It focuses on her physical appearance, diet, and vehicle to predict economic outcomes.</meta>

<page-num>24</page-num>

->**The Personifications of the Transit in the Karana**<-

Dressed in red garments, seated upon a maddened elephant, she drinks milk and wears yellow orpiment <note>original: "rochana"; a bright yellow pigment used for tilaks</note>. Holding a bow and wearing a single garland of Bilva leaves, she is a mature woman and a cow; this is her form in the **Gara** Karana. 12

Dressed in black garments, holding an iron club <note>original: "tomara"; a javelin or iron-tipped staff</note>, she is of the deer-species and seated upon a buffalo. She eats curds, holds barley, and is bold and proud; adorned with Arka flowers, this is her form in the **Vanija** Karana. 13

Dressed in dark-blue garments, she eats multicolored food; she is a twice-born <note>original: "dvija"; referring to the priestly or scholarly class</note> elderly woman seated upon a horse. Her forehead possesses the pride of a cat <note>likely referring to a specific mark or fierce expression</note>, and she holds Durva grass and a spear; this is her form in the **Vishti** Karana. 14

Dressed in variegated garments and standing upright, she eats molasses and holds a noose; she wears a mark of turmeric and is a barren woman. Wearing a garland of folded hands <note>original: "anjalimala"</note> and mounted on a horse, she is of the kingly class; this is the well-dressed Transit in the **Shakuni** Karana. 15

Sleeping on a goat, holding a goad, she eats honey and is of the merchant class with collyrium <note>black eye-salve</note> on her forehead. Adorned with Jasmine and wrapped in a blanket, she is sleeping and barren; this is her form in the **Chatuspada** <note>literally "four-footed"</note> Karana. 16

With a cow as her vehicle, holding a weapon, she is of the laborer class, naked, and eats clarified butter <note>ghee</note>; she desires a son. She is sleeping, with black aloe-wood paste on her forehead, and wears a garland of Trumpet-flowers; this is her form in the **Naga** Karana. 17

Anointed with camphor, she eats sugar; her clothes are the color of clouds and she holds an arrow. She is a renunciant <note>original: "sannyasini"</note>, associated with a wild cock and rich in Hibiscus flowers; born of the **Kimstughna** Karana, she is of mixed-caste and stands upright.

### ( Consideration of High and Low Prices from the Transit )

The vehicle, garments, and food associated with her will become expensive <note>original: "mahargha"</note>; for those whose livelihood depends on these items, it is not favorable. Likewise, whether she is standing, lying down, or seated determines whether prices will be high, low, or middling. 19

### ( The Forms of the Transit )

(45) If the Transit occurs during the constellations of the Great Fixed ones, the Sun, Vishakha, Ardra, the Serpent, the Waters, Indra, or the Wind <note>These refer to specific Nakshatras: Rohini, Punarvasu, etc.</note>, the results are categorized as low (15), others as middling (30), or equal. This is the observation of the Transit. 20

Then there shall be low prices, high prices, or stability in grains and other goods based on the Sun and other planets.

<meta>This page continues the discussion on astrological timing (Muhurta), specifically concluding the section on solar transits (Sankranti) and beginning the section on planetary transits (Gochara).</meta>

<page-num>25</page-num>

...from strength, all matters regarding kings, warfare, education, marriage, travel, and initiation <note>original: "diksha"</note> should be considered. 21

### (Determining Market Conditions from the Transit Star)

If the <term>Nakshatra</term> <note>lunar mansion or star sign</note> of the current solar transit <note>Sankranti</note> is the 1st, 2nd, or 3rd from the previous transit's star, then goods will be "Samargha" <note>available at a fair or stable price</note>. If it is the 4th or 5th, prices will be high <note>original: "mahargha"</note>. The 6th star is not considered auspicious. 22

### (Results of the Sun's Transit based on the Moon)

The results of the Sun's transit into a new sign are determined by the strength of the Moon at that specific moment.

### (The Hierarchy of Planetary Strength)

The Moon derives its strength from the stars; the Sun derives its strength from the Moon; and all other planets derive their strength from the Sun. 23

### (Auspicious and Inauspicious Results from the Transit Cycle)

Counting from the star preceding the transit, group the stars into sets of three and six repeatedly. If one's birth star <note>original: "svabham"</note> falls within a group of three, it brings labor and sorrow; if it falls within a group of six, it brings all forms of happiness.

### (Brief Explanation of the Lost and Extra Months)

A month with two solar transits is known as a <term>Kshaya-masa</term> <note>a "lost" or decayed month</note>, while a month with no solar transit becomes an <term>Adhika-masa</term> <note>an "extra" or intercalary month</note>. This occurs approximately every 32 months and 15 days. A lost month occasionally occurs during the three months starting with Kartika <note>October/November</note>.
Some authorities define a month as ending with the New Moon <note>Amanta system</note>, while others define it by the Sun's completion of a degree. If a transit occurs during the overlap of these two theories, the month is considered "confused" or "void" <note>Mudha</note>; this is the teaching of the *Brahma Siddhanta*.
After thoroughly examining many texts, this work was composed by Vitthala Dikshita. Thus ends the "Flower" <note>chapter</note> titled "Sankranti" in the book named *Muhurtakalpadruma* <note>The Desire-Fulfilling Tree of Electional Astrology</note>. 27

---

### (The Flower of Planetary Transits)

All planets are beneficial in the 11th house <note>the house of gains</note>. <gloss>3, 6, 10, 11</gloss> The Sun and Moon are auspicious in the 3rd, 6th, and 10th houses. Mercury is auspicious in all houses except the 12th, provided they are even.

Jupiter <note>Sanskrit: *Ijya*</note> is auspicious in the second, fifth, seventh, ninth, and eleventh houses from the birth sign.
<gloss>Auspicious</gloss> Venus <gloss>is good in all houses except the 6th, 7th, and 10th</gloss> while Mars and Saturn are good in three houses <note>the 3rd, 6th, and 11th</note>. || 1 ||

### (Results of the Sun and other planets from the Birth Sign)

#### (The Sun)
Travel <note>on a difficult journey</note>, loss of wealth, prosperity, disease, misery, and happiness;
Success in expeditions, sickness, sin, comfort, wealth, and physical pain.

#### (The Moon)
Food <note>abundance</note>, loss, prosperity, disease, mental agony, wealth, and beauty;
Affliction, fear, happiness, gain, and physical pain. || 2 ||

#### (Mars)
Fear, loss, prosperity, enmity, disease, and gain;
Moral conduct, decline, sin, sickness, happiness, and sorrow.

#### (Mercury)
Bondage, gain, fear, wealth, grief, and further wealth;
Decline, money, disease, enjoyment, happiness, and pain. || 3 ||

#### (Jupiter)
Fear, wealth, disease, expenses, gain, and grief;
Happiness, affliction, honor, poverty, wealth, and sickness.

#### (Venus)
Happiness, wealth, more wealth, more happiness, joy, and pain;
Enmity, happiness, religious merit, sorrow, income, and gains. || 4 ||

#### (Saturn)
Fear, grief, prosperity, sorrow, loss, happiness, and fear;
Misery, sin, sorrow, wealth, and sin—calculated from the Sun <note>the positions relative to the natal sign</note>.

<meta>This page continues the technical discussion of planetary "Vedha" (obstruction or interference) in astrological transits, specifically detailing the numerical relationships between zodiac signs.</meta>

<page-num>27</page-num>

From the half-verse, this result should be understood: Rahu acts like Saturn, and Ketu acts like Mars <note>This is a standard rule in Vedic astrology: *Shani-vad Rahu, Kuja-vad Ketu*</note>. || 5 ||

->( Consideration of the Vedha of the Sun and other planets )<-

For the Sun, the mutual obstruction <term>Vedha: a technical term for when a planet in one house cancels the effects of a planet in another</term> occurs in the following pairs of houses:
<gloss>3 and 9; 10 and 4; 5 and 11; 12 and 6</gloss>
The obstruction is mutual between the 6th and 12th, 8th and 11th, and 10th and 4th houses.
<gloss>6-12, 8-11, 10-4</gloss>

For the Moon, the pairs are:
<gloss>5 and 1, 3 and 9, 2 and 7</gloss>
For the malefic planets excluding the Sun <note>referring to Mars, Saturn, Rahu, and Ketu</note>, the pairs are:
<gloss>5 and 11, 6 and 9, 3 and 12</gloss> || 6 ||

For Mercury <note>original: "Shashisuta," meaning son of the Moon</note>, the pairs are:
<gloss>5 and 2, 8 and 10, 6 and 9, 11 and 12</gloss>
And also:
<gloss>8 and 1, 4 and 3</gloss>

For Jupiter:
<gloss>2 and 12</gloss>

Further pairs for Jupiter:
<gloss>10 and 9, 5 and 4, 8 and 11, 3 and 7</gloss>
And then:
<gloss>8 and 1, 2 and 7, 9 and 5, 8 and 5, 9 and 11</gloss> || 7 ||

For Venus <note>original: "Bhrigu"</note>, the pairs are:
<gloss>3 and 1, 10 and 4, 11 and 3, 12 and 6</gloss>

There is no Vedha between a father and son <note>specifically referring to the Sun and Saturn</note>, nor from the birth sign <term>Janma Rashi: the sign the Moon occupied at the time of birth</term>.

---

1. This refers to the malefic planets excluding the Sun—meaning Mars, Saturn, Rahu, and Ketu.

<summary>This page outlines the specific numerical house pairings that cause "Vedha" (astrological interference) for the Sun, Moon, and planets, noting that certain familial planetary relationships are exempt from these obstructions.</summary>
