<meta>This page continues the technical discussion of planetary transits and their auspicious or inauspicious effects based on geographical location and lunar strength.</meta>

<page-num>28</page-num>

Regarding other planets, it is said that in the region between the Vindhya and Himalaya mountains <note>The central heartland of classical India, often referred to as Aryavarta</note>, a planet that pierces <term>Vedha: an astronomical interference or "piercing" where one planet obstructs the influence of another</term> another celestial body yields results; elsewhere, it is quite different. || 8 ||

Some authorities cite the sequence of "piercing" starting from the Zodiac sign, while others suggest it begins inversely from the planetary house. Within the region between the Snowy Mountains <note>The Himalayas</note> and the Vindhyas, an inauspicious piercing becomes auspicious if reversed. One who does not understand this dual nature of "piercing" is merely a seeker of falsehoods and deserves ridicule. During a period of planetary distress <note>Dousthya: a state of weakness or bad positioning</note>, one should not seek auspicious outcomes nor undertake a journey; instead, one should perform a pacification ceremony <note>Shanti: a ritual to mitigate negative astrological influences</note> according to the prescribed rules.

### ( The Strength of the Moon )

During the bright fortnight <note>Shukla Paksha: the waxing phase of the moon</note>, the Moon is auspicious when positioned in the 5th, 2nd, 9th, or 11th houses, provided it is not "pierced" by the lords of the planets located in the 4th, 6th, or 8th houses. If the Moon is caught between malefic planets, joined with a wicked planet, or in a malefic Ascendant, it is unfavorable even if otherwise in a "best" position. Conversely, if it is in a beneficial or auspicious division <term>Amsha: a fractional subdivision of a zodiac sign used for fine-tuned predictions</term>, even a poorly placed Moon may become favorable. || 11 ||

### ( The Strength of the Lunar Fortnight )

If the Moon is in the first lunar day of the white fortnight <note>The waxing phase</note>, it is auspicious and most excellent for its side. In the first part of the dark fortnight <note>Krishna Paksha: the waning phase</note>, it is not auspicious for success; otherwise, these two fortnights do not lead to auspicious results.

### ( Auspicious and Inauspicious Results of the Planets )

A planet joined with or aspected by <note>Drishiti: an astrological "glance" or aspect where one planet influences another from a distance</note> an enemy or a malefic becomes inauspicious. When joined with or aspected by a friend or a benefic, it bestows good fruit. Furthermore, if a planet is in an enemy's house, in its sign of debilitation, or is combust <note>Astaga: rendered invisible by proximity to the sun</note>, or moving in a retrograde motion <note>Anrijugati: "non-straight" or backward movement</note>, it yields the opposite of its usual results. || 13 ||

### ( Days Required for Results when Sun and Others Enter a Sign )

The Sun gives its results 5 days <insert>5</insert> before entering the target sign. Mars, Venus, and Mercury give results 7 days <insert>7</insert> before. Jupiter, Saturn, and the Nodes <note>Rahu and Ketu</note> give results over several months:
<gloss>2, 6, and 3 months respectively</gloss>
The Moon gives its results in 3 <insert>3</insert> Nadis <note>A Nadi is approximately 24 minutes; 3 Nadis equal 1 hour and 12 minutes</note>. || 14 ||

### (The Results of Planets Entering Different Zodiac Signs)

When entering a new zodiac sign, Mars and the Sun <note>the Sun is referred to here as 'Tigmabhanu', the one with piercing rays</note> give their results at the beginning of the transit. Venus and Jupiter give results in the middle of the transit. Saturn and the Moon give their results at the end, while Mercury gives results throughout the entire duration. Others <note>referring to the lunar nodes Rahu and Ketu</note> act like Saturn. || 15 ||

### (Wearing Gemstones to Pacify the Planets)

Ruby, pearl, coral, emerald, yellow sapphire, diamond, blue sapphire, hessonite, and cat's eye—these should be given or worn in this specific order to please the Sun and the other planets. || 16 ||
<margin>The order follows the traditional sequence: Sun, Moon, Mars, Mercury, Jupiter, Venus, Saturn, Rahu, and Ketu.</margin>
For the two royal planets <note>Sun and Moon</note>, one may use coral and silver; for the two brothers <note>Mars and Mercury</note>, pearls and gold; for Jupiter, gold; for Saturn, iron; and for the two dark ones <note>Rahu and Ketu</note>, Lapis Lazuli should be worn by those with little wealth to gain favor. || 17 ||

### (Medicinal Baths for Pacifying the Planets)

A bath taken with the two types of turmeric <note>Haridra and Daruharidra</note>, costus, camphor, parched rice, white mustard, bala root, perfumed cherry, and purple tephrosia, combined with lodh tree bark infused in water, is said to pacify the afflictions caused by all the planets. || 18 ||

### (Donations for the Planets)

->A milch cow, a conch shell, a red bull, and gold;<-
->yellow cloth, a white horse, and a black cow;<-
->iron and goat—these are the ritual donations for the planets.<-
->The fire offerings, worship, and other rites should be learned from local tradition. || 19 ||<-

### (Consideration of Auspicious and Inauspicious Stars and Their Charities)

->The nine stars <term>Taraka: the 27 lunar mansions divided into three cycles of nine</term> are: Birth, Wealth, Danger, Prosperity,<-
->Obstacle, Auspicious, Destruction, Friend, and Great Friend.<-
->These are powerful during the dark fortnight and equal during the bright fortnight. || 20 ||<-
Counting from the birth star through the three cycles, the third, fifth, and seventh stars are not auspicious. For all activities, one should give gifts of jaggery, salt, and sesame seeds to mitigate these effects. || 21 ||

<meta>This page continues the astrological discourse on auspicious and inauspicious timings, specifically focusing on the relationship between the Moon and the stars (Nakshatras) and the effects of eclipses.</meta>

<page-num>30</page-num>

->(Auspicious and Inauspicious Stars Based on the Birth Star)<-

->1, 10, 16, 18, 23, 24<-

The first, tenth, sixteenth, eighteenth, twenty-third, and twenty-fourth stars <note>original: "Nakshatras"</note> from the birth star are considered unfavorable for a king. Specifically, the destruction of the country or one's caste/lineage is indicated if these stars are afflicted during a transit. These particular positions are deemed harmful to the ruler's stability. || 22 ||

---

->(The Auspicious and Inauspicious Effects of Solar and Lunar Eclipses Based on the Moon’s Position and the Required Charities)<-

->11-3-6-10<-

If an eclipse of the Moon or the Sun occurs when the Moon is in the "Upachaya" <note>The 3rd, 6th, 10th, and 11th houses of a natal chart, associated with growth and improvement</note> positions, it brings good fortune. If the Moon is in the second, fifth, seventh, or ninth positions, the result is mediocre. However, if the Moon is in the first, fourth, eighth, or twelfth houses at the time of an eclipse, it brings great suffering. In such cases, one should donate a gold serpent <note>representing Rahu/Ketu, the eclipse shadows</note> along with clothing and tilak. || 23 ||

---

->(The Duration and Names of the Moon’s States, and the Arrangement of Lunar and Stellar Strength)<-

The "states" <note>Avasthas</note> of the Moon last for 11 Ghatikas and 15 Palas <note>approximately 4.5 hours</note>. There are twelve such states in each zodiac sign. Their names are:
1. Exile <note>Pravasa</note>
2. Loss <note>Nashta</note>
3. Death <note>Mrita</note>
4. Victory <note>Jaya</note>
5. Laughter <note>Hasya</note>
6. <gloss>1</gloss>Joy <note>Mudu-priti</note>
7. Play <note>Kridana</note>
8. Sleep <note>Supta</note>
9. Enjoyment <note>Bhukta</note>
10. Fever <note>Jvara</note>
11. Trembling <note>Prakampa</note>
12. Stability <note>Sthira</note>
The results of these states correspond to their names; they are auspicious if the name implies a positive condition. || 24 ||

During the waxing phase <note>Shukla Paksha</note>, the Moon is powerful. Regarding the strength of the stars <note>Tarabala</note>, they are most potent during the waning phase <note>Krishna Paksha</note>. When both are strong, it is most excellent. For journeys, shaving/haircutting, taking medicine, or formal debates, a weak star strength is condemned; however, for other activities, the first strength is sufficient. The lunar strength <note>Chandra-bala</note> should be examined for all undertakings, for when the Moon is favorable, all other planets <note>term: Kheta - celestial bodies or planets</note> become favorable. || 26 ||

---

<note>1. The gloss clarifies the division between "Joy" and "Play."</note>

<meta>This page continues the astrological treatise by Vitthala Dikshita, transitioning from remedial donations for inauspicious times to a comprehensive list of astrological prohibitions.</meta>

<page-num>31</page-num>

### ( Now, the donations to be given when dates or weekdays are inauspicious )

It is said that one should donate rice on an inauspicious lunar day <term>Tithi; a lunar day in the Hindu calendar</term>, a conch shell when the Moon is poorly placed, and gems on an unlucky weekday. One should give gold during an inauspicious astrological combination <term>Yoga</term>, grain during an unlucky half-day <term>Karana</term>, a cow during an inauspicious subtle time-unit <term>Nadi</term>, and salt when the lunar mansion <term>Nakshatra</term> is corrupted. After thoroughly examining many texts, this chapter titled "The Flower of Good Planetary Transits" <note>original: "Sadgocara-kusumam"</note> is thus completed by Vitthala Dikshita in his work named the *Tree of Desires for Electional Astrology* <note>original: "Muhūrtakalpadruma"</note>. 28

### ( The Flower of Prohibitions )

One should avoid the faults of the lunar day, the faults of the weekday, the sun's entry into a new sign <term>Sankranti</term>, the transitional "knot" periods <term>Gandanta</term>, and the "bolt" <term>Argala</term> obstruction. Likewise, avoid the equality of declinations <note>referring to the inauspicious Kranti-samya or Mahapata</note>, the hour <term>Hora</term> of a malefic planet, the anniversary of a parent's death <term>Shraddha</term>, and the day of one's mother's birth.

One should avoid the four days surrounding the birth of a first-born child, and the thirteenth lunar day of the dark fortnight. Avoid the half-portions of the day or night associated with the birth-tithi or birth-day. Avoid the "short" months <term>Unamasa</term> and intercalary months <term>Adhimasa</term>, the constellation occupied by a planet, and the periods when Venus or Jupiter are combust <note>hidden by the sun's glare</note> or in their debilitation. Avoid the period of birth-impurity <term>Sutaka</term>, the "body-moon" <note>Chandra-shuddhi</note>, and the "scissor" formation <term>Kartari</term> caused by malefic planets. 3

Avoid the "unlucky" period <term>Bhadra</term>, the "kick" of a planet <term>Latta</term>, the malefic divisions of the Moon, the conjunction of multiple planets in one sign, and the planetary "piercing" <term>Vedha</term> of a constellation. Avoid constellations that malefic planets are approaching or have just left, as well as the poisonous time-units <term>Visha-nadi</term> and the Moon located in the twelfth house. 4

Avoid Mars in the eighth house from the ascendant, Venus in the sixth house, and the lord of the ninth Navamsha <note>a 1/9th division of a sign</note> from the Moon. Avoid faults in the ascendant and the seventh house, the lord of the eighth house (the house of death) located in the ascendant, and the ascendant falling in a malefic division. 5

Avoid the eighth house from the birth-ascendant or birth-moon, the sign occupied by a malefic moon, and the various other collections of faults. These have been repeated here from the various "flowers" (chapters) for the sake of easy remembrance for those of modest intellect. 6

After thoroughly examining many texts, this chapter titled "The Flower of Prohibitions" <note>original: "Tyājyābhidhāna-kusumam"</note> is thus completed by Vitthala Dikshita in his work named the *Tree of Desires for Electional Astrology*. 7

## ( The Flower of Sacraments )
### ( The First Menstruation )

The first menstruation <note>Sanskrit: Prathama-artava; the onset of puberty</note> is auspicious during the bright fortnights of the months of Vaishakha, Margashirsha, Ashvina, Magha, Shravana, and Bhadrapada. It is favorable on lunar days <note>Tithis</note> other than the "Empty" days <note>Rikta: 4th, 9th, and 14th</note>, the 8th, the New Moon, or the 6th, and on the days of Mercury, Jupiter, Venus, and the Moon <note>Wednesday, Thursday, Friday, and Monday</note>. || 1 ||

->It is praised when the lunar mansion <note>Nakshatra</note> is not of the Mixed, Sharp, or Fierce categories.<-
->During the day, in the mansions of Revati or Punarvasu, it is considered middling.<-
->It is censured if it occurs during bad astrological combinations, in another's house,<-
->in a forbidden place, during a solar transit <note>Sankranti</note>, or under an inauspicious rising sign.<- || 2 ||

It is not auspicious if it occurs during twilight, an eclipse, during ancestral rites <note>Shraddha</note>, while wearing old or non-white clothes, during illness, if the flow is too little or too much, or during sleep or the "Bhadra" period. || 3 ||

If the first menstruation possesses many virtues and few defects, it leads to happiness. If it is otherwise, peace-making rituals <note>Shanti</note> must be performed for happiness. It is desired that the rising sign <note>Lagna</note> be occupied or aspected by the two Teachers <gloss>1</gloss>. || 4 ||

### ( The Bath of the Menstruating Woman )

In the fixed lunar mansions <note>Rohini, Uttara-phalguni, Uttara-ashadha, Uttara-bhadrapada</note>, or in Ashvini, Hasta, Swati, or Anuradha, on an auspicious lunar day and weekday, the woman should bathe. By bathing under Ashvini, Anuradha, Swati, Rohini, Hasta, or Mrigashirsha, she indeed attains pregnancy. || 5 ||

### ( The Rite of Conception )

->On a day when the five limbs of the calendar <note>Panchanga</note> are pure, under the mansions of Mrigashirsha, Anuradha, Hasta,<-
->Swati, the fixed stars, or the three stars of Vishnu <note>Shravana, Dhanishtha, Shatabhisha</note>, on an auspicious day;<-
->when the benefics are in the centers <note>1st, 4th, 7th, 10th houses</note> or the trines <note>5th, 9th</note> from the rising sign,<-
->and the malefics are in the 11th, 6th, or 3rd houses, at night.<- || 6 ||

When the Moon is in a masculine sign or division and is strong, and when Saturn is in an odd-numbered house other than the rising sign, one should approach his wife for the sake of a son—avoiding the "Empty" days, the 6th lunar day, the transition days <note>Parva</note>, and the anniversary of a father's death. || 7 ||

---

<gloss>1. The "two Teachers" refers to Jupiter and Venus; the rising sign should be joined with or viewed by them.</gloss>

->( 33 )<-

> The final portions of the constellations Revati, Aslesha, and Jyestha <note>original: "paushnantake mula-maghoragani"; these are the 'junction points' or Gandanta</note>, the twilight periods, the day itself, the first four nights of the lunar cycle, the "calamity days" <note>days marked by omens like earthquakes</note>, the birth star, and the star calculated as the "piercing" star <note>Vedha</note> should be avoided. ||8||
> Avoid the eighth sign from the birth sign, any sign occupied by a malefic planet, and the constellations Chitra, Punarvasu, Vishakha, and Ashwini, which are considered only mediocre in quality. A man who loves his daughter <note>or desires a son; the text implies the father's role</note> should perform the journey <note>the ritual progression</note> within sixteen days of the menstrual cycle, avoiding the forbidden times. ||9||

### (The Parting of the Hair and the Quickening)
<note>Simantakarma: a ritual for the mother's mental health; Pumsavana: a ritual to favor the birth of a male child</note>

> On a lunar day that is not "empty" <note>Rikta tithis: 4th, 9th, 14th</note>, avoiding the transition days <note>Parva</note> and the sixth lunar day, on "male" weekdays <note>Sunday, Tuesday, Thursday</note>, and under male constellations <note>Nru-tara</note>. Or, the Parting of the Hair is said to be performed in the eighth or sixth month under mild stars, excluding the mixed, sharp, or fierce ones. ||10||
> When benefics are in the center houses <note>1, 4, 7, 10</note> or trines <note>1, 5, 9</note> and the 3rd and 2nd houses, and malefic lords of planets are in the 3rd, 6th, and 11th. The rising sign and its ninth-division <note>Navamsha</note> should not be Gemini or a neuter sign. The 12th, 1st, and 5th houses should be free of malefic planets. Even in the dark fortnight, provided it is not the last third of the month, and the Moon is white <note>waxing</note> and the Lord of the Month is strong. The Quickening <note>Pumsavana</note> should occur in the second or third month, following the same rules as the Parting of the Hair, or performed together with it.

### (Alternative Opinions on Constellations)

> Pushya, Hasta, Mula, Shravana, Punarvasu, and Mrigashira are the "male" stars. Additionally, Ashwini, Purva Bhadrapada, and Anuradha were also mentioned by the sage Vashistha and others. ||13||

### (Worship of Madhava)
<note>Madhava is a name for Vishnu; this refers to a specific devotional rite during pregnancy</note>

> When the strength of the stars and the Moon is favorable for the man and the pregnant woman, the worship of Madhava is performed in the eighth month. This should be done under the stars of Brahma <note>Rohini</note>, Vishnu <note>Shravana</note>, or Jupiter <note>Pushya</note>, in an auspicious rising sign that has a pure eighth house.

->( The Lords of the Months and Good or Evil Results Based on Their Strength )<-

The lords of the months <note>referring to the ten months of pregnancy in traditional reckoning</note> are Venus, Mars, Jupiter, the Sun, the Moon, Saturn, Mercury, the Ascendant lord, and the Moon again; the Sun is also included among these. From the strength of these planets, the nourishment of the fetus is determined; if these are weak, the fetus suffers distress. 15

->( If the Parting of the Hair Ritual is Not Performed in the First Pregnancy, )<-
->( It Should Be Done in the Second or on the Day of Naming )<-

If a beloved wife gives birth without having performed the *Simantonnayana* <note>The "Parting of the Hair" ritual, a baby shower-like rite to ensure the mental health of the mother and child</note> ritual, it should be performed during a subsequent pregnancy. Alternatively, it may be performed on the day of the naming ceremony while holding the infant in her lap. 16

->( The Birth Rite )<-

The *Jatakarma* <note>Birth ritual involving the father welcoming the newborn</note> should be performed at the birth of the child, even at night or during the period of birth-impurity. Having bathed in cold water with his clothes on, in a river or similar body of water while facing North, the father performs the rite before the umbilical cord is cut. If it is not performed then, it should be done after the period of birth-impurity ends, on an auspicious day—excluding the *Parva* <note>Full/New Moon</note> and *Rikta* <note>4th, 9th, 14th lunar days</note> days—under the Friendly, Swift, Movable, or Fixed constellations. 17

->( On Breastfeeding/Feeding )<-

Avoiding the *Rikta* days, Tuesday, and the astrological yogas of *Vaidhriti*, *Vyatipata*, and *Vishti*, and choosing Swift, Friendly, or Fixed constellations, feeding is best after five months. For the healthy eruption of teeth, it should not be done earlier. 18 <margin>1. One should not be confused that this refers to the mother's breastfeeding. Rather, it appears to refer to the drinking of cow's milk or similar; otherwise, the child's survival would not be possible for that long. Furthermore, since the auspicious time for drinking milk is mentioned again in Verse 25, this verse appears to be an interpolation <note>a later addition not in the original text</note>.</margin>

->( The Ritual Bath of the New Mother )<-

Avoiding the 6th and 8th lunar days, the *Rikta* days, Sunday, and the constellations of *Mula*, *Mishra*, *Shravana*, *Magha*, the three *Ardras* <note>Ardra, Punarvasu, Pushya</note>, *Bharani*, and *Chitra*, as well as Wednesday and Saturday; the ritual bath of the mother after childbirth should be performed under Fixed, *Pushan* (Revati), *Vata* (Svati), *Arka* (Hasta), *Ashwini*, *Indu* (Mrigashira), or *Mitra* (Anuradha) constellations. 19 <margin>2. By the word "Ravi" (Sun), the 12th lunar day should be understood.</margin>

->( The Naming Ceremony )<-

The naming ceremony should be performed by the father on the twelfth or eleventh day, at the end of the month, or even on the eighteenth day.

<meta>This page continues the discussion of childhood rites of passage (Samskaras) and the specific astrological timings required for their performance.</meta>

Page 35

For the protection of the child <note>original: "bhūpālakṣveḍape"</note>, the ceremony should be performed in the twentieth month. If that time passes, it should be done on the lunar mansion <term>Nakshatra: one of the 27 sectors of the sky the moon passes through</term> or the day of the week mentioned in the birth horoscope. 20

When the appointed time has passed, one must examine the strength of the Moon and the stars for both the father and the son to find an auspicious day. 21

This should not be performed on a Tuesday, on "empty" lunar days <term>Rikta tithis: the 4th, 9th, and 14th days of a lunar fortnight, considered unlucky for new ventures</term>, or on a Saturday. It should also be avoided after midday, during an unlucky planetary alignment, or when the Moon is in a "fickle" sign. It is forbidden when the <term>Karana</term> is "Kinstughna," or when a planet is in the eighth or twelfth house <note>original: "riḥphastha"</note> from the rising sign. In these cases, the three-syllable name <gloss>1</gloss> should not be given.

### ( Placing the Child in the Cradle )

On a day governed by soft, light, or fixed stars, and on an auspicious day of the week, specifically on the 22nd, 12th, or 16th day after birth, the protector of the child should perform the cradle ceremony. From the sun-sign of birth, the 21st or 12th signs are considered auspicious. 23

### ( Worship of the Waters )

The mother should not perform the ritual worship of water <note>referring to the Jalapujanam or the first trip to the well after childbirth</note> during the months of Chaitra or Pausha, during an intercalary month, or when Venus or Jupiter are combust <term>combust: when a planet is too close to the sun to be seen, weakening its influence</term>. It is best performed under the stars Anuradha, Hasta, Pushya, Punarvasu, Rohini, or Shravana, in the middle of a month when the Sun is in a favorable position. 24

### ( Drinking Milk and the First Outing )

On the thirty-first day or in the second month, on the child's birth star, the first feeding of milk should occur using a conch shell. Then, the first outing <note>original: "nishkramanam"</note> should take place in the fourth month on a day prescribed for travel, or on the twelfth day after birth. 25

### ( Sitting on the Ground )

When Mars is astrologically purified, under light, gentle, friendly, fixed, or Indra-governed stars, and on a lunar day that is not "empty," one should worship the Earth and the Divine Boar <note>Varaha, the incarnation of Vishnu who lifted the Earth from the depths</note>. The child should be seated upon the ground in the fifth month. 26

### ( Piercing the Ears )

The ears should be pierced in the months of Phalguna, Pausha, Kartika, or Chaitra, during the first fortnight on an auspicious day. This should avoid "empty" days and festival days, and should occur in an odd-numbered year for both boys and girls.

---

<note>1. Three-syllable: referring to the three-syllable name given to the child.</note>

<page-num>36</page-num>

In the constellations of Dhanishta, Shatabhisha, Anuradha, Mrigashira, Revati, Ashwini, Punarvasu, and the three "Ushas" <note>The "Ushas" refer to the three later constellations: Uttara Phalguni, Uttara Ashadha, and Uttara Bhadrapada</note>, when the planets are in auspicious divisions of the zodiac, and during the daytime—avoiding the birth star, the birth month, and the lunar day of omission <note>term: Avama; a lunar day that is skipped in the calendar</note>—one should proceed. 27

In the tenth, sixth, eighth, or seventh month, or in the first month, if the child is born on the 12th or 16th day; with the eighth, sixth, and twelfth houses free of planets, and with auspicious planets in other houses or in the third, eleventh, and sixth positions. 28

### ( Now, the Consumption of Betel Leaf )

In the second and a half month, under the stars of Shravana, Jyeshtha, Hasta, Revati, Punarvasu, Mrigashira, Pushya, or Rohini; avoiding the days of Mars and Saturn, the first eating of betel leaf <note>term: Tambula-bhakshana; a minor ceremony where the child is introduced to the auspicious betel leaf</note> is recommended, perhaps along with the first meal. 29

### ( Now, the First Feeding of Solid Food )

The first feeding of grain <note>term: Annaprashana; the sacred rite of a child's first solid food</note> should occur in the sixth or eighth month for boys, and in an odd-numbered month (like the fifth or seventh) for girls. This should avoid the "Empty" lunar days <note>Nanda and Rikta tithis</note>, Sundays, and the eighth lunar day. It should be done on the days of Venus, Mercury, or Jupiter, when the Sun is in a favorable sign or division. 30

Under the "Soft," "Fixed," "Swift," or "Mobile" groups of stars, during the waxing moon or when the moon is not excessively dark, and in the ascendant signs of Aries, Scorpio, or Pisces. The benefics should be in the center <note>term: Kendra; the 1st, 4th, 7th, and 10th houses</note> or trines, and in the third, second, eleventh, or tenth houses. 31

Malefic planets should be in the third, sixth, or eleventh houses, and the tenth house must be pure. The Moon is not praised in the twelfth, eighth, or sixth houses. The Moon is auspicious from the eighth day of the bright fortnight to the eighth day of the dark fortnight <note>original: "Arjuna"; a synonym for the bright half of the month</note>.

### ( Results of the First Feeding Based on Planetary Positions )

If the Sun and other planets are in the centers, trines, or the eighth and twelfth houses at the time of feeding, the child will respectively be: a leper, a performer of sacrifices, one of bilious temperament, a wise person, one who lives a long life, one who enjoys good fortune, or one who suffers from diseases.

<gloss>Positions: 1-4-7-10-5-9-8-12</gloss>
If the Sun and others are in the centers, trines, or the eighth and twelfth houses, and if the Moon is waning, the child may become one who lives on alms <note>a beggar or a wandering monk</note>. 33

<page-num>37</page-num>

### ( Now, the Completion of the Year )

Having worshipped Markandeya <note>The sage Markandeya is associated with longevity and is traditionally invoked on birthdays</note> and others with offerings of jaggery, one should also worship the goddess Shashti <note>The protector of children</note> and tie white mustard and Durva grass to the child's hand. If the birthday falls on a Tuesday or Saturday, or if the birth star is unfavorable, there will be obstacles for the child during that year.

### ( On Unavoidable Time Constraints from Conception to First Feeding )

In the rituals starting from Conception to the First Solid Feeding <term>Annaprashana: the ritual of a child's first intake of solid food</term>, one should not avoid the following even if they are usually considered unfavorable: intercalary months <note>extra months added to the lunar calendar</note>, the period when Vishnu is sleeping, the Sun's southern transit, or the periods when Venus and Jupiter are in their "infancy," "old age," or "combust" states <note>astrological phases where planets are too close to the sun to be effective</note>.

### ( Days of Infancy and Old Age of Planets, and Differing Opinions )

After Venus rises in the west or east, its "infancy" lasts for ten days and three days respectively. Its "old age" is said to last five days or a fortnight. For Jupiter, they say these periods last a fortnight. 36
Some say ten days, others say two; some say seven days, and for the "combust" state, others say three days for both.

### ( Special Considerations for the Moon )

The infancy, combustion, and old age of the Moon last for half a day, two days, and three days respectively. These are considered unfavorable, though some authorities do not count the infancy of the Moon as a defect. 37

### ( Other Special Cases: Comets and Eclipses )

These [unfavorable times] should be avoided in the Tonsure ceremony <term>Chuda: the first ritual haircut</term>, Upanayana <term>Vrata: the sacred thread ceremony</term>, Consecration of deities, and Marriage. One should also avoid seven days following a natural portent or the rising of a comet, or sixteen days in the case of a comet. 38
After a planetary setting, seven days—

### ( Jupiter in Leo: Prohibitions and Exceptions )

->Even when Jupiter is in Leo<-

Jupiter is to be avoided when it is in the first quarter of the sign Leo. This applies to the region between the Gautami and Godavari rivers, even if it occurs in months other than Vaishakha <note>April-May</note>. 39

### ( Conjunction of Jupiter and Sun, and Jupiter in Debilitation )

When Jupiter and the Sun are in conjunction, auspicious ceremonies do not bring good results; the same applies when Jupiter is in its sign of debilitation <note>Capricorn</note>. However, in the regions of Magadha, Konkan, Nirvriti, and Gauda <note>Ancient regions in modern Bihar, the West Coast, and Bengal</note>, it is considered auspicious once Jupiter has moved past its point of maximum debilitation. 40

### (Prohibition of Marriage and Other Rites During Jupiter’s Retrograde or Accelerated Motion and the Thirteen-Day Fortnight)

When Jupiter is in retrograde <note>moving backward from our perspective on Earth</note> or in accelerated motion <note>moving faster than its average speed through the zodiac</note>, no auspicious ceremony should be performed. However, after twenty-eight days <note>original: "manu-dvaya" or 28</note> have passed, it becomes favorable again. Likewise, during a lunar fortnight that lasts only thirteen days, ceremonies such as marriage or starting a journey will not bring good fortune. || 41 ||

### (Now, the Tonsure Ceremony)

The Tonsure <term>Chudakaranam: the first ritual cutting of a child's hair</term> should be performed in the second, third, or fifth year of age. It should not be done if the mother has been pregnant for more than five months. It may also be performed along with the Investiture of the Sacred Thread <term>Maunji: the Upanayana initiation</term>. After the child is five months old, the rite should be performed within the five months starting from the month of Magha <note>roughly January/February</note>, excluding the month of Chaitra <note>roughly March/April</note>. || 42 ||

### (Prohibition of Tonsure and Vows While the Mother is Menstruating or in Postpartum Recovery)

The taking of vows, the Tonsure, or the taking of a hand in marriage <note>marriage ceremony</note> should not be performed if the mother is currently menstruating or in the period of postpartum impurity. One must wait for the mother’s purification. If one cannot wait for a different astrological rising sign <note>Lagna</note>, the rite must only be done after performing pacification rituals <term>Shanti: rites to neutralize ill effects</term>.

### (Auspicious Dates and Stars for the Tonsure)

The ceremony should avoid the "Empty" lunar days <note>the 4th, 9th, and 14th days of the lunar month</note>, as well as the 6th, 12th, 15th (Full Moon), and 30th (New Moon). It should avoid Sundays and festival days. It should be done during the day when Mercury, Venus, the Moon, or Jupiter are rising. Auspicious stars <term>Nakshatras</term> include the "Three Hands" <note>Hasta, Citra, Svati</note>, the "Three Vishnus" <note>Shravana, Dhanishta, Shatabhisha</note>, Jyeshtha, Mrigashira, Revati, Ashvini, Punarvasu, and Pushya. || 44 ||

Malefic planets are best situated in the 3rd, 6th, or 11th houses. Benefic planets are desired in the center houses <note>1st, 4th, 7th, 10th</note>, the trines <note>5th, 9th</note>, or the 2nd, 3rd, and 12th houses. Venus is not auspicious in the 7th house, nor is the Moon in the 8th or 12th. The birth star is considered mediocre.

### (Special Rules for Shaving)

Shaving <term>Kshauram</term> is auspicious when performed on the days and under the stars mentioned for the Tonsure. It is also permitted during a pilgrimage to the Ganges, at a sacrifice, or during a marriage. In the event of the death of parents, or by the command of a priest or a king, shaving may be done at any time. A king should regularly have this done on the fifth day.

<page-num>39</page-num>

When the Moon is in a constellation suitable for shaving <note>original: "kshaurarkshe"; refers to specific lunar mansions like Pushya, Punarvasu, etc.</note> or during its rising, one should avoid unfavorable stars. The constellations Pitrya (Magha), Maitra (Anuradha), the three Uttaras, and Brahmya (Rohini) are considered wise choices. Shaving after a previous shaving is acceptable, except on the 9th lunar day (Nanda). If the stars are weak, the strength of the weekday may provide benefit.
One should not recommend shaving for a man in the morning or evening twilights, at night, when he is facing battle, or when he is already highly decorated. || 48 ||
Those who have just eaten, anointed themselves with oil, bathed, or are about to depart on a journey; those in the midst of a sacrifice, or the very young; as well as masters, women, royal servants, and ascetics—these should not undergo shaving unless it is absolutely necessary. || 49 ||

### ( Now, the Beginning of Learning the Alphabet )

The commencement of writing <term>Akshara-arambha: the ceremony of first writing letters</term> is auspicious during the Northern Transit of the sun <note>Uttarayana</note>, excluding the sign of Aquarius, in the fifth year of age. After worshiping Ganesha, Saraswati (the Goddess of Speech), Vishnu, and Lakshmi, the ceremony should take place under the three Hasta stars, Revati, Punarvasu, Pushya, or Anuradha.
It should occur when Vishnu or Shiva are the presiding deities, in an Ascendant free from malefic planets or fast-moving influences, on a good weekday and an auspicious lunar day, ensuring the "Death-point" <note>Mrityu-dosha</note> is purified. The "House of Intelligence" (5th house) should be occupied or aspected by a benefic planet or its own strong lord.

### ( Now, the Beginning of Formal Education )

The study of sciences <term>Vidya-arambha: formal schooling or Vedic study</term> should occur under constellations excluding Magha, Krittika, Vishakha, Chitra, and Bharani. It is best on Sundays, Wednesdays, Thursdays, or Fridays. The 2nd, 3rd, 5th, 6th, 10th, or 13th lunar days are preferred. Some authorities suggest the fixed and final stars are also acceptable.

### ( Now, the Thread Ceremony )

The sacred thread ceremony <term>Maunji-bandha or Upanayana</term> is best in the 8th year from conception or birth for Brahmins, and in the 11th or 12th year for others. The 5th, 6th, and 8th years are considered "desirable" <note>Kamya</note> or secondary; the primary time extends up to twice the initial age limit.

### ( The Strength of Jupiter )

If the strength of Jupiter <note>Guru-bala</note> is lacking at the prescribed time, Jupiter may attain favorability through pacification rituals <note>Shanti</note>. Even in an unprescribed year, if Jupiter is strong, the rite may proceed; however, the vow should not be taken if the Lord of one's Vedic Branch <note>Shakha-adhipati</note> is devoid of strength. || 54 ||
If Jupiter is not auspicious during the primary time, the ceremony for men or women <note>referring to various initiations</note> may take place in the months of Chaitra or when the sun is in Pisces.

# Page 40

The planet is excellent when situated in the fifth, second, ninth, seventh, or eleventh houses from the birth sign <note>Rashi</note>; it is not auspicious in the eighth, fourth, or twelfth houses, and in other positions, it is considered middling. || 55 ||

In a middling position, the ritual worship <note>Puja</note> is performed; if in an inauspicious position, that worship should be doubled. If the planet is in its own sign, its sign of exaltation, its own Navamsha <note>a one-ninth division of a sign</note>, its Vargottama <note>the best division</note>, or a friendly sign, it is excellent even if the timing has passed. However, if it is in its sign of debilitation or an enemy's sign, even a generally favorable planet becomes unfavorable. || 56 ||

### (Purity of Month, Lunar Day, and Weekday)

In the five months beginning with Magha <note>roughly January to June</note>, the ceremony is auspicious during the first three parts of the dark fortnight or during the light fortnight. The ceremony should take place on the 2nd, 3rd, 5th, 6th, 10th, 12th, 11th, or 13th lunar days <note>Tithis</note>, excluding Tuesdays and Saturdays. || 57 ||

### (Purity of Asterisms according to Veda)

For those of the Rigveda, the ceremony is favorable in Purva Phalguni, Hasta, the three following stars, Mrigashira, and Ardra. For the Yajurveda, it is favorable in Anuradha, Punarvasu, the fixed stars <note>Rohini and the three Uttaras</note>, Mrigashira, Hasta, Revati, and Pushya. For the Samaveda, the investiture of the girdle <note>Maunji</note> should occur in Shravana, Dhanishta, Ardra, Hasta, Ashwini, or the three Uttara stars, or Jyeshtha. For the remaining Vedas <note>Atharvaveda</note>, it should be in Dhanishta, the light/short stars, Mrigashira, Revati, Punarvasu, or Anuradha. || 58 ||

### (General Asterisms)

The stars Magha, Krittika, Vishakha, and Bharani are not desired; others consider Shatabhisha to be unfavorable as well.

### (Purity of the Ascendant)

The ceremony should not be performed in the afternoon. Malefic planets are good in the 3rd, 6th, and 11th houses; benefic planets are good except in the 6th, 12th, and 8th houses. The lords of the Ascendant <note>Lagna</note>, of Jupiter, and of the Moon are censured if they fall in the 6th or 8th houses; Venus and the Moon are also censured in the 12th house. Malefics in the 1st, 8th, or 5th houses are avoided, though some consider the Sun favorable in Cancer, Taurus, or the Moon's sign. || 60 ||

Planets are especially excellent in their own houses, exaltations, or in the angles <note>Kendras</note> and trines <note>Trikonas</note>; the same applies to the Lord of the Veda branch <note>Shakhesha</note>. The days and strengths of the Lord of the branch are praised; the vow is successful when the Sun is strong. If the Lord of the branch, or Venus, or Jupiter is debilitated, defeated in planetary war, in an enemy's house, or combust, the initiate does not truly become a "Vrati" <note>one who holds the vow</note>. The same applies to the Lord of the Navamsha. However, if the Ascendant is in the division of Mercury, the initiate becomes a performer of sacrifices <note>Yajva</note>. || 62 ||

If Jupiter is located in the first, fifth, or ninth house <note>The Trikona or trine houses</note>, or if Venus is in a favorable division of the zodiac while the Moon is present, the boy will become a learned scholar. If Jupiter, Venus, and the Moon are associated with malefic planets, his character will be extremely cruel. If the Moon is in its own sign but lacks the strength of the Sun's rays <note>original: "Aditya-karna-pada-hine," referring to specific lunar phases or degrees</note>, he will be deeply unhappy.

->(Prohibitions regarding the birth month and related factors)<-

For the first-born child of Brahmins, the birth month and birth star are auspicious for the ceremony; however, for a king <note>the Kshatriya caste</note>, the subsequent months are preferred.

->(Now, the days when study is prohibited)<-

The strength of Jupiter is not required here—

->( The Binding of the Knife )<-
<note>Churikābandhana: A ritual for the Kshatriya (warrior) and Shudra classes where a knife or dagger is formally bound to the waist, often serving as a rite of passage or a substitute for the sacred thread ceremony.</note>

For those born of the kingly caste or the lowest caste,
The binding of the knife occurs before the "grasping of the hand" <note>marriage</note>. || 68 ||
In the months beginning with Chaitra <note>March/April</note>, excluding the month of the sacred thread ceremony, and even on a Tuesday;
When Mars has risen, the binding of the knife for a girl <note>or to win a maiden</note> leads to the attainment of auspiciousness. || 69 ||
Having thoroughly examined many texts and properly composed by Vitthala Dikshita,
In this work named the *Muhurtakalpadruma* <note>The Desire-Yielding Tree of Electional Astrology</note>, the flower <note>chapter</note> titled "Sacraments" is now complete. || 70 ||

---

->( The Flower of Marriage )<-
->( The Purpose )<-

Pleasure, enjoyment, and religious duties are dependent upon a wife;
If she is endowed with good character, then those goals are truly attained.
That character is obtained through an auspicious astrological moment <term>Lagna: the rising sign or specific time</term>; therefore,
I shall describe the marriage moment correctly according to the vision of the Shastras <note>sacred sciences</note>. || 1 ||

->( Inquiry into the Marriage Moment )<-

The patron, with devotion and by giving wealth and betel leaves,
Should worship the astrologer properly and then inquire.
First, having joyfully announced the birth stars <term>Nakshatras</term>
Or the names of the bride and groom. || 2 ||
If the moment of inquiry falls in Taurus, Libra, or Cancer, or if the Moon and Venus are situated there;
Or if those two signs are aspected by planets in even signs or divisions, the man will successfully obtain a maiden.
If the Moon is in the 5th, 10th, 11th, 7th, or 3rd house from the rising sign, and its body is aspected by Jupiter;
It is for the gain of a maiden. But if the Moon is set <note>combust</note> or in its sign of debilitation, then there is no obtaining of a maiden.

<page-num>43</page-num>

If the ascendant <note>the zodiac sign rising on the eastern horizon at birth</note> or its divisional portions are occupied or aspected by male planets, it bestows a suitable groom upon the maiden. || 5 ||

If the Moon is in the ascendant and Mars is in the seventh house <note>the house of marriage</note>; or if the Moon is in the eighth or sixth house from the ascendant and is strong; or if a malefic planet is in the ascendant and Mars is in the seventh house: in these three planetary combinations, a woman attains widowhood. || 6 ||

If a malefic planet is in the fifth house from the ascendant, in its sign of debilitation, or aspected by an enemy, the woman will suffer the loss of her children. If the Moon is waning <note>original: "krishna-pakshe"</note>, visible, aspected by malefics, and placed in the sixth or eighth house, it causes physical affliction in marriage. || 7 ||

### ( Auspicious and Inauspicious Omens )

If a woman whose husband is alive <note>original: "suvasini," a sign of good fortune</note> is present, or if there is the sound of auspicious musical instruments, or the hearing of sacred words and news of pleasant things, then the marriage will be prosperous. However, if ill-omened sights like crows and such are seen, it is not considered auspicious. || 8 ||

### ( Now, the Consideration of Horoscope Matching for the Bride and Groom )

The Class <term>Varna: the four-fold classification of signs</term>, the Attraction <term>Vashya: the natural control one sign has over another</term>, the Birth Star <term>Tara</term>, the Species <term>Yoni</term>, the Friendship between the planetary lords, the Temperament <term>Gana: categorized as Divine, Human, or Demonic</term>, the Sign-Relative position <term>Bhakuta</term>, and the Pulse <term>Nadi</term>—these factors are progressively more powerful in granting points for compatibility. || 9 ||

### ( Consideration of Class )

->The Brahmin and other classes are determined from the signs starting with Pisces. The bride's class should not be<-
->higher than the groom's.<-

### ( Consideration of Attraction )

->Humans and water-dwelling creatures are the "food" <note>meaning they are susceptible to being influenced</note>.<-
->Excluding the Lion, all are controllable;<-
->except for the Scorpion, others are under the control of the King of Beasts <note>the Lion/Leo</note>. || 10 ||<-
->The Lion is not a friend to the Scorpion. For a Human,<-
->the Water-dwellers and the Lion are not desired; otherwise, they are acceptable.<-

### ( Consideration of the Stars )

->When calculating the distance between the birth stars of the man and the woman,<-
->if the sum is divided by nine and the remainder is 3, 7, or 5, it is not auspicious. || 11 ||<-
<note>This refers to the 'Tara' calculation where specific remainders indicate 'Vipat' (misfortune), 'Pratyari' (opposition), and 'Naidhana' (death).</note>

### ( The Animal Origins of the Stars )

Horse, elephant, goat, two snakes, and dog; then goat, cat, wolf, rat, and cow;
Buffalo, tiger <gloss>1</gloss>, buffalo <gloss>2</gloss>, lion <gloss>3</gloss>, two deer, monkey, and mongoose. || 12 ||
Mongoose <gloss>3</gloss>, monkey, lion <gloss>3</gloss>, and horse are the animal origins <term>Yoni: the biological/instinctive category assigned to a birth star</term>, along with the lion, elephant, and horse.

### ( Hostility Between Animal Types )

Dog and deer; cat and rat; elephant and lion; mongoose and snake; goat and monkey—these are fierce enemies. || 13 ||
Also, the cow and tiger; the black cobra and the horse; and others known from common worldly knowledge.
One should make an effort to avoid these pairings between a king and a servant, or a woman and her beloved, if the best results are desired. || 14 ||

### ( Natural Friendships of Planets )

For the Sun: Jupiter, Mars, and the Moon are friends. For the Moon: the Sun and Mercury. For Mars: the Sun, Jupiter, and the Moon. For Mercury: Venus and the Sun. For Jupiter: Mars, the Sun, and the Moon. For Venus: Saturn and Mercury. For Saturn: Venus and Mercury.
The Sun and Moon are not friends with Mercury. Venus and Mercury are enemies of the Moon and Sun.
The Sun, Moon, and Mars are neutral to others. On both sides, those in the third position are desired. || 15 ||
<note>This verse outlines 'Nisarga Maitri', the inherent planetary relationships used to judge the harmony between two horoscopes.</note>

### ( Consideration of Class Friendships )

The stars Rohini, Bharani, Ardra, and the three pairs <gloss>3</gloss> belong to the Human class <term>Gana</term>. Mula, Ashlesha, Dhanishta, Shatabhisha, Jyeshtha, Vishakha, Magha, and Chitra belong to the Demon class. The remaining—Punarvasu, Mrigashira, Anuradha, Pushya, Hasta, Swati, Shravana, and Revati—belong to the Divine class. || 16 ||
Between two people of the same class, love flourishes. Between the Divine and Human classes, it is middling.
Between the Demon and Divine classes, there is strife. The sages say that a union between the Demon and Human classes leads to death. || 17 ||

# Page 45

If the groom belongs to the Rakshasa <note>Demon</note> group and the bride to the Human group, the marriage is auspicious if the compatibility of the reproductive organs <term>Yoni: an astrological classification of sexual compatibility based on animal types</term> and other factors is good. If the groom's birth star is near the bride's, it is considered unfavorable; if it is distant, it is acceptable. However, if there is friendship between the planetary lords and the stars are favorable, it is good. 18

### ( Consideration of the Six-Eight Relationship and Others )

Death is foretold in the "Death" relationship <note>the 6th and 8th house positions</note>, and fear of enemies or poverty is seen in the "Enemy" relationship. A lack of children occurs in the "Wealth-Loss" relationship. Childlessness also occurs in the "Child-Grief" relationship, while in other positions, wealth and progeny will flourish. 19

If there is friendship between the lords of the zodiac signs, or if they have the same lord, and if the pulse <term>Nadi</term> and stars are pure, the marriage is excellent. Even if the "Zodiac-Group" <term>Bhakuta</term> is unfavorable, if the lords of the signs or the lords of the divisional charts <term>Amsha</term> are friendly, the fault of the "Class" <term>Gana</term> is nullified. 20

Even if the lords of the Moon signs are not friendly, the marriage is auspicious if the lords of the divisional charts are friends. The great sages have declared that even if the "Zodiac-Group" is flawed, it is acceptable if the pulse, star, and mutual attraction are present. 21

When planetary friendship exists, the faults of the compatibility points are destroyed. It is certain that planetary enmity is likewise destroyed when the compatibility points are excellent. 22

If the stars are different but the zodiac sign is the same, or if there are two signs but the star is the same, or if the star is the same but the quarters <term>Pada</term> are different, then there is no fault of the pulse <term>Nadi-dosha</term> nor any fault of the class <term>Gana-dosha</term>.

### ( Gifts to be given for Pulse-Piercing and other faults )

If "Pulse-Piercing" <term>Nadi-vedha</term> occurs, some say the marriage should only be performed in cases of necessity after gifting gold and a cow. In the case of the "Two-Sun" fault, one should give copper; for the "Five-River" fault, silver; and for the "Six-Death" fault, the gift of a pair of cows is prescribed. 24

### ( Consideration of the Pulse-Piercing )

The stars Ashvini, Jyeshtha, Purvashadha, Ardra, Punarvasu, Uttaraphalguni, Shatabhisha, Uttarabhadrapada, and Kritika constitute the first pulse <note>Adi Nadi</note>. Bharani, Pushya, Purvaphalguni, Chitra, Vishakha, Purvabhadrapada, Anuradha, Shravana, and Dhanishtha constitute the middle pulse <note>Madhya Nadi</note>. Rohini, Mrigashira, Magha, Hasta, Swati, Mula, Uttarashadha, Abhijit, and Revati constitute the final pulse <note>Antya Nadi</note>. If the couple shares the first or last pulse, it is not auspicious; if they share the middle pulse, it causes death. 25

# Page 46

If the birth stars of the bride and groom are unknown, one should determine the compatibility based on the zodiac sign of their given names. If the birth star is known for only one of them, then factors like the strength of Jupiter <note>Guru-bala, a transit calculation used to determine auspicious timing</note> should be assessed, and the union should be judged by the name-sign of the other. 2

->( Specific rules regarding the knowledge of the birth stars of the bride and groom )<-

If a man has many names, the name by which he is most commonly known in his daily affairs should be used. If a name is a compound or complex word, the very first letter of that name should be used to determine the zodiac sign and the corresponding lunar mansion <term>Nakshatra: one of the 27 sectors of the sky used in Vedic astrology</term>. 2

->( Decision regarding a man having multiple names )<-

In matters concerning one's country, during illness, when entering a new village or house, in service, in war, in legal disputes, in gambling, and in giving gifts, the name-sign <note>Nama-rashi</note> should be used. However, for journeys and marriages, the birth-sign <note>Janma-rashi, based on the moon's position at birth</note> must be used. 2

->( Arrangement of the use of Name-sign versus Birth-sign )<-

->( Now, the consideration of the calculated points (Gunas) for the bride and groom )<-

If the caste <term>Varna: the first of the eight compatibility tests</term> is equal or if the groom is of a higher caste, one point is gained. In the attraction test <term>Vashya</term>, if the groom's sign dominates, half a point is given. If there is mutual attraction, one point is given. If there is friendship between the planetary lords of the signs, two points are awarded, but if there is natural enmity, there is a total loss of points. 30

->Regarding the auspiciousness of the stars for the groom and bride:<-
->Three points if both are auspicious; half of that (1.5) if one is good and the other is neutral.<-
->In cases of great enmity, neutral friendship, or specific mathematical combinations,<-
->the points are measured as 0, 1, 3, or 4 respectively. 31<-

In cases of enmity, neutral-enmity, or neutral-friendship, or if both are equal, or in cases of mutual friendship, or where both signs are ruled by the same planet, the points awarded shall be 0, 1, <gloss>3</gloss> 2, <gloss>3</gloss> 4, or 5 respectively. 32
If the temperaments <term>Gana: categorized as Deva (Divine), Manushya (Human), or Rakshasa (Demonic)</term> are the same, 6 points are given. If the groom is Human and the bride is Divine, 5 points are given; in the reverse case, 6 points are given.

<meta>This page continues the technical instructions for marriage compatibility and the preliminary rituals of selection, following the mathematical calculations of the Kuta system.</meta>

If one partner belongs to the divine class and the other to the demonic class, only one point is assigned <gloss>1</gloss> <note>original: "anyathādvauvatha duṣṭakūṭe" - some versions suggest zero points if the compatibility is particularly poor</note>; otherwise, two points are given. In the case of poor compatibility, if there is "Yoni" friendship <note>biological/instinctive compatibility between the symbolic animals of the birth stars</note>, the distance between the bride's and groom's stars is far, or if the "Vedha" <note>astrological piercing or opposition</note> is absent, then one point is granted. If the birth stars or their quarters are identical, it is considered auspicious. If the stars are different but within the same zodiac sign, six points are assigned. ॥३४॥

If the stars and signs are different, five points are assigned; otherwise, seven points are given. Regarding the "Nadi" <note>the pulse or internal temperament</note> division, if there is no conflict, eight points are given. If the total sum of these compatibility points is greater than eighteen <note>out of a possible 36</note>, it is declared to bring happiness to the woman and her husband. ॥ ३५ ॥

### ( On the Selection of the Bride )

Under the marriage stars <note>Rohini, Mrigashirsha, Magha, Uttara Phalguni, Hasta, Swati, Anuradha, Mula, Purva Ashadha, Uttara Ashadha, Shravana, Dhanishta, Shatabhisha, Uttara Bhadrapada, and Revati</note>, Shravana, Dhanishta, and the three "Purva" stars <note>Purva Phalguni, Purva Ashadha, Purva Bhadrapada</note>, along with Krittika, one should formally select the maiden at an auspicious time using ornaments, flowers, consecrated rice, beautiful garments, and fruits. ॥ ३६ ॥

### ( On the Selection of the Groom )

Under the three "Purva" stars, Krittika, or the fixed stars <note>Rohini, Uttara Phalguni, Uttara Ashadha, Uttara Bhadrapada</note>, a Brahmin or the brother of the bride <gloss>2</gloss> <note>original: "kanyāsahottha" - literally 'born with the girl'</note>, desiring a favorable outcome, should perform the selection of the groom on an auspicious day with music and honors, presenting garments and a sacred thread.

### ( Proper Years for Marriage )

Marriage is recommended in the third year from conception or birth; some say up to the fifth or tenth year. Even-numbered years are generally considered good for taking the hand in marriage. Some authorities suggest the twelfth year, while others suggest odd-numbered years for the groom. ॥ ३८ ॥

### ( Consideration of Jupiter, Sun, and Moon Strength )

The marriage of a maiden should take place when Jupiter is astrologically strong; for the groom, the Sun must be strong. When the Moon is strong, it benefits both. The people of the Gauda region <note>Northern/Eastern India</note> emphasize the Sun and Jupiter being strong for the southern residents.

### ( Proper Months for Marriage )

The five months beginning with Magha <note>roughly January to May</note>, excluding Chaitra, are suitable. Also, the month of Margashirsha is acceptable when the Sun is in a specific position, until the time the Lord wakes from sleep <note>referring to Vishnu's four-month slumber, Chaturmasya</note>. Marriage is performed when the Sun is in Aries, Scorpio, or Capricorn. For maidens, the months of Chaitra, Kartika, and Pausha are also permitted by some. ॥ ४० ॥

### ### ( Now, Various Scriptural Interpretations )

For a man, the ritual of marriage <note>original: "karapidanam" - literally 'pressing the hand'</note> should not be performed, nor should a woman undergo tonsure <note>ritual head-shaving</note> within the same year as a previous auspicious event in the family. If a major auspicious ceremony has occurred, a minor one should not follow it within that year. 42

Similarly, the marriage of two brothers or two sisters should not be performed simultaneously. Furthermore, a bride should not enter her new home at the same time as a daughter is departing for her husband's home, unless a full year has passed or they are separated by a great river or a mountain range. 43

### ### ( Prohibitions Regarding the First-Born Son and Daughter )

The marriage of a first-born son and a first-born daughter is not auspicious if performed during their birth month, on their birth date, or under their birth star. However, if only one of the pair is a first-born, the union is considered beneficial.
->The thirty days following birth constitute the birth month.<-
->Some say this entire period is forbidden for all auspicious acts.<-
->Others suggest that only the first ten days are forbidden, while the rest are acceptable.<-
->Still others claim the entire lunar month of birth must be avoided. 44<-

### ### ( Exceptions Regarding the Birth Month )

If the birth month falls in the opposite lunar fortnight <note>the dark vs. light half of the month</note>, or if the birth day/night distinction differs, or if the birth star <note>Nakshatra</note> falls in a different half of the month, then ancient authorities state that the marriage may proceed auspiciously. 45

### ### ( Prohibitions Regarding the Month of Jyeshtha )

In the month of Jyeshtha <note>typically May-June</note>, the marriage of a first-born <term>Jyeshtha: meaning both the month and the eldest child</term> son and a first-born daughter should never be performed. If only one of them is the eldest, the marriage may occur. Some authorities state this fault only applies when the Sun is in the constellation of Rohini <note>original: "analasthe" - referring to the heat of the sun in specific positions</note>.

### ### ( Further Various Scriptural Interpretations )

One should not give two daughters in marriage to two brothers at the same time. Additionally, ancestral rites <note>Shraddha</note> should not be performed in a house where an auspicious ceremony is being held. Two major auspicious events should not be celebrated together without the passing of a year. 47

If someone in the family of the groom or the bride dies after the engagement ceremony <note>Vagdana - the 'giving of the word'</note>, the marriage is not desired. It may only proceed after a month has passed and after performing a propitiatory ritual <note>Shantika</note> dedicated to Lord Ganesha <note>Vainayaka</note>.

--- Page 67 ---
<meta>This page continues the discussion of astrological timing and compatibility, specifically focusing on familial prohibitions, birth stars, and the technical divisions of the zodiac signs.</meta>

<page-num>49</page-num>

A younger brother should not perform his own marriage while his elder uterine brother remains unmarried. However, there is no fault in doing so if the elder brother is impotent, has renounced the world <note>entered the Sannyasa stage of life</note>, is living in a far-off land, or suffers from a disability of the senses. 49

### (The Results of Two Children Born in Mula and Other Constellations)

A son or daughter born in the constellations of *Mula* or *Ashlesha* <note>original: "ahi," referring to the serpent deity of Ashlesha</note> destroys the father-in-law or mother-in-law respectively. If born in the first or last quarters of these stars, they do not cause this harm. A woman born in *Jyeshtha* destroys her husband's elder brother, and one born in *Vishakha* destroys the husband's younger brother, though this does not apply if born in the sign of Libra <note>original: "juke"</note>. 50

### (The Combination for a Poison-Maiden)

If a girl is born on a Saturday, Tuesday, or Sunday, falling on the constellations of *Shatabhisha*, *Ashlesha*, or *Krittika*, and on the lunar days <term>Tithis</term> of the *Bhadra* group <note>the 2nd, 7th, or 12th days of the lunar fortnight</note>, she is a "Poison-Maiden" <term>Visha-kanya</term>. A man should avoid her, for union with her leads to many afflictions. 51

### (Now, the Lords of the Zodiac Signs)

The lords of the signs <note>the twelve signs of the zodiac</note> are, in order: Mars, Venus, Mercury, the Moon,
->the Sun, Mercury, Venus, Mars, Jupiter,<-
Saturn, Saturn, and Jupiter.

### (The Lords of the Ninth-Divisions)

->The lords of the Ninth-Divisions <term>Navamsha</term><-
begin from Aries, Capricorn, Libra, and Cancer respectively <note>for the four groups of signs: Fire, Earth, Air, and Water</note>. 52

### (Calculating the Third, Twelfth, and Half-Divisions)

The Third-Divisions <term>Drekkana</term> are ruled by the lords of the 1st, 5th, and 9th signs from the current sign. The Twelfth-Divisions <term>Dvadashamsha</term> begin from the sign itself. Regarding the Half-Divisions <term>Hora</term>:
In an odd-numbered sign, the first half belongs to the Sun and the second to the Moon; in an even-numbered sign, they belong to the Moon and the Sun respectively. 53

### (Calculating the Thirty-Degree Divisions)

The Thirty-Degree Divisions <term>Trimshamsha</term> are measured in units of five, five, eight, seven, and five degrees: 5 | 5 | 8 | 7 | 5.
These belong to Mars, Saturn, Jupiter, Mercury, and Venus.
This order applies to odd signs; in even signs, the order is reversed.

### ( The Purpose of the Sixfold Divisions )

The House <note>Rashi</note>, its half <note>Hora</note>, the third part <note>Drekkana</note>, and the ninth part <note>Navamsha</note>, || 54 ||
along with the twelfth part <note>Dwadasamsha</note> and the thirtieth part <note>Trishamsha</note>, constitute the **Sixfold Divisions** <note>original: "Shadvarga"</note>.
If these six divisions are occupied by or associated with "gentle" planets <note>benefics like Jupiter or Venus</note>, the result is auspicious; otherwise, it is not good.
When three or more of these divisions are favorable, the Ascendant <note>Lagna</note> is considered desirable, provided it does not fall on a "void" lunar day <note>Rikta Tithi</note> or the New Moon. || 55 ||

### ( Constellations for Marriage )

Marriage brings auspicious results when performed under the following constellations <note>Nakshatras</note>:
Swati, Anuradha, the "Fixed" group <note>Rohini, Uttara Phalguni, Uttara Ashadha, and Uttara Bhadrapada</note>, Mula, Magha,
Hasta, Mrigashira, and Revati.

### ( Rejection of Pierced Constellations )

If a constellation is "pierced" <note>original: "viddha"; an astrological interference or affliction</note> by a benefic planet, one should only avoid the specific quarter <note>charana</note> of the constellation affected. However, if it is pierced by a "cruel" planet <note>malefics like Mars or Saturn</note>, the entire constellation must be abandoned. || 56 ||

### ( The Five-Spoke Obstruction )

The last quarter of Uttarashadha, the four minutes <note>nadis</note> of Shravana, and the constellation Abhijit—if a planet is situated here, Rohini is "pierced" <note>obstructed</note>.
Bharani obstructs Anuradha; Shravana obstructs Magha;
Hasta obstructs Uttara Bhadrapada; and Mrigashira obstructs Uttarashadha. || 57 ||
Mula obstructs Punarvasu; Shatabhisha obstructs Swati;
and Aryaman <note>Uttara Phalguni</note> obstructs Revati.
This "piercing" <note>Vedha</note> is particularly relevant for marriage <note>original: "karapidana"; literally 'taking the hand'</note> when it occurs between the first and last quarters, or the second and third quarters of the respective stars. || 58 ||

### ( The Seven-Spoke Obstruction )

<note>This section describes a more complex grid for determining planetary interference.</note>
Dhanishta and Ashlesha; Bharani and Magha; Swati and Shatabhisha; Ashwini and Purva Phalguni; Punarvasu and Mula;
Chitra and Purva Bhadrapada; Mrigashira and Uttarashadha; Rohini and Abhijit; Shravana and Krittika. || 59 ||

<summary>This page details the Shadvarga (sixfold planetary divisions) and provides specific rules for selecting marriage constellations, including the Pancha-shalaka and Sapta-shalaka systems for identifying astrological obstructions.</summary>
<keywords>Shadvarga, Marriage Astrology, Nakshatras, Vedha, Pancha-shalaka, Sapta-shalaka, Muhurta, Hindu Wedding Timing</keywords>

--- Page 69 ---
<meta>This page continues the technical discussion of astrological timing (Muhurta), specifically focusing on planetary obstructions and inauspicious periods for marriage and other sacred rites.</meta>

In the lunar mansions of Revati, Aryaman (Uttara Phalguni), Ashlesha, Anuradha, Punarvasu, Jyeshtha, Shravana, and Shatabhisha, one should avoid marriage if there is a piercing obstruction <note>original: "vedha"; a technical term for a planetary alignment that "pierces" or spoils the efficacy of a lunar mansion</note>. Some authorities state that this obstruction should be avoided in all seven lines of the astrological chart during a wedding.

->( Remedy for the Obstruction Fault )<-

If the Moon is aspected by <note>viewed by</note> benefic planets, or if a benefic planet is situated in the first house (ascendant) during a favorable planetary hour <note>term: "hora" (one-twenty-fourth of a day)</note>, or if the Lord of the Ascendant is in the eleventh house of gains, the fault of the obstruction—even if it is the dreaded eighth-house fault—flees far, far away. 61

->( Other Faults Described )<-

One should avoid the lunar mansion occupied by the Sun and the fourteen subsequent mansions for six months if they coincide with one's birth sign or birth moon. One should also avoid mansions affected by portents <note>natural calamities or omens</note> or those occupied by malefic planets. One must especially avoid the mansion currently occupied by the Moon if it is joined by a malefic planet. 62

In the junction of lunar mansions <note>term: "sandhi"</note>, one should avoid an uneven number of days. If two eclipses occur within a single fortnight, the first affected mansion is purified after one fortnight, but the second mansion remains impure for six months. In the case of a partial eclipse, the period of avoidance is three months; for a total eclipse, the mansion should be avoided for a full month. Following this, for each quarter-increase of the eclipse <gloss>3</gloss>, one should avoid it for four, <gloss>6</gloss> six, or eight times the duration, starting from the half-mansion. 64

During the equinoxes and solstices, one should avoid the days immediately preceding and following the transition. For other planetary transits <note>term: "sankranti"</note>, a wise person should avoid sixteen *ghatikas* <note>a unit of 24 minutes; 16 ghatikas equals 6.4 hours</note> on both sides of the event for auspicious works.

->( The Three Types of Junction Points )<-

The final half-hour <note>term: "nadi"</note> of the signs Pisces, Scorpio, and Cancer, and likewise the first half-hour of Aries, Leo, and Sagittarius; the first half-hour of the "Full" lunar dates <note>the 5th, 10th, and 15th days</note> and the "Joyous" dates <note>the 1st, 6th, and 11th days</note>; and the final hours of the mansions Jyeshtha, Revati, and Ashlesha—these are the junction points <note>term: "gandanta"</note>. 66
The first two hours of Mula, Ashwini, and Magha are also considered junction points; these are never praised for auspicious ceremonies.

->( Declination Equality )<-

When the Sun and Moon have equal declination <note>term: "kranti-samya"</note> and their combined longitude is either six or twelve signs, it is considered unfavorable. 67

--- Page 70 ---
<meta>This page continues the technical discussion of astrological "doshas" (afflictions) and their remedies, following the standard structure of a Jyotisha (astrological) manual. It focuses on planetary configurations that can spoil an auspicious moment.</meta>

->( The Scissor Configuration )<-

If two malefic planets <note>planets like Mars or Saturn that generally bring hardship</note>, one moving forward and one in retrograde, are situated in the 12th and 2nd houses from a specific point, it forms a "Scissor" <term>Kartari: a configuration where a house or planet is 'cut' between two others</term> which is not auspicious. However, if those two planets are in an enemy's sign, their sign of debilitation, or are combust <note>hidden by the sun's glare</note>, the Scissor effect is neutralized. (68)

->( The Kick )<-

A planet "kicks" <term>Latta: a planetary affliction where a planet strikes a specific star ahead or behind it</term> the stars located at the 12th, 3rd, 6th, and 27th positions from itself. Mars specifically strikes the 8th star ahead. The Sun strikes the 12th star ahead, while the full Moon strikes the 7th star behind. (69)

->( The Falling Stars )<-

->The specific stars associated with the "Falling" <term>Pata: a mathematical point where the paths of the sun and moon intersect</term> yoga-junctions should be avoided in all auspicious works.<-

->( Remedies for These Afflictions )<-

->If the Ascendant <note>the zodiac sign rising on the eastern horizon</note> or the Sun and Moon are exceptionally strong, they destroy the faults arising from the "Kick," the "Falling Stars," and other such defects. (70)<-

->( Afflictions of the Moon and Their Remedies )<-

The Moon is auspicious when associated with Mercury or Jupiter. It is not desired if it is placed in the 12th, 8th, or 6th houses from the Ascendant. However, if Jupiter occupies its own division <term>Varga: a harmonic chart or subdivision of a sign</term> or a quadrant and aspects <note>looks toward</note> the Moon, it destroys the fault known as "Planetary Association." (71)

->( The Poisonous Intervals of the Stars )<-

<note>This section lists specific time units called Ghatikas (24-minute periods) for each of the 27 stars that are considered "poisonous" or unlucky.</note>
Fifty, twenty-four, thirty, forty, fourteen, twenty-three, twenty, thirty-two, thirty, twenty, eighteen, eleven, twenty, fourteen, fourteen, ten, thirty-three, seventy-two... <note>The text continues listing numerical values for the remaining stars</note>
fifty-six, twenty-four, twenty, ten, ten, eighteen, sixteen, twenty-four, and thirty-two.

The following are the "Poisonous Minutes" <note>Sanskrit: *Visha-nadika*; specific inauspicious durations within a lunar mansion</note> for the constellations starting from Ashvini, occurring after the first four minutes of the designated starting point. || 73 ||

| Ashvini | Bharani | Krittika | Rohini | Mrigashira | Ardra | Punarvasu | Pushya |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| 50 | 24 | 30 | 40 | 14 | 21 | 30 | 20 |
| Ashlesha | Magha | Purva Phalguni | Uttara Phalguni | Hasta | Chitra | Svati | Vishakha |
| 32 | 30 | 20 | 18 | 21 | 20 | 14 | 14 |
| Anuradha | Jyeshtha | Mula | Purva Ashadha | Uttara Ashadha | Shravana | Dhanishtha | Shatabhisha |

| Purva Bhadrapada | Uttara Bhadrapada | Revati | | | | | |
| 16 | 24 | 30 | | | | | |

Multiply the duration of the constellation by the constant <note>the fixed starting point mentioned in the table</note> and divide by sixty (60). The result is the precise "Poisonous" duration. These calculated minutes must be avoided in journeys, marriages, and other auspicious ceremonies. || 74 ||

### (The Remedy for This)

> If the Moon is not in the cardinal houses <note>1st, 4th, 7th, or 10th houses</note> or the trines <note>5th or 9th houses</note>, but is aspected by benefic planets, or if the Lord of the Ascendant is in a cardinal house; or if the Moon is in a sign owned by a benefic, aspected by a friend, or in its own divisional chart, then the Lord of the Ascendant destroys the "Venomous Defect" <note>Sanskrit: *Shveda-dosha*; the astrological affliction caused by the poisonous minutes</note>. || 75 ||

### (Remedy for Defects of the 8th and 12th Houses and their Lords)

> The eighth or twelfth sign from one's birth sign or the Ascendant, and the lords of those signs, are not considered auspicious when placed in the Ascendant. The same applies to their divisional signs <note>Navamsha</note>. However, if there is friendship between the lords or if they are the same planet, this defect does not apply. || 76 ||

### (Statement on the Auspiciousness of the Eighth House in Even Signs)

> If the eighth house falls in an even sign <note>Taurus, Cancer, Virgo, Scorpio, Capricorn, or Pisces</note>, it leads to happiness, long life, and the increase of children and wealth. || 77 ||

### (Purification of the Ascendant, its Division, and the Seventh House)

When the Ascendant and its divisional sign are occupied or aspected by their own lords or friends, they bring happiness to the husband.

Page 54

If the seventh house <note>the house of marriage</note> is occupied or aspected <note>viewed by a planetary influence</note> by these two <note>referring to the planets mentioned on the previous page</note> and by the friends of the lord of the ascendant, it is suitable for a maiden. If it is otherwise, it is not auspicious, even if the ascendant is occupied or aspected by its own lord. However, if the seventh house is aspected or occupied by the lord of its own ninth division <term>Navamsha: a 1/9th sub-division of a zodiac sign used for fine-tuning predictions</term>, it leads to the wealth and happiness of the maiden. || 79 ||

### (The Kulika and the Half-Watch)

The *Kulika* <note>a specific inauspicious point in time</note> occurs during the day starting from the second period of the day onwards, and at night in reverse order starting from Saturday. The Half-Watch <term>Yamardha: a period of ninety minutes</term> is calculated from the first, third, and other odd periods, measured by the numbers:
<gloss>2, 5, 7, 3, 6</gloss>
two, five, seven, three, six, five, seven, three, and six. || 80 ||

### (Purification of the Signs and Ninth-Divisions)

Any sign from Aries onwards, when occupied or aspected by benefics, brings happiness in marriage and other ceremonies. According to some, the first part of Sagittarius, Gemini, Libra, Virgo, and also Pisces are auspicious ninth-divisions. The lord of the ascendant's ninth-division should not be Mercury, nor should it be in setting <note>combust or disappearing behind the sun</note>, nor joined with malefic planets or the Moon. One should avoid the final ninth-division of a sign unless it is a *Vargottama* <term>Vargottama: when a planet occupies the same sign in both the main chart and the ninth-division chart, increasing its strength</term>, and one should not choose a movable ninth-division when the ascendant itself is in a movable sign. || 81 ||

When the Moon is in Libra or Capricorn, the ascendant and the ninth-division are considered the same.
<gloss>3, 10, 9, 5, 4, 18</gloss>

### (Aspects)

Regarding the *Vargottama*, the aspects are three, zero, nine, and five; and four and eight. Saturn, Jupiter, and Mars <note>original: "Viputra," referring to the son of the Earth</note> || 83 ||
view fully in sequence, while others view with an increase of a quarter-step until the seventh house. A sign is considered strong when it is occupied or aspected by its own lord, or by Jupiter <note>original: "Vakpati," Lord of Speech</note> or Mercury <note>original: "Chandraja," Moon-born</note>. || 84 ||
If the conjunction of the Sun and Moon... is destroyed,
<gloss>0, 1, 4, 6, 10, 11, 15</gloss>
then the values are one, four, six, ten, eleven, and fifteen.
