<meta>This page continues the discussion on auspicious timing (Muhurta) for marriage, specifically addressing various planetary afflictions and their exceptions. It follows the standard mathematical calculations for determining favorable conditions.</meta>

<page-num>55</page-num>

The three days of the *Dhriti* <note>The 18th, 19th, and 20th lunar mansions or asterisms</note> are considered favorable for marriage when the *Lagna* <note>The rising sign or ascendant</note> is joined or aspected by two teachers <note>referring to the planets Jupiter and Venus</note>. || 85 ||

## (The Five-fold Affliction and its Exceptions)

Starting from the first day of the bright fortnight, the lunar dates (*Tithis*) should be counted and added to the number of the rising sign (*Lagna*), then divided by nine. If the remainders are 1, 2, 8, 6, or 4, they represent, in order: Loss, Fire, Disease, Theft, and the King <note>These are the five *Panchaka* afflictions representing different types of misfortune</note>. || 86 ||

One should avoid "Disease" when dealing with jewels and finery; "Fire" is always avoided in the house of Mars; "King" <note>representing government or legal trouble</note> is avoided in the house of Saturn. In service, travel, or night-journeys, avoid "Theft." In marriage and at the junctions of time, avoid "Death" <note>the remainder indicating total loss</note>. || 87 ||

## (The Affliction of the Seventh House)

Take the previous *Navamsha* <note>a 1/9th division of a zodiac sign</note> from the desired time, multiply by ten, and subtract it from the clear *Lagna* including the Moon. From the seventh house <term>Jamitra</term> <note>The 7th house from the ascendant, specifically relating to the spouse</note>, whether occupied by a benefic or malefic planet, calculate the minutes of arc and divide by 200. || 88 ||

If the result is 4 or 2, then disease and death may occur. However, this does not apply if the Sun is situated in the 3rd, 11th, or 6th house. If a benefic planet is in a center or a trine and not setting, and views the Moon with a full aspect, then the affliction does not occur. || 89 ||

## (Exceptions for Venus and Mars Afflictions)

Venus <insert>Bhrigu</insert> does not cause an affliction if situated in the first three signs starting from Cancer <note>Cancer, Leo, Virgo</note> or in Sagittarius, even if in the sixth house. Mars <insert>Bhauma</insert>, if setting in the houses of Mercury or the Moon, removes the affliction previously stated for the eighth house.
................................................................
................................................... || 90 ||

## (The Yogas of Water, Wind, and Lightning)

Add the lunar date (*Tithi*), the day of the week (*Vara*), and the constellation (*Nakshatra*). Multiply by 9 and divide by 7; if the remainder is 3, it is "Water."

<summary>This page details the mathematical calculation of the Panchaka and Jamitra afflictions in elective astrology, providing specific planetary positions that cancel out these negative influences during a marriage ceremony.</summary>

<meta>This page continues the technical discussion of astrological combinations (Yogas) and the purification of the Ascendant (Lagna) for auspicious timing, specifically transitioning into marriage rituals.</meta>

<page-num>56</page-num>

If the remainder is five (5), it is called the "Wind" combination (*Prabhanjana*); if the remainder is seven (7), it is indeed called the "Thunderbolt" combination (*Vajra Yoga*). || 91 ||

->( The results of Mercury, Jupiter, and Venus situated in Centers or Trines )<-

If Mercury <note>original: "Saumya"</note> is placed in a Center <term>Kendra: the 1st, 4th, 7th, or 10th houses</term> or a Trine <term>Trikona: the 1st, 5th, or 9th houses</term>, excluding the seventh house, it certainly destroys a hundred defects. Venus <note>original: "Asurapujita," the teacher of the demons</note> destroys two hundred, and Jupiter <note>original: "Vachasamadhisha," the Lord of Speech</note> destroys a hundred thousand defects. || 92 ||

->( Specific considerations for the purification of the Ascendant )<-

> When the Moon <note>original: "Piyushabhanu," the nectar-rayed one</note> or the Sun <note>original: "Chandabhanu," the hot-rayed one</note> is in the eleventh house, and Jupiter is in a Trine or a Center (excluding the seventh), and the Ascendant is in the best division <term>Vargottama: when a planet occupies the same sign in both the main chart and the ninth divisional chart</term>, the multitude of defects perishes. || 93 ||

> Benefic planets are best when situated in the 3rd, 6th, 9th, 11th, or 10th houses. Malefic planets are best in the 3rd, 11th, 8th, and 6th. The Moon is auspicious in the 11th, and Venus in the 3rd. In the 8th house, they are only half-effective. The lords of the Ascendant, the Navamsha <note>the 1/9th divisional chart</note>, and the Drekkana <note>the 1/3rd divisional chart</note>, along with Venus and the Moon, should not be in the 6th or 8th houses. All planets are auspicious in the 10th. Mars in the 12th, Saturn in the 1st, and Mercury or Jupiter in the 8th are considered unfavorable by the wise. || 94 ||

> Having understood the nine defects regarding lunar days, constellations, and weekdays mentioned for the marriage ceremony <note>original: "Kusume," literally 'in the flower'</note>, and considering the local customs and practical traditions, a learned scholar should pronounce the results of the Ascendant. || 95 ||

An Ascendant is auspicious if four desired factors are present; if more, it is excellent; if fewer, it is not recommended.

->( The Gandharva Marriage )<-

For those of mixed castes <note>original: "Sankirnabhuva"</note>, some authorities say that even on an unfavorable day or Ascendant, the marriage is auspicious <insert>if it is a Gandharva marriage, based on mutual attraction</insert>. || 96 ||

->( The Purification of the Three Strips )<-

In the "Three-Strip" system <term>Tripatti: a specific grid used to calculate planetary transit effects</term> starting from the Sun's constellation, the numbers 4, 2, 3, 1, 1, 8, 3, 1, and 2 are declared inauspicious. Others are auspicious for the remarriage of a woman <note>original: "Punarbhu," a woman marrying a second time</note>, provided the strength of the Moon is exceptional. || 97 ||

-> ( Auspicious Times for Marriage Components and Day Purification ) <-

One should perform the collection of tasks such as decorating the night, the pavilion, and the altar. This should be done under the marriage <term>Nakshatras</term> <note>lunar mansions or star clusters</note> when the Moon is strong, but not on the ninth, third, or sixth days from the previous phase. || 98 ||

-> ( The Twilight Hour ) <-

When Saturn is in the seventh house, Jupiter is setting, and the Moon is absent from the eighth or sixth house from the <term>Lagna</term> <note>the ascending sign on the eastern horizon</note>; or during the union of the Moon and Sun, or when the Sun is absent from the sixth house—the <term>Godhuli</term> <note>literally "cow-dust" time; the auspicious period of sunset</note> lasts for half a <term>Nadi</term> <note>approx. 12 minutes</note> on either side of sunset. || 99 ||

> Even in times troubled by cold or hot water <note>likely referring to unseasonable weather</note>, whether the Sun is visible, half-visible, scorching, or setting; the Twilight Hour is desired if the Moon is in the third, second, or eleventh house. However, Mars is not praised if situated in the seventh, eighth, or first house. || 100 ||

-> ( Knowledge of Auspiciousness through the Placement of the Water-Clock ) <-

The <term>Ghatika</term> <note>a copper bowl used to measure time by sinking in water</note> should be placed in a vessel made of a noble metal, filled with clean water, when the Sun is half-visible. If it tilts due to wind in the first three <term>Nakshatras</term>, it is not desired; it is considered auspicious in the fifth or other subsequent positions when full. || 101 ||

-> ( Height of the Marriage Altar and Pillars ) <-

The altar should be to the left of the house, four cubits <note>approx. 6 feet</note> square and one cubit high, joined with pillars and sloping towards the East. The pillar should be erected when the Sun is in Leo, Aries, or Gemini, placed in the Northeast, Northwest, Southwest, or Southeast corners.

-> ( Calculation of the Precise Ascendant ) <-

For Aries and the other signs, multiply by two, three, three, three, two, one, zero, one, one, one, zero, and one respectively. <insert>This involves</insert> doubling the degrees passed since the solar transit <note>Sankranti</note>. Subtract the minutes and hours for the signs from Pisces to Mountain <note>seven</note>, and add for the others.

| :---: | :---: | :---: | :---: | :---: | :---: | :---: | :---: | :---: | :---: | :---: | :---: |
| 2 | 3 | 3 | 3 | 2 | 1 | 0 | 1 | 1 | 1 | 0 | 1 |
| x | x | x | x | x | x | + | + | + | + | + | x |

--- Page 76 ---
<meta>This page continues the technical instructions for calculating astronomical positions and the timing of domestic rituals, specifically focusing on the transition from wedding ceremonies to the bride's entry into her new home.</meta>

<page-num>58</page-num>

The true Sun at the time of the Shaka era <note>The Shaka era is a historical calendar era beginning in 78 CE</note>, diminished by five hundred and four <gloss>444</gloss> <note>The gloss indicates a specific numerical value used in the calculation</note> and divided by sixty, gives the Precession of the Equinoxes <term>Ayanamsha: the longitudinal difference between the tropical and sidereal zodiacs</term>. By adding these to the Sun's position, or at nighttime by adding six signs to the Ascendant <term>Lagna: the zodiac sign rising on the eastern horizon</term>, one calculates the remaining portion of the sign to be traversed. || 104 ||

Multiply the traversed degrees by the local rising time of the sign and divide by thirty <gloss>30</gloss>; this gives the elapsed and remaining time respectively. By adding the rising times of the intervening signs and dividing by sixty, the time in ghatikas <term>Ghatika: a unit of time equal to 24 minutes</term> is determined when the Sun and the Ascendant are known. || 105 ||

When the Sun and the Ascendant, adjusted for precession, occupy the same sign, the difference between them is multiplied by the rising time. This is divided by thirty to find the time. If the Ascendant is less than the Sun, the time is subtracted from the full night duration. || 106 ||

->( Prohibitions until the dismantling of the marriage pavilion )<-

A wise man should not perform ancestral rites <term>Shraddha</term>, New Moon rites, or annual ceremonies—which require the sacred thread to be worn over the right shoulder—until the dismantling of the marriage pavilion is complete.

->( Days for dismantling the marriage pavilion )<-

One should perform this on even-numbered days, excluding the sixth day. On odd-numbered days, it is considered auspicious if done on the fifth or seventh day. || 107 ||

->( The bride’s entry into the home )<-

Avoiding the third, eleventh, and thirteenth lunar days, and avoiding the day of the week corresponding to the King <note>likely referring to Sunday or a specific unfavorable regnal day</note>, the new bride should enter her new home at night during a fixed zodiac sign, following the wedding. || 108 ||

->( The second arrival )<-

This ceremony of the second arrival <term>Dviragama: the formal return of the bride to her husband's home after the initial wedding</term> should be performed in an odd-numbered year, month, and day. After five years have passed, this rule no longer applies, as it is no longer considered necessary.

<meta>This page concludes the section on marriage (Vivāha) and begins the section on the establishment of the sacred fire (Agnihotra). It continues the technical astrological instructions for timing domestic rituals.</meta>

The marriage [is auspicious] under the constellations of Ashvini, Shravana, Dhanishta, Chitra, Swati, and Shatabhisha, provided it is not a "Empty" lunar day <note>Rikta tithis: the 4th, 9th, and 14th days of the lunar fortnight, considered unlucky</note> and avoids Tuesday and Sunday. || 109 ||

The second entry <note>Dvirāgama: the ceremony of the bride returning to her husband's home for the second time</note> should occur when the Sun is in Aries, Aquarius, or Scorpio, under soft, swift, or mobile constellations. It is desired in an odd-numbered year [of marriage] when the Sun and Jupiter are astrologically pure, on a day ruled by Mercury, Jupiter, or Venus, and avoiding the Mula constellation.

### (Prohibition of residing in the father's house after marriage)

In the month of Chaitra <note>March-April</note>, the bride should not stay in her father's house; in the first month [of marriage], she should not dwell in her husband's house. If she stays during an intercalary month <note>Adhika-masa: an extra month added to the lunar calendar to keep it in sync with the solar year</note>, or the months of Ashadha, Jyeshtha, or Pausha, it is said to grant undesirable results. || 111 ||

### (Exceptions to the affliction of Venus)

If the physical signs of puberty <note>literally: "the budding of the breasts"</note> appear while the girl is still in her father's house, then the "Counter-Venus" <note>Prati-shukra: an astrological position where Venus is directly in front of the traveler or bride</note> does not cause harm; how much less then could Rahu or Ketu <note>the shadow planets causing eclipses</note> cause harm? For those of the lineages of Bharadvaja, Atri, Angiras, Vatsa, Vashistha, Kashyapa, and Bhrigu; or in times of theft, famine, or danger from a king; or at a sacred pilgrimage site, a festival, or the unification of villages—in these cases, the "Counter-Venus" is not considered an obstacle for marriage or the bride's entry into her new home. A pregnant woman, a woman with a young child, or a newlywed should not travel in the direction facing or to the right of Venus. || 114 ||

The first enjoyment or the taking of the hand [marriage] under Revati or Mrigashira makes the husband dear to the woman. Under Swati, Hasta, Anuradha, Aryaman <note>Uttara Phalguni</note>, or Magha, there will be deep love between the young couple. If under Mula or Vishakha, the young woman becomes even more beloved to her husband. || 115 ||

Having thoroughly examined many texts, this "Flower" <note>chapter</note> named "Marriage" in the book titled *Muhūrtakalpadruma* <note>The Desire-Granting Tree of Electional Astrology</note>, composed by Vitthala Dikshita, is now complete. || 116 ||

### ( Now, the Flower of Establishing the Sacred Fire )

The Agnihotra <note>the ritual of maintaining the sacred fire</note> is a boat for crossing the ocean of worldly existence and a ladder to heaven that is not far off. Since it is intended for Brahmins, Vaishyas, and Kings <note>Kshatriyas</note>, I shall now speak of its timing as ordained by the Sages. || 1 ||

<summary>This page concludes the marriage chapter of the Muhūrtakalpadruma, detailing the timing for a bride's movements and exceptions to astrological taboos, before introducing the ritual requirements for the Agnihotra fire sacrifice.</summary>
<keywords>marriage astrology, Vitthala Dikshita, Agnihotra, Venus affliction, Gotra lineages, bride's homecoming, Muhurtakalpadruma</keywords>

--- Page 78 ---
<meta>This page continues the technical instructions for Vedic rituals and royal ceremonies from the previous section of the Muhurta Kalpadruma.</meta>

<page-num>60</page-num>

The Agnihotra <note>The sacred fire-offering ritual performed daily by householders</note> is auspicious when the sun is in its northern course <note>Uttarayana</note>, and during the lunar mansions <term>Nakshatras: constellations used in Vedic astrology</term> of Mrigashira, Pushya, Revati, the three fixed stars <note>Uttara Phalguni, Uttara Ashadha, Uttara Bhadrapada</note>, Rohini, Jyeshtha, Chitra, and Punarvasu. It should be avoided on "empty" days <note>Rikta Tithis: the 4th, 9th, and 14th days of the lunar fortnight</note>, Tuesdays, and Saturdays. The ritual is best performed when the moon is in the three signs beginning with Capricorn <note>Capricorn, Aquarius, Pisces</note> or in the divisions of those signs. || 2 ||

It should not be performed when the Moon or Venus is in the ascendant <note>Lagna</note>, nor when Jupiter, the Moon, or Mars are setting, in their sign of debilitation, or in an enemy's sign. The eighth house must be pure <note>empty of planets</note>. It is excellent when the Sun, Moon, Jupiter, and Mars are in the center <term>Kendra: 1st, 4th, 7th, or 10th houses</term>, the trines <term>Trikona: 5th or 9th houses</term>, or the houses of increase <term>Upachaya: 3rd, 6th, 10th, or 11th houses</term>. Other planets should be in the 3rd, 11th, or 6th houses. If the Moon is in the 11th, 3rd, or 6th house, the sacrificer prospers. If Jupiter is in Sagittarius in the ascendant, or if Mars is in Aries in the 7th or 10th house, the lord of the twice-born <note>the Brahmin performing the rite</note> emerges victorious.

After thoroughly examining many texts, this "Flower" <note>chapter</note> named the "Establishment of the Sacred Fire" has been completed by Vitthala Dikshita in the work titled *Muhurta Kalpadruma* <note>The Desire-Fulfilling Tree of Electional Astrology</note>. || 5 ||

### ( Now, the Chapter on Royal Consecration )

Since no legal or worldly affairs can be accomplished without the command of kings, the ruler must be consecrated upon the throne by the people to gain prestige. Therefore, I shall declare the proper time for this according to the scriptures.

The consecration of a king should occur during the six months beginning with Magha <note>the winter and spring months</note>, excluding the intercalary month and the month of Chaitra. It should be in the bright fortnight, avoiding the "empty" days, the new moon, and Tuesdays. The ceremony should take place during the day under the "swift," "gentle," "fixed," "royal" <note>Jyeshtha</note>, or "Vishnu" <note>Shravana</note> constellations. || 2 ||

The consecration is recommended when the lords of the Dasha <note>planetary period</note>, the birth sign, Venus, Jupiter, and the Sun—as well as the lords of the birth ascendant, Mars, and the Moon—are rising with strength. It should be performed in a "movable" sign for growth or a "fixed" sign for stability. || 3 ||

->Benefic planets should be placed in the trines,<-
->the centers, or the 3rd, 2nd, and 11th houses.<-
->Other planets are auspicious in the 3rd, 11th, and 6th.<-
->The Moon situated in the 1st, 12th, 8th, or 6th house<-
->is not auspicious for granting the royal title. || 4 ||<-

If Jupiter is in the 4th or 10th house, or in the 3rd or 11th, and the Sun and Saturn are well-placed, the lord of the earth lives a long life. If Jupiter is in the ascendant or a trine, Venus is in the 10th, and Mars is in the 6th, the King becomes like Indra <note>the King of the Gods</note>.

After thoroughly examining many texts, this "Flower" <note>chapter</note> named "Royal Consecration" has been completed by Vitthala Dikshita in the work titled *Muhurta Kalpadruma*. || 6 ||

## ( Now, The Flower of Travel )

For the lords of the twice-born <note>original: "dvijeshvaranam"; referring to the priestly or scholarly class</note> and the lords of the earth <note>original: "avanishvaranam"; referring to kings and the ruling class</note>, the desired success does not manifest without a properly timed journey. Therefore, following the teachings of the ancient sages, I shall first explain the considerations for travel based on careful deliberation.

### ( Decision in the Absence of Knowledge of Birth Chart or Moon Sign )

For those who truly have no knowledge of their birth ascendant <term>Lagna: the zodiac sign rising on the eastern horizon at the time of birth</term> or the position of the Moon, the outcome should be determined through omens <note>original: "shakuna"; signs observed in nature or the environment</note> and similar signs. From these, all auspicious results should be declared, just as one would through the gateway of the birth ascendant and zodiac sign. 2

### ( Whether the Journey Will Be Successful or Not )

If the ascendant at the time of the inquiry <term>Prashna Lagna: a chart cast for the exact moment a question is asked</term> is a movable sign <note>Aries, Cancer, Libra, or Capricorn</note> and is joined or aspected by benefic planets, it indicates a favorable outcome. A fixed sign <note>Taurus, Leo, Scorpio, or Aquarius</note> does not favor travel; however, the aspect or conjunction of a benefic planet must still be considered for the final result. 3

If the inquiry ascendant is joined or aspected by malefic planets, or if it is otherwise afflicted, and a person sets out by force or stubbornness, they will return with their spirit and strength broken. 4

If, at the time of the inquiry, there is the sound of auspicious musical instruments, pleasant speech, the scent of white flowers, or the sight of gold and other auspicious items, success is indicated. However, the sight of oil and similar substances does not lead to success. 5

If a malefic planet is in the eighth house from the inquiry ascendant, or in the ascendant itself without the aspect of a benefic, the journey will not be successful. Similarly, a retrograde planet in the ascendant, or the lord of the ascendant in the eighth house <note>the house of death and obstacles</note>, is unfavorable. 6

If one or two powerful planets are in the inquiry ascendant and are aspected by benefics, one should predict immediate gain for an ordinary person, and for a king, the acquisition of land. 7

### ( Travel According to the Sun’s Transit )

When the Sun is in the signs of the Horse <note>Sagittarius</note>, the Lion <note>Leo</note>, or the Ram <note>Aries</note>, travel brings endless happiness. If the Sun is in the houses of Mercury, Saturn, or Venus <note>Gemini, Virgo, Capricorn, Aquarius, Taurus, or Libra</note>, the result is mediocre. In other signs, it is not good. 8

### ( Auspicious Lunar Days for Travel )

->The second, third, fifth, eleventh, tenth, and seventh lunar days<-
->as well as the thirteenth, are the lunar days that grant the fruits of travel. 1<-

White and other bright colors are considered auspicious, as are the days and divisions of the zodiac <term>vargas; the sixteen sub-divisions of a sign</term> associated with benefic planets. || 9 ||

### (The Yogini)
<note>The Yogini is a mystical energy or spirit that moves through the eight directions on a lunar cycle. Traveling toward the Yogini is considered highly dangerous.</note>

The numbers of the lunar days <term>tithis</term> are: nine and one; eleven and three; thirteen and five; twelve and four; fourteen and six; fifteen and seven; eight and ten; and two. Subtract these in order starting from the Northeast to find the positions of the Yoginis.

### (The Noose of Time)
<note>Kalapasha; literally 'The Noose of Death/Time,' referring to planetary combinations that 'strangle' the success of a journey.</note>

Saturn and the Moon in the East; Jupiter, the Sun, and Venus in the South; Mars and Mercury in the West—these are not recommended for setting out.
However, Mars, Saturn, and the Moon; Mercury and Jupiter; and Venus and the Sun grant wealth and happiness when traveling toward the South and other directions <note>depending on their specific placement</note>.
The Sun moves in reverse order from the direction of Kubera <note>the North</note>; Time <term>Kala</term> is always directly in front of him, and the Noose <term>Pasha</term> follows. At night, these two also move in reverse. They must be avoided during travels and in war. 1

### (Constellations for Travel)
<note>Nakshatras; the 27 lunar mansions of the zodiac.</note>

The constellations of Dhanishtha, Mrigashira, Pushya, Punarvasu, Hasta, Anuradha, Shravana, Dhanishtha <insert>repeated</insert>, and Revati are the best.
The three "pairs" <note>Purva-Phalguni/Uttara-Phalguni, Purva-Ashadha/Uttara-Ashadha, Purva-Bhadrapada/Uttara-Bhadrapada</note>, Mula, Shatabhisha, and Krittika are middling. Others are unfavorable. <gloss>3 4 5</gloss> The third, fourth, and fifth <term>taras; star-strengths</term> are not desired. The constellation of Indra <note>Jyeshtha</note> is considered middling by some sages.

### (The Spear of the Constellations)
<note>Nakshatra-Shula; specific directions that are 'pierced' by certain stars, making travel there fatal.</note>

Jyeshtha, Purva-Bhadrapada, Rohini, and Aryaman <note>Uttara-Phalguni</note> are the "Spears" of the constellations for the East and other directions; they are not favorable. || 14 ||

### (Inauspicious Hours of the Constellations)
<note>Tyajya-ghatika; specific moments within a constellation's duration that must be abandoned or avoided.</note>

In the constellations of Bharani, Krittika, Magha... sixteen <note>ghatikas</note>—

The first <term>Ghatikas</term><note>A unit of time equal to 24 minutes</note> of the following <term>Nakshatras</term><note>Lunar mansions or star signs</note> are considered unfavorable: seven for *Magha*, twenty-one for *Svati*, and eleven for *Revati*. In the mansions of *Svati*, *Vishakha*, *Jyeshtha*, and *Ashlesha*, one must avoid the first fourteen *Ghatikas* when embarking on an essential journey. || 15 ||

A wise man should write the seven sets of seven stars starting from the mansion of Fire (*Krittika*), placing them in a clockwise direction starting from the East. One should draw a line from the Southeast toward the Northwest; this is the <term>Paridhi</term><note>Literally a 'circumference' or 'boundary' line used in protective rituals</note>. For minor tasks, one should not cross this line. || 16 ||

---

->**The Rule of the Boundary**<-

However, for an urgent or mandatory task, a King may travel even after crossing the *Paridhi* line, provided he avoids the "Spikes" (*Shulas*) <note>Astrological directions of sharp negative energy</note> of his own direction and if the <term>Lagna</term><note>The ascendant or rising sign</note> is found to be pure and auspicious. || 17 ||

One should travel to the North using the stars situated in the East; to the West using those in the East and South; and to the South using those in the East. To reach the Southeast corner, one should proceed from the right; in this manner, one should travel to the intermediate corners using the stars assigned to those directions. || 18 ||

During the three parts of the day, one should be guided by the Mixed, Fixed, Sharp, and Swift stars respectively. Likewise, during the three parts of the night, one should be guided by the Friendly, Terrible, and Movable stars. One should not set out for travel otherwise. || 19 ||

->( Travel in All Directions )<-

In the lunar mansions of Shravana, Dhanishta, Shatabhisha, and likewise in Pushya, this "Yama" <note>A specific inauspicious astrological period or "gate" of death</note> arising from time is not prescribed. One may travel in all directions during the mansions of Hasta, Pushya, Ashwini, and Maitra <note>Anuradha</note>. (2)

->( Travel According to the Solstices )<-

If the Sun and Moon are in the Northern Solstice <term>Uttarayana: the period between the winter and summer solstices</term>, one should travel toward the East or North. If they are in the Southern Solstice <term>Dakshinayana: the period between the summer and winter solstices</term>, travel toward the South or West. If the day and night are divided, the Sun’s solstice governs the day and the Moon’s solstice governs the night. (21)
Otherwise, there is great loss, as well as the suffering of pain and imprisonment for the King; therefore, the solstice must be carefully considered. (22)

->( The Designations of Living and Dead Wings of Nakshatras )<-

The lunar mansion <term>Nakshatra: one of the 27 sectors of the lunar path</term> currently occupied by Rahu <note>The north node of the moon, envisioned as a celestial dragon</note> is called the "Mouth." The thirteen mansions previously passed by it are called "Living." Beyond that is the "Tail" mansion, and the thirteen mansions following that are considered the "Dead" or "Wing" mansions. (23)

->( Results of the Living and Dead Wings )<-

If the Sun is in the "Living" wing and the Moon is in the "Living" wing, travel is auspicious; otherwise, it is unsuccessful. If both are in the "Living" wing, it is declared extremely auspicious. If both are in the "Dead" wing, it is prescribed as bringing great difficulty. (24)
Travel toward the "Tail" mansion from a "Dead" mansion gives desired results; likewise, the "Mouth" mansion is auspicious for departures. The Sun is considered stationary while the Moon is fleeting; when either is in the "Living" wing, it grants victory in due order. (25)
When both are in the "Living" wing, there is a peaceful alliance; if both are in the "Dead" wing, there will be defeat.

->( The Cat and Adala Yogas )<-

When the Sun’s position is added to the current lunar mansion <note>Abhijit is included in this calculation</note> and the sum is divided by seven, if the remainder is three or six, the unfavorable configurations known as *Vidala* <note>The Cat</note> and *Adala* are formed.

->( The Results of Vidala and Adala )<-

The sage Devala states that one should never set out on a journey during these times. Furthermore, these two configurations are not beneficial for building tanks <note>reservoirs</note>, engaging in battle, or dealing with elephants and other livestock.

->( The Chart of Family, Sub-Family, and Mixed Groups with Results )<-

In the "Family" group <term>Kula: a category of auspicious stars</term>, the stars are Ashvini, Purva Phalguni, Mrigashira, Magha, Vishakha, Krittika, Dhanishta, Chitra, Pushya, and the three "Ushas" <note>Uttara Phalguni, Uttara Ashadha, Uttara Bhadrapada</note>, and Mula. The odd-numbered lunar days <term>Tithis</term> and the days of the Sun, Jupiter, Saturn, and the Moon are "Sub-Family" <term>Upakula</term>.
Next, Punarvasu, Hasta, Purva Ashadha, Purva Bhadrapada, Revati, Shravana, Swati, Bharani, Ashlesha, and Rohini <gloss>14, 4, 13</gloss> along with the lunar days 4, 8, 12, 14, and the days of Venus and Mars are the other "Mixed" <term>Kulakula</term> groups.

| | Lunar Mansions (Nakshatras) | Lunar Days (Tithis) | Weekdays (Varas) |
|:---|:---|:---|:---|
| **Family (Kula)** | Ashvini, P. Phalguni, Mrigashira, Magha, Vishakha, Krittika, Dhanishta, Chitra, Pushya, U. Phalguni, U. Ashadha, U. Bhadrapada, Mula. | 1, 3, 5, 7, 9, 11, 13, 15 | Sunday, Thursday, Saturday, Monday |
| **Sub-Family (Upakula)** | Punarvasu, Hasta, P. Ashadha, P. Bhadrapada, Revati, Shravana, Swati, Bharani, Ashlesha, Rohini. | 4, 8, 12, 14 | Tuesday, Friday |
| **Mixed (Kulakula)** | Ardra, Shatabhisha, Anuradha, Jyeshtha. | 2, 6, 10, 30 | Wednesday |

In the "Family" group, the king who stays in the city is victorious. In the "Sub-Family" group, the king who is on the move <note>attacking</note> is victorious. In the "Mixed" group, there will be a peace treaty between the two lords of the earth even after much fighting.

->( The Result of Totaling All Factors )<-

When the values of the stars, weekdays, and lunar days are combined and multiplied by three, seven, or eight... <note>The text breaks off here regarding the calculation for auspicious moments</note>
Auspicious Moment <term>Muhurta</term> 5.

Others find no happiness in the loss of wealth; labor and death are marked here among the pleasures. || 31 || <note>This verse likely refers to the paradoxical nature of worldly pursuits where effort often leads to exhaustion rather than joy.</note>

->( The Fruitful Wheel of Rahu on the Path )<-

One should write the four groups of lunar mansions <term>Nakshatras: the 27 (or 28) sectors of the sky the moon passes through</term> starting from Ashvini, in both regular and reverse order, including Abhijit. When the Sun is positioned at the "Face of Dharma," there is a state of liberation, wealth, cattle, virtue, and final beatitude. ->( The Dangerous Moon for Aries and other Zodiac Signs, with Exceptions )<-

Aries, Virgo, Aquarius, Leo, Capricorn, Gemini, Sagittarius, Taurus, Pisces, Leo, and Sagittarius: for Aries and the subsequent signs, these are the "Dangerous Moons" <term>Ghatachandra: a specific lunar position considered harmful for a person based on their birth sign</term>. They are forbidden in travel, war, royal service, and disputes. || 33 ||

They are also prohibited in illness, mounting a vehicle, the sacred thread ceremony, the first hair-cutting, pilgrimages, and weddings. However, they need not be considered during the first feeding of solid food or other auspicious celebrations; such is the authoritative command of the ancients. || 34 ||

## ( Harmful Lunar Days )

The *Nanda* days <note>The 1st, 6th, and 11th lunar days</note> are harmful for Scorpio and Aries; the *Bhadra* days <note>2nd, 7th, and 12th</note> for Gemini and Cancer; the *Jaya* days <note>3rd, 8th, and 13th</note> for Leo, Sagittarius, and Aquarius; the *Rikta* days <note>4th, 9th, and 14th</note> for Libra and Capricorn; and the *Purna* days <note>5th, 10th, and 15th/Full Moon</note> for Pisces, Virgo, and Taurus. These are known as the *Ghata Tithis* <term>Ghata Tithi: specific lunar days considered dangerous or unlucky for an individual based on their moon sign</term>. (35)

### The Flaw of Facing Venus and its Remedy

> In whichever celestial sphere it rotates, or wherever it rises, or in whichever directional axis it moves; in all three ways, the state of "facing Venus" <note>Sanskrit: *sanmukha-shukra*; considered highly inauspicious for travel</note> is considered undesirable. However, those learned in the sacred laws especially advise avoiding the direction of its rising. || 39 ||

### Exceptions to the Flaws of Venus's Retrograde and Combustion

When Venus is in its sign of debilitation <note>Virgo</note>, or is combust <note>hidden by the sun's glare</note>, or is in the house of an enemy, or is in retrograde motion; then a king's military expedition will not lead to victory. However, if Mercury is positioned behind [the traveler], there will be well-being. || 40 ||

### The "Blindness" of Venus and the Results of Travel

> As long as the Moon is in the constellations of Revati <note>original: *pusha*</note> or Aries, Venus is considered to be "deprived of eyes" <note>blind</note>. In such a state, it is not harmful if it is to the south or in front; or conversely, if it is to the east or west during a journey. || 41 ||

> If the Moon <note>original: *dwijeshvara*</note>, Jupiter, or Venus should happen to set <note>become combust</note> while one is in the middle of a journey, the King should halt and stay where he is until their rising. In the case of Mercury, the rules regarding "facing the planet" are applied in reverse. || 42 ||

It advises rulers to halt expeditions if key celestial bodies become invisible during their journey.</summary>

<page-num>69</page-num>

### (Considerations for Purifying the Ascendant for Travel)

The sign which is the eighth from one's birth Ascendant <term>Lagna; the rising sign at birth</term> or from one's birth Moon-sign <term>Janma Rashi</term> should not be the Ascendant at the time of departure. Furthermore, the seventh division <term>Saptamsha; a 1/7th divisional chart</term> of that sign, or a sign that is the sixth from one's enemy's house, is not auspicious for the Ascendant. || 43 ||

In all cases, one should abandon the following for travel: a weak sign, the sign of one's enemy, a malefic sign, Aquarius, signs that rise from the back <term>Prishthodaya; signs like Aries or Taurus that are considered less auspicious for certain beginnings</term>, a sign containing a retrograde planet, and a sign lacking in prosperity <gloss>1</gloss> <note>original: "riddhi-hinam"</note>. One should also avoid Pisces and its divisional portions during a journey. || 44 ||

A retrograde planet positioned in a cardinal house <term>Kendra; the 1st, 4th, 7th, or 10th houses</term> is forbidden. Neither the day of the week associated with such a planet nor its divisional portion <term>Varga</term> is recommended in the Ascendant. The sages have declared that a planet inimical to the lord of one's birth sign or birth Ascendant is also not auspicious when placed in the Ascendant of departure. || 45 ||

### (Lords of the Directions; Success when the Lord is in a Cardinal House)

The Sun, Venus, Mars, Rahu <term>the North Node of the Moon</term>, Saturn, the Moon, Mercury, and Jupiter are the lords of the directions starting from the East. If the lord of the direction of travel is in the eleventh house <term>Labha-sthana; the house of gains</term>, one should not go; however, if that lord is in a cardinal house <term>Kendra</term>, one should proceed.

### (The Forehead-Marking Combination and its Results)

If the Sun is in the Ascendant, Venus in the second house, Mars in the twelfth, Rahu in the tenth, the Moon in the eighth, Saturn in the seventh, Mercury in the sixth or fifth, and Jupiter is without strength in the second or third house—these are called "Forehead-Marking" <term>Lalatikayoga</term> combinations. They are declared by the authorities to be inauspicious for travel. || 47 ||

---

<margin>1. Prosperity: Refers to the houses of increase <term>Upachaya-sthana; the 3rd, 6th, 10th, and 11th houses</term>.</margin>

<meta>This page continues the technical discussion of astrological timing for journeys, specifically focusing on the auspicious placement of planets and signs relative to the traveler's birth chart.</meta>

<page-num>70</page-num>

If the *Veshi* <note>original: "veshi" — a specific solar yoga or planetary configuration where a planet (other than the Moon) occupies the 2nd house from the Sun</note> is associated with a benefic planet and is located in the Ascendant <note>the zodiac sign rising on the eastern horizon at the time of travel</note>, or if those signs which are occupied by benefics at the time of birth are present; or if the signs in the Ascendant are the same as the birth sign <note>the sign the Moon occupied at birth</note>, or are in the "houses of increase" <note>original: "vriddhi" or "upachaya" — the 3rd, 6th, 10th, and 11th houses, which signify growth and improvement</note> from the birth Ascendant, they are considered auspicious. **48**

If the Ascendant at the time of the journey is the same as the birth sign, or the birth Ascendant, or if the lord of that sign is placed in the Ascendant, these are auspicious for travel—provided that the lord of the sign is a benefic planet. **49**

Even malefic planets, if they are in their own signs, in a friend's sign, or in their sign of exaltation and placed in the Ascendant, produce auspicious results. If the lords of the birth sign and the birth Ascendant are in a quadrant <note>the 1st, 4th, 7th, or 10th houses</note>, even a day ruled by an enemy planet yields little negative fruit if a benefic is in the Ascendant. **50**

->( Rules for Timing Journeys according to the Directions )<-

The dawn is praised for travel, except when heading toward the direction of Vishnu <note>the North</note>. Similarly, the *Abhijit* period <note>a short auspicious interval around midday</note> is excellent, except when traveling toward the South. The "cow-dust" hour <note>original: "godhulika" — twilight/sunset</note> is best, except when heading West. Midnight is most excellent, except when heading North. **51**

In the forenoon, one should travel toward the direction of the Moon <note>the North</note>; at midday, one should travel toward the direction of Indra <note>the East</note>.

For the success of your task, travel toward the South in the afternoon; travel toward the West during the midnight hours. || 52 ||

### (Specifics on Setting Out According to Lunar Mansions)

If one sets out under the lunar mansion <term>Mrigashira</term><note>original: "chandre," referring to the mansion ruled by the Moon</note> and halts during <term>Ardra</term><note>original: "shivabhe," the star associated with Shiva</note>, or sets out under <term>Punarvasu</term>, he attains great comforts. Likewise, one who begins a journey under <term>Anuradha</term><note>original: "maitre," the star associated with Mitra</note> and stays under <term>Jyeshtha</term><note>original: "shakre," associated with Indra</note> should then proceed to <term>Mula</term> for certain victory. || 53 ||

->Setting out under <term>Hasta</term> and staying<-
->under the pair of <term>Chitra</term> and <term>Vishakha</term>, one becomes victorious.<-
If a King sets out under <term>Dhanishtha</term><note>original: "vasu"</note>, <term>Revati</term><note>original: "antya"</note>, or <term>Pushya</term>, and stays within his own borders for one night before proceeding, he conquers the earth. || 54 ||

### (Specifics on the Purity of the Ascendant)

Benefic planets are not auspicious if placed in the sixth, eighth, or twelfth houses; they are auspicious in other houses. Malefic planets are best in the houses of growth <note>the 3rd, 6th, 10th, and 11th houses</note>. Saturn is auspicious in the tenth house. Venus should not be in the seventh house. The Moon is unfavorable in the ascendant, the sixth, the eighth, or the twelfth houses, as well as in the "three-fold" positions <note>referring to specific difficult alignments in the 1st, 12th, and 6th</note>. || 55 ||

### (The Twelve Houses from the Ascendant and the Results of Planets Therein)

The body <note>1st house</note>, wealth <note>2nd</note>, servants <note>3rd</note>, vehicles <note>4th</note>, counsel <note>5th</note>, enemies <note>6th</note>, the path <note>7th</note>, life/longevity <note>8th</note>, heart/religion <note>9th</note>, business <note>10th</note>, gains <note>11th</note>, and expenditure <note>12th</note>—if a benefic planet resides in these houses, the result is good; if a malefic planet resides there, it brings sorrow. || 56 ||

### (Which Houses are Not Harmed by Benefic or Malefic Planets)

A malefic planet does not destroy the third, eleventh, or sixth houses. The Sun does not destroy the position it occupies, nor is Mars destroyed <note>rendered ineffective</note> in certain placements.

The gentle planets <note>benefics like Mercury, Jupiter, and Venus</note> nourish all aspects of life, except when they are placed in the house of enemies <note>the 6th house</note> or are setting <note>combust/too close to the sun</note>. ->(The author explains with an illustration how a planet gives specific results)<-

Just as poison may be used for a beneficial purpose depending on the disease, or as clarified butter <term>Ghee: a staple of Indian medicine and ritual</term> becomes poisonous when mixed in equal parts with honey; so too does a planet here abandon its own natural fruit to extend a specific result born from its combination with others. || 58 ||

->(Attainment of results for the priestly class and others based on constellations and other factors)<-

For the twice-born <note>the upper social classes, particularly Brahmins</note>, results come through the qualities of the constellations <term>Nakshatras: the 27 lunar mansions of the zodiac</term>. For rulers, results come through planetary combinations. For thieves and outlaws, success comes through omens, and for the rest of the world, through the power of the auspicious moment <term>Muhurta: a calculated favorable time to begin an action</term>. || 59 ||

->(Special combinations for the purity of the Ascendant)<-

If Jupiter is in the Ascendant, Mercury in the second house, and the Sun in the third; or if the Moon is in the seventh house while the Sun is rising, and Mercury, Jupiter, and Venus have left the house of wealth <note>the 2nd house</note>: || 60 ||

If the Moon is situated in the fourth house and Venus is joined with Mercury in the seventh; then a King conquers his enemies and the wicked through righteous means. || 61 ||

When the Moon is in the seventh house and Venus and Mercury are taking shelter in the fourth; then the enemies of one who has set out on a journey wander about like scornful beggars. || 62 ||

If Jupiter is in the 3rd or 11th house, the Sun is in the 3rd, Saturn is in the 3rd, Jupiter is in the Ascendant, and Mars is in the house of enemies <note>the 6th house</note>; if Venus is also favorably disposed, what could a world-conquering Emperor <term>Chakravartin: an ideal universal ruler</term> not achieve? || 63 ||

If at the time of a king’s march for victory <note>Jayaprayana: a ritualized departure for war</note>, the Sun <note>Dinesha: "Lord of the Day"</note> is in the Ascendant <term>Lagna: the zodiac sign rising on the eastern horizon</term>, Saturn <note>Shani</note> is in the sixth house, and the Moon <note>Shashi</note> is in the tenth house, the enemy's army quickly comes under his control, just as a woman overcome by the fierce arrows of passion yields to her lover. 64

->If Jupiter <note>Guru</note> is in the body <note>the first house/Ascendant</note>, the Moon <note>Himanshu</note> is in the seventh,<-

When Mars and Saturn are in the sixth, third, or eleventh houses, and Jupiter, Mercury, and Venus are placed anywhere else but are powerful <note>Ojasvin: possessing vital strength</note>, the king will be victorious in a long war, like the hero Balarama <note>Musali: "The one who wields the plow"</note>. 67

If Mercury, Venus, and Jupiter <note>Amaranathapujya: "The one worshiped by the Lord of the Gods"</note> are in the second house, the Moon <note>Yaminisha: "Lord of the Night"</note> is in the seventh house, and the Sun is in the Ascendant, the king who sets out is like Garuda <note>Vainateya</note> against a swarm of enemy serpents. 68

If Jupiter <note>Surejya</note> or the Moon <note>Kshepesha: "Lord of the Night"</note> is in the Ascendant, the Sun is in the sixth, Saturn is in the tenth, and Venus and Mercury are in the fourth or fifth houses, the journey will bring great happiness, like a mother. 70

If Venus is in the fourth, eleventh, or third house and is aspected <note>viewed</note> by Jupiter located in a quadrant <term>Kendra: the 1st, 4th, 7th, or 10th houses</term>, and if no malefic planets are in the seventh, third, or eleventh, what wealth is there that the king cannot obtain through his own valor? 71

When Venus, Mars, Jupiter, Saturn, and Mercury <note>Shashankaputra: "Son of the Moon"</note> are placed respectively in the Ascendant, the sixth, the seventh, the third, and the fourth houses, the streams of tears from the eyes of the enemy's wives will mock the falling rain from the clouds. 72

When the Sun resides in the house of enemies <note>6th house</note>, the Moon is in the house of gains <note>11th house</note>, and Jupiter is in the Ascendant, the king who sets out is like Arjuna <note>Dhananjaya</note> in burning down the forest of the enemy's army. 73

The tenth day of the bright fortnight in the month of Ashvin <note>September–October</note> brings success and victory for those departing. ### ->( Prohibitions Regarding Travel )<-

As long as the ceremonies for marriage, the sacred thread investiture <note>the *maunji* ceremony for young students</note>, or the consecration of a deity's image are not yet completed, or while a period of ritual impurity <note>*ashaucha*, usually following a birth or death</note> persists, one should not undertake a journey. Furthermore, one who desires a long life should not travel during unseasonable rainfall. If unseasonable rain occurs during the four months beginning with the month of *Sahasyam* <note>roughly mid-December to mid-January</note>, and the ground is marked by the footprints of bipeds <note>humans</note> or quadrupeds, it is said to bring misfortune; the severity of the result depends on whether the rain was light or heavy. However, there is no fault for a king who is already in the midst of his journey. If a journey is absolutely necessary, the King should proceed only after making offerings of gold or images representing the discs of the Sun and the Moon. || 92 ||

### ->( Prohibition on Entering and Leaving in the Same Week )<-

If a King must both depart and return within the same week, he must consider the timing carefully. It is considered inauspicious to depart on the ninth day after an entry, or to enter on the ninth day after a departure.

### ->( What Must Be Done Before Departure )<-

Having gratified the sacrificial fire with oblations and the twice-born <term>Brahmins: the priestly class</term> with gifts of cows, land, and gold; having bowed to his elders and satisfied his wife, friends, and sons, the King may set forth. After bowing with faith to his personal deity <note>*Ishta-deva*</note> and the guardian deity of his direction, and wearing white garlands and garments, he should step out with the foot corresponding to his dominant breath <note>referring to *Swarodaya* yoga, or the flow of air through the nostrils</note>, or with his right foot, while remaining silent. After walking thirty-two <gloss>32</gloss> steps, the King should proceed with a happy heart while observing auspicious omens, traveling by elephant, chariot, or palanquin <note>literally: a "man-powered vehicle"</note> toward the east or other intended directions.

### ->( What to Do if One Cannot Go Personally )<-

If, due to urgent business or other reasons, the King himself cannot depart at the auspicious moment, he should instead send a Brahmin or a representative carrying a fruitful sacred thread <term>Upavita: the sacrificial cord worn by the upper castes</term>. Alternatively, he may send his weapon, honey, sweet fruit, or his favorite clothing and similar items in his stead. || 97 ||

### (From which place and to what distance a journey should be made)

From one’s own palace, a temple, the hall of the sacred fire <note>original: agnihotra-geha; the room where the perpetual Vedic fire is maintained</note>, or from the queen's palace;
One should travel to the edge of the boundary, to another house, or outside the village for a distance of at least one hundred units <note>likely referring to 'dhanus' or bows, roughly 200 yards</note>. 98

### (How many days kings and others should stay in one place)

A King, a provincial governor <note>term: mandalapa; a ruler of a district or circle of territory</note>, or others should stay for seven, five, or three <gloss>3</gloss> days respectively.
One should not remain in a single place longer than that; however, if the preparations are already complete, a stay of two days is permitted for the sake of the sacred fires and the axle <note>referring to the maintenance of the sacrificial fires and the transport wagons</note>. 99

### (Distance to be traveled according to the day)

On the first day, the journey should be one *krosha* <note>term: krosha; a measure of distance, approximately 2 miles or 3 kilometers</note> in length; on the second day, exactly one *yojana* <note>term: yojana; a distance of about 8 to 9 miles</note>.
On the third day, one and a half *yojanas* should be covered; after that, the King may travel as much as he desires. 100

### (Prohibitions before starting a journey)

Anger, oil, gambling, meat, jaggery <note>unrefined cane sugar</note>, wine, vomiting, and weeping should be avoided at the time of departure.
One should avoid shaving for five days, drinking milk for three nights, and sexual intercourse for seven days prior. 101

### (Prohibition of intercourse on the day of travel and exceptions for a menstruating wife)

One must never engage in sexual intercourse on the day of the journey; however, if the wife is in her menstrual period <note>original: kusumena yukta; literally "joined with the flower"</note>,
The King should indeed engage in intercourse <insert>before departing</insert> if it is necessary <note>referring to the duty of 'rtugamana' or the husband's obligation to conceive during the fertile window</note>, as it may not be easily possible later. 102

### (Consideration of foods to be eaten before the time of departure)

Calotropis leaves <note>original: arka-patra</note>, rice-water, clarified butter <note>ghee</note>, boiled rice-gruel <gloss>1</gloss> <note>original: shrana; a local term for rabadi or porridge</note>, and sacrificial grains <note>term: havishyaka; pure food fit for an offering</note>.
Gold-infused water, cakes, citron, water, and cow urine; 103
Barley, milk-porridge with jaggery, and red mung bean rice;
From the start of the fortnight, one should eat what is prescribed and remember what is forbidden. 104

### (Foods according to the days of the week starting with Sunday)

<gloss>2</gloss> Natron <note>original: sarjika; a mineral alkali or soda</note>, milk-porridge, sour gruel, and boiled milk.

1. *Shrana*—Rabadi <note>a thick flour-based porridge</note>. 2. *Sarjika*—Natron/Alkali salt.

### ( Offerings for the Lunar Mansions )

Unbroken parboiled beans, sesame seeds mixed with rice, black gram, cow's curd, clarified butter, milk, honey, meat, and blood; ॥ 106 ॥
Milk-porridge, the meat of a blue jay, deer meat, rabbit meat, and *Shashtika* <note>a variety of rice that ripens in sixty days</note>;
*Priyangava* <note>foxtail millet</note>, flour cakes, green vegetables, fruits, tortoise meat, and the meat of a common myna; ॥ 107 ॥
The meat of a monitor lizard, *Shali* rice, sacrificial offerings, *Krusara* rice, and mung beans;
Cooked rice, barley flour, and likewise rice mixed with fish; ॥ 108 ॥
Variegated rice, and rice mixed with curd—this is the *Dohada* <note>ritual nourishment or longing</note> for the Lunar Mansions <term>Nakshatras: the 27 divisions of the sky through which the moon passes</term>. These offerings are for the pacification of the faults arising from the lunar day, the day of the week, the lunar mansion, and the directions. ॥ 109 ॥

### ( Offerings for the Directions )

Rice with clarified butter in the East; flour-based foods in the South; fish with rice in the West; and likewise, milk-porridge with clarified butter in the North. Having eaten these, the King may go forth for the success of his desired goals. ॥ 110 ॥

### ( Auspicious Omens )

Sandalwood, flowers, kings, fragrant substances, lotuses, beautiful fly-whisks, and fish;
Yellow pigment <note>Gorochan</note>, garments, drums, a woman with her son, white cows, elephants, horses, and conch shells; ॥ 111 ॥
The sound of bells and kettledrums, courtesans, canopies, umbrellas, mirrors, smokeless fire, and lamps;
Swings, lutes, singing, jars filled with water, turbans, milk, and adorned women and young girls;
Freshly upturned earth, cow dung, clay, Brahmins, deer, one's spiritual teacher, and a person with wealth in hand;
*Durva* grass, garlands, *Darbha* grass, chariots, grain, clarified butter, curd, an astrologer, and pleasant speech;
Cleanly washed clothes, pearls, sugarcane, weapons, and even a corpse—provided it is free from wailing;
Meat, wine, a fragrant cow with her calf, and the sounds of "Go!", "Live!", and "Victory!" ॥ 119 ॥

->**Chapter on Auspicious Omens**<-

Gems, a ceremonial water vessel <note>original: "bhringarka," a spouted pitcher used in rituals</note>, a shallow dish, banners, a tethered animal, and various fruits; white mustard seeds, friends, and a person—whether man or woman—dressed in beautiful attire; barley, copper, and silver. || 115 ||

Gold, betel leaves, various kinds of edible foods, anything red in color that is pleasing to the mind, and the sight of a king or a woman who has recently given birth; even hearing of these or experiencing a favorable wind is considered auspicious. || 116 ||

Freshly cooked white rice is highly praised here. However, one should not look at the rising moon on the left side. If a person carrying an empty pot follows behind, the King should circle them to the right <note>The 'pradakshina' or clockwise circumambulation is a mark of respect and a way to neutralize bad luck</note> before proceeding. || 117 ||

A camel, a large black bee, a female cuckoo, a speaking parrot, or a woman whose name matches one's own purpose; these are declared to be more auspicious if positioned on the left. The braying of a donkey is also favorable if heard from the left or from behind. || 118 ||

A cow, a black bird, a rooster, and those creatures known by masculine names are declared by the ancients to bring success if seen on the right. The skylark, the swan, the goat, the peacock, the blue jay, and the mongoose <note>original: "sarpa-dvit," literally 'the enemy of the snake'</note> bring good fortune the very moment they are sighted. || 119 ||

When birds or forest animals move in odd numbers, they are said to grant the fulfillment of one's goals if they move in a clockwise direction around the traveler.

In the first watch <note>a 'yama' or watch is a three-hour period</note> of the day, the jackal is in the Northeast; in the next three watches, it moves through the directions in pairs starting from the East. Moving thus clockwise through the eight watches of the day and night, the howling of a jackal is considered unfavorable in all directions. 127

### ( The Movement of Rahu according to the Directions )

In the East, Northwest, South, Northeast, West, Southeast, North, and Southwest;
->the shadow-planet Rahu <note>the ascending node of the moon, personified as a celestial serpent</note> stays for a period of one-and-a-half hours in each. For those intending to travel, it is auspicious if Rahu is positioned to one's right or behind. 128<-

<meta>This page continues the discourse on auspicious timing (Muhurta), transitioning from the conclusion of travel omens to the commencement of the section on architecture and home-building.</meta>

<page-num>82</page-num>

If a bad omen occurs at the start of a journey, one should turn back, purify oneself by sipping water <note>original: "achamana," a ritual purification</note>, and remain self-controlled. After performing eight <note>original: "gaja," literally 'elephants,' a poetic term for the number eight</note> breath-control exercises, a man may proceed. If a second bad omen occurs, he should perform double that number; however, he should not proceed at all if a third bad omen appears. || 132 ||

If the journey is absolutely necessary despite an inauspicious omen, one should consume clarified butter <note>ghee</note> mixed with gold before departing. These omens described for travel are also to be understood as applying to the consecration of a king <note><term>Rajyabhisheka</term>: the ritual anointing or coronation of a monarch</note>. || 133 ||

Having thoroughly examined many different texts, this "Flower of Travel" is thus completed within the work titled *The Wish-Fulfilling Tree of Auspicious Moments* <note><term>Muhurta-Kalpadruma</term>: a famous 17th-century treatise on electional astrology</note>, composed by Vitthala Dikshita. || 134 ||

## ( Now begins the Flower of Architecture )

The duties of a householder <note><term>Grihastha-dharma</term>: the social and religious obligations of the second stage of Hindu life</note> cannot exist without a home; indeed, the sages say a home is the very dwelling-place of peace. Therefore, having carefully reflected upon the opinions of the seers, I shall describe the purification of the house-site and related matters. || 1 ||

### ( Auspiciousness of a Town or Village based on one's Name-Sign )

If the land is located in a direction corresponding to the fifth or ninth zodiac sign from one's own name-sign <note>The "name-sign" is the zodiac sign determined by the first syllable of a person's name</note>, it brings wealth; otherwise, it does not. The corners <note>intermediate directions like Northeast or Southwest</note> are favorable for those of the lower classes; for other castes, the direction of Yama <note>the South</note> does not bring happiness. || 2 ||

<meta>This page continues the technical discussion of Vastu Shastra (architectural science) and divination, focusing on how the zodiac signs and phonetic categories of a person's name interact with the environment.</meta>

<page-num>83</page-num>

->( Consideration of Auspicious and Inauspicious Effects According to Zodiac Signs )<-

The signs of Virgo, Cancer, Sagittarius, and Libra, as well as Aries, Aquarius, Scorpio, and Pisces, do not find happiness <note>original: "na sukhaashinah"; implying lack of prosperity or comfort</note> when situated in the center of a city. However, these groups are indeed powerful when positioned in the direction of the Lord of the Gods <note>The East, ruled by Indra</note>. || 3 ||

->( Now, Consideration of Debtor and Creditor Relations Between a Village and an Individual )<-

The phonetic groups <term>Vargas: categories of the alphabet used to determine compatibility</term> are 'A', 'Ka', 'Ca', 'Ta', 'Ta' (dental), 'Pa', 'Ya', and 'Sha'. Their respective lords are the Eagle, the Lion, the Dog, the Snake, the Mouse, the Elephant, and the Deer. Those who are wise in these matters do not desire a residence that faces their natural enemy <note>Based on the traditional hierarchy of predator and prey among these animal symbols</note>. || 4 ||

Multiply the value of one's own phonetic group by two and add the value of the other group; then divide by eight to find the remainder of <term>Kakinis: a small unit of measurement or value used in divination</term>. Whosoever obtains the higher number in this calculation shall provide abundant wealth to the other. || 5 ||

->( Consideration of Land According to Social Class )<-

The earth is white, red, yellow, and black <note>These colors correspond to the four traditional social classes: Brahmin, Kshatriya, Vaishya, and Shudra</note>. For the final class, the soil is of a mixed nature. The tastes of these soils are sweet, pungent, bitter, and astringent, respectively. Their scents are similar to clarified butter, blood, food, and wine. || 6 ||

->( Purification of the Land )<-

For the classes starting with the Brahmins, the slope of the land should ideally be toward the North and subsequent directions. But why say more? The mind's satisfaction is the ultimate guide.

<meta>This page continues the technical instructions for site preparation and architectural orientation, following the selection of land. It transitions from spiritual purification to physical measurements.</meta>

<page-num>84</page-num>

One should build a house where the heart finds delight.
->If the builder of the house asks a question at that time:<-
The first letter of the sound they utter indicates the direction where a <term>Shalya</term> <note>original: "शल्य" (Shalya); refers to hidden obstacles or foreign objects like bones or charcoal buried underground that disturb the energy of a site</note> is located. After performing the <term>Shalyashuddhi</term> <note>The ritual purification and removal of hidden underground obstacles</note> through the methods described, a wise man should proceed with the pure dimensions of the site.

### ( Determination of the Directions )

Using a compass or a cord measured in <term>Angulas</term> <note>A traditional unit of measurement roughly equal to the width of a finger</note> based on the patron's proportions, one should draw a perfect circle. Placing a <term>Shanku</term> <note>A gnomon or vertical pillar used to cast a shadow</note> in the center, one must observe where the shadow enters and exits the circle. By drawing a line between these two points, the West and East directions are established respectively. From the "fish" intersection <note>A geometric method using intersecting arcs to find a perpendicular line</note> of those points, the South and North directions become clearly revealed for the purpose of the <term>Grihakarma</term> <note>The sacred work of house construction</note>.

### ( Auspicious Months for Beginning a House )

A house should be commenced in the months of <term>Radha</term> <note>Vaisakha (April-May)</note>, <term>Shravana</term> <note>July-August</note>, <term>Margashirsha</term> <note>November-December</note> when the sun is in Scorpio, or <term>Pausha</term> <note>December-January</note> when the sun is in Capricorn. It may also be done in <term>Magha</term> <note>January-February</note> or <term>Phalguna</term> <note>February-March</note> when the sun is in Aquarius; however, some authorities state that <term>Magha</term> is not auspicious.

### ( Orientation of the House Entrance based on Solar Transit )

When the sun is in Capricorn or Cancer, the house should face East or West. When the sun is in Libra or Aries, the house should face South or North. If these alignments are ignored, it is said to bring sorrow.

### ( Placement of Doors according to Social Class )

The wise desire the placement of the door starting from the East for the <term>Kshatriya</term> <note>Warrior/Ruler class</note>, <term>Vaishya</term> <note>Merchant class</note>, <term>Shudra</term> <note>Laborer class</note>, and <term>Brahmin</term> <note>Priest/Scholar class</note> respectively, according to their zodiac signs.

### ( Contemplation of the Vastu Purusha )

For three months starting with <term>Bhadrapada</term> <note>August-September</note>, the <term>Vastu Purusha</term> <note>The "Spirit of the Site," envisioned as a cosmic man lying on the ground</note> sleeps with his head to the East; in the subsequent periods, he rotates to other directions. The noble sages declare that a house is best when its orientation matches the direction of the head during those three-month periods.

### ( Consideration of Doors based on Income/Energy Flow )

In the directions starting from the East, the doors known as <term>Go</term> (Cow), <term>Gaja</term> (Elephant), <term>Dhvaja</term> (Flag), and <term>Mrigendra</term> (Lion) are ordained to be the givers of auspiciousness.

It emphasizes the alignment of the house with the Vastu Purusha (the spirit of the site) to ensure prosperity.</summary>

## Consideration of the House According to Social Class

> For a member of the priestly class <note>original: "Dwija" (twice-born); refers to Brahmins</note>, a house facing West is auspicious;
> For a member of the ruling class <note>original: "Nripa"; refers to Kshatriyas</note>, a house facing North brings prosperity.
> For the merchant class <note>original: "Vaishya"</note>, a house facing East is beneficial;
> For the laboring class <note>original: "Shudra"</note>, a house facing South is prescribed. || 15 ||

## Specifics Regarding the Doorway

If the length of the house is divided into nine parts starting from the East, <unclear>the placement of the door</unclear> is discussed. Sages state that a door placed in the fourth section <note>the number 4 is written in the text</note> from the left is auspicious in all cases. || 16 ||

## The Diagram of the Door

Based on the Sun's current constellation <note>original: "Suryarksha"</note>, the auspicious and inauspicious effects are determined by the <unclear>numerical values</unclear> associated with the head, corners, door-jambs, threshold, and center of the door respectively. || 17 ||

## Constellations for Beginning House Construction

The construction of a house should take place under the constellations <term>Dhanishtha, the three Uttaras, Rohini, Pushya, Swati, Shatabhisha, Hasta, and the "soft" constellations (Mrigashira, Chitra, Anuradha, and Revati)</term>.

From the Sun's constellation, the counts of seven, eleven, and eight <note>referring to specific positions in the diagram</note> determine the auspicious and inauspicious results in the "Calf Diagram" <note>original: "Vatsachakra"; a symbolic tool used to find the best time for construction</note>.

<page-num>86</page-num>

### (Raising the Pillars, Laying the Foundation Stone, and Measuring)

One should not perform the raising of the pillars <note>original: "stambhocchriti"</note>, the laying of the foundation stones <note>original: "shilanyasa"</note>, or the initial cord-marking <note>original: "sutradi"</note> on "empty" lunar days <note>The 'Rikta' or empty Tithis are the 4th, 9th, and 14th days of the lunar fortnight, considered unlucky for new beginnings.</note>, nor on Tuesdays or other inauspicious weekdays. Likewise, these should be avoided when the Moon is positioned in the signs of Pisces or Aquarius. ॥ 19 ॥

### (Astrological Purity of the Ascendant for House Construction)

The construction of a house should be commenced when the Ascendant <note>term: Lagna (the zodiac sign rising on the eastern horizon)</note> is in a "fixed" or "dual" sign, and is occupied or aspected by benefic planets. It is especially auspicious if powerful benefic planets are situated in the 1st, 4th, 5th, 7th, 9th, or 10th houses. ॥ 20 ॥
Sages declare that it is beneficial if malefic planets are placed in the 3rd, 6th, or 11th houses. However, a cruel planet in the 8th house does not bring good fortune; similarly, the Sun, Venus, the Moon, and Jupiter should not be in their signs of debilitation, in the 7th house, or in the houses of their enemies. ॥ 21 ॥

### (Calculation of the Area)

The area <note>original: "phalam"</note> is determined by multiplying the width by the length. Divide this product by eight; the remainder indicates the "Income" <note>term: Aya (a formula used in Vastu to ensure the building's dimensions harmonize with cosmic energy)</note>, starting with the "Banner" type. Multiply the area by eight and divide by twenty-seven; the remainder is the "Birth Star" <note>original: "dhishnyam" or Nakshatra</note> of the house. Divide that same product by eight to find the "Expenditure" <note>original: "vyaya"</note>. ॥ 22 ॥
Starting from the East, the values for the rooms <note>original: "shala"</note> are represented by the numbers one, two, four, and eight; their sum determines the "Dhruvaka" <note>a constant used for classification</note>.

->1-2-3-4-5-6<-

By adding one, the house types range from one to six; <insert>13</insert> <gloss>13</gloss> <insert>3</insert> <gloss>3</gloss> the total number is represented by double letters. ॥ 23 ॥

The seventh house <note>original: 'alaya', referring to a division in the architectural grid</note> is designated by the syllable of the Veda <note>The number four, as there are four Vedas</note>, while the remaining syllables make up the sixteen types. These are: Constant, Grain, Victory, Joy, Harsh, Radiant, and Pleasing. || 24 ||

Fair-faced, Ugly-faced, Cruel, Hostile, Wealth-giver, Decay, Wailing, Extensive, and the sixteenth is Victory. || 25 ||

Those who have attained wisdom through the architectural scriptures <term>Shilpa Shastra: ancient Indian manuals on arts, crafts, and design</term> declare that the results of these houses correspond to their names. To find the specific result, one should take the value of the house name and its syllables, multiply them, and divide by three; the remainder reveals the 'Amsha' <note>a portion or quality of the building's influence</note>. || 26 ||

->( Names of the Income/Aya )<-

The Banner, the Smoke, the Lion, the Dog, the Bull, the Donkey, the Elephant, and the Crow. These are the 'Ayas'; the odd-numbered ones are considered auspicious. Among them, the Banner <note>Dhvajaya</note> is auspicious in every direction and position. || 27 ||

A high income with low expenditure is ideal. The portions <note>Amshas</note> related to Indra, Yama, the King, and Death are considered inauspicious. Furthermore, if the house constellation <note>Nakshatra</note> falls either directly in front of or behind the direction of the main door, there will never be happiness in that dwelling. || 28 ||

->( A verse by the ancient teachers regarding the calculation of results through an alternative method )<-

Take the desired constellation minus one and multiply by one hundred and fifty-two <gloss>152</gloss>. Add seventeen <gloss>17</gloss> and eighteen. Then, take the desired income minus one, multiply by twelve and eight, and add two hundred and sixteen <gloss>216</gloss>. Multiply these by the factor of six... <note>The text breaks off mid-calculation; these are complex mnemonic verses for astrological architectural mathematics.</note>

<page-num>88</page-num>

The remaining area <note>Sanskrit: kshetraphala</note> should be determined based on the desired constellation <note>Nakshatra</note> chosen for the owner. The length is found by dividing the area by the desired width, and the width is found by dividing the area by the length. (1)

### ( Another Method for Calculating the Nine Factors like Income )

When the area is multiplied respectively by nine, nine, six, eight, three, eight, eight, four, and eight; and then divided by eight, seven, nine, twelve, eight, twenty-seven, fifteen, twenty-seven, and one hundred and twenty: (29)

| Multiplier: | 9 | 9 | 6 | 8 | 3 | 8 | 8 | 4 | 8 |
| :--- | :---: | :---: | :---: | :---: | :---: | :---: | :---: | :---: | :---: |
| Divisor: | 8 | 7 | 9 | 12 | 8 | 27 | 15 | 27 | 120 |

Then the Income <note>Aya</note>, Day of the week, the Part <note>Anshaka</note>, Assets, Debts, Constellation, Lunar day, Yoga, and Lifespan are determined. Just as in a marriage, the compatibility between the house-lord and the house must be matched; however, here the union of the pulses <note>Nadi-yuti</note> is considered auspicious, not the union of the signs. (30)

### ( Consideration of Zodiac Signs according to Sage Garga )

In Aries, the three stars starting with Ashwini are mentioned; in Leo, the three starting with Magha; and in Sagittarius, the three starting with Mula. For the remaining signs, they are taken in pairs. This is what the Sage Garga has declared. (31)

Avoiding the sharp, fierce, and mixed constellations, as well as the Sun, Mars, Scorpio, and Aquarius, the entrance into the house should occur in the ninth month. In the case of a maternity room, the ceremony should be performed immediately upon birth for the sake of auspiciousness. (32)

### ( The Face of Rahu and the Act of Digging )

When the Sun is in Pisces, Capricorn, or Scorpio, and in the three signs following each of these: for temples, water reservoirs, and houses, the movement is counter-clockwise respectively. (33)
The face of Rahu <note>the ascending node of the moon, personified as a shadow planet</note> is said to be in the direction of Ishana <note>Northeast</note> and the intermediate points. Therefore, the wise have declared that digging is auspicious when done from the back. (34)

### (Where the Ayadi Calculations are Not Observed)

->The calculations of <term>Aya and Vyaya</term><note>Income and expenditure; a set of six formulas used in Vastu to determine the energetic suitability of a building.</note> should not be considered for houses made of grass or straw.<-
->The purification of months and other timing factors are not required for temporary sheds or entryways.<-
->Furthermore, if a house is built with dimensions exceeding the standard "elephant-hand" <note>original: "danta-hasta"; likely referring to a specific large-scale cubit measurement</note> measure,<-
->there too, one need not examine the <term>Ayadi</term> formulas. || 35 ||<-

### (Considerations for Wells According to Direction)

->Wealth, the birth of sons, the loss of a young woman, death, wealth, fear, happiness, and prosperity are the respective results of a well in the eight directions starting from the East.<-
->However, if a well is situated exactly in the center of the house, the sages have predicted the total loss of wealth. || 36 ||<-

### (Now, the Consideration of the House's Lifespan)

->If Jupiter is in the first house, Venus is in the fourth, Mercury is in the seventh,<-
->the Sun is in the sixth, and Saturn is in the third, then the house is said to have a lifespan of one hundred years. || 37 ||<-
->When Venus is in the first house, Mercury in the tenth, Jupiter in any of the <term>Kendras</term><note>The 1st, 4th, 7th, or 10th houses in a birth chart, considered the pillars of the horoscope.</note>,<-
->and the Sun <note>original: "Sarojini-nayaka" (Lord of the Lotuses)</note> is in the eleventh house, the house will be prosperous with happiness for a hundred years. || 38 ||<-
->With Jupiter in the fifth, Venus in the first, Mars in the sixth, and the Sun in the third,<-
->the residence in that house will last for two hundred years, enriched by vast wealth and the merchant classes. || 39 ||<-
->When Venus is in its sign of exaltation in the first house, and Jupiter is exalted or in the fourth house,<-
->and Saturn is in its own sign of exaltation in the eleventh house, the house rejoices with prosperity for a long time. || 40 ||<-
->If the Moon is in its own sign in the first house,<-
->and Jupiter is in a <term>Kendra</term>, the house will be blessed with fortune for a long time.<-
->But if the planets are in the signs or divisions of friends or their own houses,<-
->yet fall into their <term>Neecha</term><note>Debilitation; the weakest point for a planet.</note> divisions, there will be no happiness. || 41 ||<-
->If the Moon is in the tenth house, Jupiter is in the fourth,<-
->and Mars and the Sun are in the eleventh house...<-

### ( The Flower of Entering the Home )

Even in a very spacious residence adorned with balconies, verandas, and lattices of windows, entry results in happiness only if done at a virtuous time; therefore, I shall now declare that auspicious time. || 1 ||

### ( The Three Types of Home Entry )

The entry is called "Apurva" <note>unprecedented/new</note> when it concerns a brand new house. It is called "Sapūrva" <note>previously inhabited</note> when returning after a journey. It is called "Dvandva" <note>dual/renovated</note> when entering a house previously damaged by fire, water, or similar disasters. This threefold entry is to be honored. || 2 ||

### ( Purity of Months and Other Factors for Home Entry )

In a new house, or even when returning from a journey, entry is desired in the five months beginning with Magha, excluding Chaitra <note>The months of Magha, Phalguna, Vaishakha, Jyeshtha, and Ashadha are generally preferred</note>. It should be avoided on Tuesdays and Sundays, and on days of the New Moon, "Empty" lunar days <note>Rikta Tithis: 4th, 9th, and 14th</note>, or when the Ascendant is unstable. || 3 ||

Entry is best when the direction of the door matches the Nakshatra <note>lunar mansion</note>, specifically the "soft" or "fixed" stars, and when the sign is a fixed or dual-natured one. In conjunction with these, malefic planets should be in the 3rd, 6th, or 11th houses, while benefics should be in the 2nd, 3rd, 11th, 5th, 9th, or the center houses <note>1st, 4th, 7th, 10th</note>. || 4 ||

The Moon must be pure in the 8th and 4th houses <gloss>benefic in the 4th</gloss> and not in the 6th, 8th, or 12th houses.
When the Moon—

### ( Specifics for Old Houses )

—is thus, then for an old house, the months of Shravana, Kartika, and Margashirsha <gloss>in the house</gloss> [are acceptable].
As previously stated, the month...
