### (Constellations for Entering a New Home)

The constellations of Pushya, Swati, Shatabhisha, and Dhanishtha <note>original: "Pushya-Vayu-Jalesha-Vasvarksha"</note>, as well as those previously mentioned, are auspicious for entering a home. || 5 ||

### (Exemption from Faults for Old Houses)

In the case of an old or renovated house, the usual faults associated with the position of the sun <note>original: "Hastadi-dosha," referring to specific astrological obstructions</note> do not apply. Entry is auspicious provided there is purity of the five limbs of the calendar <term>Panchanga: the five sources of energy—tithi, vara, nakshatra, yoga, and karana</term>.

### (Constellations for the Vastu Ritual)

The worship of the Vastu <note>the spirit or deity of the architecture</note> should be performed beforehand during constellations that are categorized as soft, fixed, swift, or mobile. || 6 ||

### (Consideration of the Sun’s Position)

Count from the eighth, fifth, second, and eleventh houses respectively to determine the Sun's position. One should consider the orientation of the house starting from the East. When the Sun is situated to the left or behind the person entering, it brings auspiciousness. || 7 ||

### (Purification of the Kalasha)

In the diagram of the entry-vessel <term>Kalasha-chakra: a symbolic diagram used to calculate the suitability of a time based on the Sun's position</term>, count from the Sun’s constellation in groups of five, eight, eight, and six. These represent the various parts of the body, some of which are declared inauspicious and others auspicious. || 8 ||

| 5 | 8 | 8 | 6 |
|---|---|---|---|
| Inauspicious | Excellent | Inauspicious | Excellent |

### (When Entry Should Not Be Performed)

If the house is not yet roofed, if the Vastu worship has not been performed, if the doors lack panels, if the Brahmins have not been fed, or if it lacks the marks of offerings and festive garlands, the entry will bring great sorrow. || 9 ||

### (The Manner in Which to Enter the Home)

Placing a water-filled pitcher <term>Kalasha</term> in front, accompanied by Brahmins chanting auspicious and holy hymns, and observing favorable omens, one should enter the beautiful home with joy. || 10 ||

After thoroughly examining many texts, this has been composed by Vitthala Dikshita. Thus ends the flower <note>chapter</note> on House Entry in the work titled "The Desire-Fulfilling Tree of Electional Astrology" <note>original: "Muhurta Kalpadruma"</note>. || 11 ||

## ( The Flower of Deity Consecration )

### ( Determining Auspicious Months and Dates for Consecration )

The consecration of a deity <note>original: "Devapratishtha"; the ritual process of infusing an idol with divine life-force</note> grants all desires when performed during the five months beginning with Magha <note>approximately January to June</note>. It is considered most auspicious from the fifth day of the bright lunar fortnight until the tenth day of the dark lunar fortnight. (1)
Within that period, one should avoid the "empty" lunar days <note>the 4th, 9th, and 14th days of a lunar cycle, considered unlucky for new ventures</note> and avoid Tuesdays. There is a specific distinction: the installation of Ganesha <note>original: "Vighnaraja," the Lord of Obstacles</note>, Parvati <note>original: "Girija," the Mountain-Born</note>, and Shiva <note>original: "Maheshwara"</note> should take place on their respective dedicated lunar dates. (2)

### ( Auspicious Constellations for Consecration )

The "Light" constellations, the "Fixed" constellations, Swati, Mrigashira, Anuradha, Jyeshtha, Shravana, Mula, Revati <note>original: "Pusha"</note>, and the "Prior" constellations <note>the three Purva stars</note> are recommended for consecrations.

### ( Specific Constellations for Particular Deities )

->The Light and Fixed constellations, Revati,<-
->Shravana, Rohini <note>original: "Aditya"</note>, Punarvasu, Mrigashira, and Anuradha (3)<-
->are beneficial for the consecration of Vishnu. Shravana and Pushya<-
->are for the Moon and Sun; Pushya is also for Skanda and Kubera <note>original: "Dhanesha"</note>.<-
->Anuradha <note>original: "Maitra"</note> is for the Sun. For the leader of the vanguard,<-
->Durga and the other goddesses, installation here is praised. (4)<-

Yakshas, spirits, serpents, and Ganesha <note>original: "Vagnatha"</note> are suited to the final constellation <note>Revati</note> or Ashlesha; the Buddha <note>original: "Sugata"</note> is suited to Shravana. The World-Guardians <note>Lokapalas</note> are suited to Dhanishta <note>original: "Vasubha"</note>, and all the planets or all deities generally are suited to Pushya and the "Fixed" signs. (5)

### ( Purity of the Ascendant Sign for Consecration )

->Shiva is best installed when the Sun is in Leo or in "Double-Natured" signs <note>Gemini, Virgo, Sagittarius, Pisces</note>.<-
->Vishnu is best in Virgo, and Brahma <note>original: "Dhata"</note> in Aquarius.<-
->Goddesses should be installed in Double-Natured or "Movable" signs <note>Aries, Cancer, Libra, Capricorn</note>.<-
->All minor deities should be placed in "Fixed" signs for the sake of auspiciousness. (6)<-

When Jupiter <note>Guru</note> and Venus <note>the teacher of the Daityas</note> are visible, and the Moon is in the eleventh house <note>the house of gain</note>, all malefic planets are in the third or sixth houses, Rahu <note>the shadow planet</note> and the Sun are in the tenth house <note>the house of kingdom</note>, and the benefic planets are in the angles <note>Kendras</note>, trines <note>Trikonas</note>, or the second and third houses, it is considered auspicious. || 7 ||

->( Consecration during the Southern Transit of the Sun )<-

The venerable Varaha, the Mother Goddesses, Bhairava, and Chandi may be installed even during the Southern Transit <note>Dakshinayana; the period between the summer and winter solstices</note>.

->( Nighttime is also excellent for Consecration )<-

The installation of a deity may also be performed at night,

->( Installation of the Goddess in the month of Ashvina )<-

especially for the Goddess in the month of Ashvina <note>September-October, coinciding with Navaratri</note>. || 8 ||

->( Specifics for Water Reservoirs )<-

The consecration of water <note>such as wells or tanks</note> should occur when the Sun is in the Northern Transit <note>Uttarayana</note>, on the 3rd, 13th, 2nd, 12th, 5th, 10th, or 15th <gloss>3</gloss> <gloss>13</gloss> <gloss>2</gloss> <gloss>12</gloss> <gloss>5</gloss> <gloss>10</gloss> <gloss>15</gloss> lunar days <note>Tithis</note>. Under the fixed constellations <note>Dhruva</note>, Jyeshtha <note>Indra</note>, Mula, Mrigashira <note>Indu</note>, the light constellations <note>Laghu</note>, Shravana, Revati <note>Antya</note>, or Anuradha <note>Maitra</note>, the consecration of water is auspicious during a day-sign. || 9 ||

->( The Flower of Initiation Rites )<-
->( Purity of Months for Initiation )<-

The rite of initiation <term>Diksha; the formal induction into a spiritual path or mantra</term> is auspicious in the month of Vaishakha <note>Radhe</note>, the two months of Magha and Phalguna, and the three months starting from Ashvina. It should be done in the bright fortnight, or the first half of the dark fortnight. The intercalary month <note>Malimlucha</note> and the 14th lunar day <note>Bhuta</note> should be avoided. || 1 ||

<page-num>94</page-num>

All lunar days <note>tithis</note> are generally praised, but specifically, the second day is for the Goddess of Prosperity <note>Lakshmi</note>, and the twelfth day is for the Wielder of the Discus <note>Vishnu</note>. The fourteenth day <gloss>14</gloss> is the day of the Spirits <note>Bhuta-dina</note> and belongs to Shiva. For the ritual of the Saraswati mantra, the thirteenth day <note>Kama</note> is preferred. 2
The fourteenth, the eighth, and the second days are prescribed for the rituals of the Katyayani <note>a fierce form of the Mother Goddess</note> mantra. Sunday is for the Sun God, and the fourth lunar day is praised for the rituals of the Lord of Hosts <note>Ganesha</note>. 3

### (Auspicious Stars for Initiation)

The "Light" stars, the "Fixed" stars, Punarvasu, Magha, Anuradha, Shravana, Ashwini, Revati, Mrigashirsha, and Swati are considered excellent. <note>These refer to specific Nakshatras or lunar mansions in Vedic astrology.</note>

### (Purity of the Ascendant for Initiation)

Malefic planets should be placed in the third, sixth, and eleventh houses. Benefic planets are auspicious when located in the corners <note>Trikona</note>, the centers <note>Kendra</note>, or the second, fifth, and ninth houses. 4
When the eighth house is pure, a mantra for Shiva is auspicious in a "Movable" sign, a mantra for Vishnu in a "Fixed" sign, and a mantra for the Goddess in a "Dual-natured" sign.
The mantra is auspicious—

### (Prohibition of the Setting of Jupiter and Venus)

—except when Jupiter is in its sign of debilitation or defeated in planetary combat, or when Venus is in its "infancy," "old age," or has set <note>is combust or invisible in the sky</note>. 5

### (Exceptions where Time-Purity is Not Required)

On the day of a solar transit <note>Sankranti</note>, during eclipses of the Sun or Moon, in the month of Shravana <note>July-August</note>, on the fourteenth day of the lunar month, or at holy sites like Prayag, Shri Parvata, Avimuktaka <note>Varanasi</note>, or the great seats of the Goddess <gloss>in these places</gloss>, one does not need to wait for a purified astrological time. 6
Having thoroughly examined many texts, this "Flower" <note>chapter</note> on the Rules of Initiation in the book named *The Wish-Fulfilling Tree of Auspicious Moments* <note>Muhurta Kalpadruma</note>, composed by Vitthala Dikshita, is now complete. 7

# The Multicolored Flower
<note>original: "Vicitra-kusumam"; likely the title of this specific chapter or treatise on astrological combinations.</note>

## First, the letters assigned to the quarters of the lunar mansions to determine the zodiac signs

The letters *Chu, Che, Cho, La* belong to the mansion of the Ashvins <note>Ashwini</note>; through the division of its quarters, and with the four quarters of the Bearing One <note>Bharani</note>, a zodiac sign is formed. In the Fire mansion <note>Krittika</note>, the letters are *Li, Lu, Le, Lo*. Then follow *A, I, U, E* and *O, Va, Vi, Vu*—this is the collection of letters. || 1 ||

*Ve, Vo, Ka, Ki* belong to the Moon’s mansion <note>Rohini</note>; *Ku, Gha, Nga, Cha* belong to the Fierce One <note>Ardra</note>; *Ke, Ko, Ha, Hi* belong to the mansion named for the Mother of Gods <note>Punarvasu</note>. *Hu, He, Ho, Da* are in the Nourishing mansion <note>Pushya</note>; *Di, Du, De, Do* are in the Serpent’s mansion <note>Ashlesha</note>; *Ma, Mi, Mu, Me* are in the Ancestral mansion <note>Magha</note>. || 2 ||

*Mo, Ta, Ti, Tu* are in the Mansion of Fortune <note>Purva Phalguni</note>; then in the mansion of Aryaman <note>Uttara Phalguni</note> are *Te, To, Pa, Pi*. In the Sun’s mansion <note>Hasta</note> are *Pu, Sha, Na, Tha*. *Pe, Po, Ra, Ri* are in the Carpenter’s mansion <note>Chitra</note>; in the Wind mansion <note>Svati</note> are *Ru, Re, Ro, Ta*. In the mansion of the Two Lords <note>Vishakha</note> are *Ti, Tu, Te, To*. || 3 ||

*Na, Ni, Nu, Ne* are in the Friendly mansion <note>Anuradha</note>; *No, Ya, Yi, Yu* are in Indra’s mansion <note>Jyeshtha</note>. *Ye, Yo, Bha, Bhi* are assigned to the Root <note>Mula</note>. In the Water mansion <note>Purva Ashadha</note> are *Bhu, Dha, Pha, Dha*. In the Universal mansion <note>Uttara Ashadha</note> are *Bhe, Bho, Ja, Ji*. Thus it is said by the wise: *Ju, Je, Jo, Kha*. || 4 ||

*Khi, Khu, Khe, Kho* are in the mansion of Vishnu <note>Shravana</note>; *Ga, Gi, Gu, Ge* are in the mansion of the Vasus <note>Dhanishtha</note>. *Go, Sa, Si, Su* are in the Lord of Waters <note>Shatabhisha</note>. *Se, So, Da, Di* are in the first "Prosthapada" <note>Purva Bhadrapada</note>; *Du, Jha, Jna, Tha* are in the "Mansion of Intelligence" <note>Uttara Bhadrapada</note>. In the mansion of the Nourisher <note>Revati</note> are *De, Do, Cha, Chi*. || 5 ||

### The Zodiac Signs according to the Lunar Mansions

The two Ashvins <note>Ashwini and Bharani</note> and the first quarter of the Fire mansion <note>Krittika</note> constitute Aries. The remaining three quarters of the Fire mansion and the mansion of the Moon <note>Rohini</note> along with half of the Deer's Head <note>Mrigashira</note> are called Taurus. The other half of the Deer's Head, the Moist One <note>Ardra</note>, and three quarters of the Mother of Gods <note>Punarvasu</note> constitute Gemini <note>term: "Jittama" (the Greek-derived 'ituma')</note>. || 6 ||

One quarter of the Mother of Gods, the Nourishing mansion <note>Pushya</note>, and the Serpent mansion <note>Ashlesha</note> are Cancer. The two "Mighty Ones" <note>Magha and Purva Phalguni</note> and a quarter of Aryaman's mansion <note>Uttara Phalguni</note> are Leo. The remaining three quarters of Aryaman, the Hand <note>Hasta</note>, and half of the Carpenter <note>Chitra</note> are Virgo. || 7 ||

->The other half of the Carpenter, the Wind <note>Svati</note>, and three quarters of the Two Lords <note>Vishakha</note> are Libra.<-
->The last quarter of the Two Lords, the Friendly mansion <note>Anuradha</note>, and Indra's mansion <note>Jyeshtha</note> are Scorpio.<-
->The two "Roots" <note>Mula and Purva Ashadha</note> and one quarter of the Universal mansion <note>Uttara Ashadha</note> are Sagittarius.<-
->The remaining three quarters of the Universal mansion and the mansion of Vishnu <note>Shravana</note> are Capricorn.<- || 8 ||

Half of the Wealthy mansion <note>Dhanishtha</note> is also Capricorn. The other half of the Wealthy mansion, the Lord of Waters <note>Shatabhisha</note>, and three quarters of the first "Goat-Foot" <note>Purva Bhadrapada</note> are Aquarius. The last quarter of the Goat-Foot, the Mansion of Intelligence <note>Uttara Bhadrapada</note>, and the Nourisher <note>Revati</note> are called Pisces. || 9 ||

<summary>This page details the phonetic system used to link specific syllables to the 27 lunar mansions (Nakshatras) and explains how these mansions are grouped to form the twelve signs of the zodiac.</summary>

## The Twitching of Limbs

->The twitching of limbs on the right side is for men,<-
->And on the left side for beautiful women, a sign of good fortune.<-
->For everyone, twitching in the forehead or head brings happiness and such;<-
->If reversed <note>i.e., left for men or right for women</note>, it is considered harmful according to its strength. || 10 ||<-

## The Results of a House Lizard or Chameleon Crawling on the Body

If it moves upon the face, the chin, or the entire right side of the body,
Or on the navel, the heart, or the belly, it grants auspicious results; it is mixed on the left side.
If it falls upon the spine, the throat, the penis, the anus, or the bladder, it is undesirable.
This is the result for a house lizard <term>Palli: the common house gecko</term>; for a chameleon <term>Krikalasa: a forest lizard or calotes</term> climbing the body, the results for women are the opposite. || 11 ||

## The Consideration of Omens and Dreams

In a dream, it is auspicious to climb a mountain, a great elephant, a tree, a horse, a chariot, or a man.
So too are smearing oneself with feces, or seeing semen, cooked rice, a corpse, being soaked in blood, drinking <note>likely wine or milk</note>, or a snake bite. || 12 ||
Seeing the burning of one's bed, house, limbs, or seat; the cutting of one's head; the flowing of blood; or eating milk-rice <term>Payasa: a sweet medicinal or ritual rice pudding</term>.
Intercourse with a well-dressed woman; obtaining fruit, water, a lotus, a parasol, a flag, or a mantra. || 13 ||
Seeing cows, kings, or gold is praised here; the words of ancestors or Brahmins <term>Dvija: "twice-born" priestly class</term> in a dream are truths.
Beholding a palace, crossing the ocean, and the eating of human flesh are also good. || 14 ||
Harmful are marriage festivals, drinking liquor, mocking laughter, and anything black or reddish-brown.
Except for gods, Brahmins, armies, and cows, anything "extra" <note>deformed or excessive</note> is bad in omens and otherwise. || 15 ||

->The fruit of a dream manifests in a year, a half-year, three months, or one month,<-
->Depending on the watch <term>Prahara: a three-hour period</term> of the night in which it occurred.<-
->A dream at dawn yields immediate results, especially if one stays awake;<-
->But a dream during the day, or if one falls back to sleep, is in vain. || 16 ||<-

<summary>This page details the divinatory significance of involuntary muscle twitches, the omens associated with lizards crawling on specific body parts, and a comprehensive list of auspicious and inauspicious dream symbols.</summary>
<keywords>physiognomy, twitching, lizard omens, dream interpretation, auspicious signs, Indian divination, Prahara, Palli, Krikalasa</keywords>

--- Page 115 ---
<meta>This page concludes the main text of the Muhurtakalpadruma, transitioning from the final technical verses on medical astrology to the author's formal colophon and lineage.</meta>

Page 97
Signature: Muhurta 7
<note>The right side of this page shows significant water staining and foxing, though the text remains legible.</note>

That [dream] which arises from anxiety, illness, or an imbalance of the humors—phlegm, wind, or bile <note>original: "kapha-vata-pitta"; the three doshas of Ayurvedic medicine</note>—is considered fruitless. One should report a good dream to a priest, but never speak of a bad one; instead, in that case, one should meditate upon the name of Murari <note>A name for Lord Vishnu, the "Destroyer of the demon Mura"</note>. || 17 ||

Having thoroughly examined many ancient texts, Vitthala Dikshita has composed this work titled *The Wish-Fulfilling Tree of Auspicious Moments* <note>original: "Muhurtakalpadruma"</note>, of which this final section is a wondrously named flower. || 18 ||

---

->( Now, the Ornamentation of the Book )<-

In the exceedingly pure lineage of Krishnatri <note>The Krishna-Atri clan or family line</note>, there was born one of holy deeds named Vuva Sharma. By his son, Vitthala Dikshita, this *Wish-Fulfilling Tree of Auspicious Moments* was created. || 1 ||

If this is indeed a Wish-Fulfilling Tree <note>original: "Kalpadruma"; a divine tree in Hindu mythology that grants all desires</note>, then I consider the *Jewel-Garland* <note>Ratnamala</note> and the *Thought-Gem* <note>Chintamani</note> to be mere pebbles by comparison. Since this small branch <note>Manjari</note> is but a single part of it, O seekers of truth, consider its essential wisdom! || 2 ||

->Bearing various fruits and flowers,<-
->The Wish-Fulfilling Tree grants all things imagined.<-
->Until the end of the world-cycle, may it remain<-
->In the homes of the masters of the learned.<- || 3 ||

Thus ends the *Muhurtakalpadruma* composed by the illustrious Vitthala Dikshita, the foremost among the best of astrologers, whose feet are worshiped by the entire community of scholars.

<note>Technical vocabulary:
- Muhurtakalpadruma: The title of the work, meaning "The Wish-Fulfilling Tree of Auspicious Timing."
- Vitthala Dikshita: The author's name.
- Krishnatrigotra: The specific ancestral lineage (gotra) of the author.
- Vuva Sharma: The name of the author's father.
- Granthalankara: The "ornamentation" or concluding poetic flourishes of a book.
- Kalpadruma: A celestial tree that grants wishes.
- Vibudhadhipanam: Referring to the leaders of the wise or the gods.</note>

<summary>This concluding page of the Muhurtakalpadruma provides the final verse on dream interpretation and the author's colophon, detailing Vitthala Dikshita's lineage and his poetic dedication of the work. The author asserts the superiority of his text over other contemporary astrological treatises through the metaphor of a divine tree.</summary>
<keywords>Vitthala Dikshita, Muhurtakalpadruma, astrology, dream interpretation, Ayurvedic humors, lineage, colophon, Sanskrit literature</keywords>
